\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\psi^{+}\>\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi^{+}\>\mathrm{d}x-\gamma\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi^{+}\>\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi^{+}\>\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\psi^{+}\>\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi^{+}\>\mathrm{d}x-\gamma\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\psi^{-}\>\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi^{-}\>\mathrm{d}x.

Then apply Proposition
A.4
and
A.13
combined with the coercivity of
P(\Sigma^{\pm})
to obtain the estimate

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi^{+}\>\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi^{+}\>\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{S}^{+}}P(\Sigma^{+})\nabla^{\mu}_{x,z}\phi^{+}\cdot\nabla^{\mu}_{x,z}\phi^{+}\>\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\gamma\int_{\mathcal{S}^{-}}P(\Sigma^{-})\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}\phi^{-}\cdot\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}\phi^{-}\>\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{1+M}\|\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}\phi^{+}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{+})}^{2}.

Moreover, since
\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi^{+}\in\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})\subset\dot{H}_{\mu}^{\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
we have (
A.6
) at hand. In particular, we obtain from the above estimates and (
A.4
) that

(2.26)

\displaystyle|\psi^{+}|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\leq M|\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi^{+}|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

Equivalently, estimate (
2.24
) holds in the case
s=0
. We may also use this estimate to prove the invertibility as in
[
65
]
. In particular, we have from the lower bound (
2.26
) and Proposition
2.4
with estimate (
2.11
) that
\mathcal{J}_{\mu}
is an injective and closed operator since,

\gamma|(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi^{+}|_{\dot{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}\leq\mu^{-\frac{1}{4}}\gamma|(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi^{+}|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}\leq\gamma M|\psi^{+}|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}.

Moreover, from the lower bound, (
2.26
), on
\mathcal{J}_{\mu}
we know that it is also semi-Fredholm. Then since for small enough values of
\gamma\in(0,1)
it is invertible by a Neumann series expansion, we have from the homotopic invariance of the index that the operator is in fact Fredholm of index zero
[
52
]
. Consequently, the operator is also surjective and therefore invertible.

For the general case of
s\in[0,t_{0}+1]
, the proof is the same as Lemma
2
in
[
65
]
.
∎

We may now use Lemma
2.10
to prove Proposition
2.9
.

Proof of Proposition
2.9
.

We first consider the existence of a unique solution
\phi^{+}
in the lower domain. Since
\psi\in\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
we can use Lemma
2.10
to make the definition:

\psi^{+}=(\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\psi\in\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})\quad\text{and}\quad\psi^{-}=(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi^{+}\in\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})\subset\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}}),

where we let
\phi^{\pm}|_{z=0}=\psi^{\pm}
. Then we can use the first point of Proposition
A.4
to deduce a unique solution
\phi^{+}
in the lower domain, where the estimate follows from (
A.5
) and (
2.24
):

\displaystyle\|\Lambda^{s}\nabla^{\mu}_{x,z}\phi^{+}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{\pm})}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\mu}M|\psi^{+}|_{\dot{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\mu}M|(\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\psi|_{\dot{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\mu}M|\psi|_{\dot{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}.

For the upper half plane, we use Proposition
2.4
we use Remark
A.9
together with the first point of Proposition
A.13
to deduce a unique solution
\phi^{-}
. The estimate is a consequence of estimates (
A.23
), (
2.11
), and then (
2.24
):

\displaystyle\|\Lambda^{s}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}\phi^{-}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{-})}

\displaystyle\leq\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}M|\psi^{-}|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle=\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}\gamma M|(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi^{+}|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\mu}M|\psi^{+}|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\mu}M|\psi|_{\dot{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}.

∎

We may now give the proof of the main result of the section.

Proof of Proposition
2.1
.

We prove each point individually in four separate steps.

Step 1.
The proof of estimate (
2.2
) and (
2.3
) follows by (
A.7
), (
2.24
), and Plancherel’s identity:

\displaystyle|\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle=|\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\psi|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq\mu^{\frac{3}{4}}|(\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\psi|_{\dot{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}

\displaystyle\leq\mu^{\frac{3}{4}}|\psi|_{\dot{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\mu}|\partial_{x}\psi|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}.

Step 2.
The proof of (
2.4
) and (
2.5
), is proved the same way as in Step
1
, but we instead use (
A.8
).

Step 3.
The coercivity estimate (
2.6
) follows by construction where use the identities in Remark
2.11
to get that

\displaystyle\big(\psi,\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi\big)_{L^{2}}=\big(\psi,\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\psi\big)_{L^{2}}=\big(\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi^{+},\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi^{+}\big)_{L^{2}}.

Now, argue as in the proof Lemma
2.10
where estimates (
A.4
) and (
2.25
) implies

\displaystyle\big(\psi,\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi\big)_{L^{2}}\geq\frac{\mu}{1+M}|\psi^{+}|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}\geq\frac{\mu}{1+M}|\psi|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}.

Step 4.
The symmetry follows by the second point in Propostions
A.4
and
A.13
.

Step 5.
Finally, for the proof estimate (
2.8
) we use (
A.6
) and Lemma
2.10
.

∎

3.
Symbolic analysis of the Dirichlet-Neumann operator

In this section, we will give a symbolic description of the operator
\mathcal{G}_{\mu}
defined given by (
2.1
). The estimates need to be precise in terms of the parameters
\mu,{\varepsilon}
, and where we carefully track the Sobolev regularity with respect to
\psi
and
\zeta
. One reason for these expressions is that we need to have an estimate of the type:

(3.1)

|\big(\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\circ(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\big(\partial_{x}(fg)\big),f\big)_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}|\leq M(t_{0}+3)|g|_{H^{t_{0}+1}}|f|^{2}_{L^{2}},

which appears naturally in the energy estimates and the quasilinearisation of the main equations. As we can see from (
3.1
), one needs to absorb a derivative and be uniform with respect to the small parameters. Also, recall that

\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi=\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\Big(1-\gamma(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\Big)^{-1}\psi,

which means we need a good symbolic description of each of the operators involved in the expression. This is the strategy that was implemented in
[
63
,
65
]
, where we first will consider the symbolic description of
\mathcal{G}^{\pm}_{\mu}
, then we treat the inverse operators that are involved.

3.1.
Symbolic analysis of
\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{\pm}

For the symbolic description of
\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}
, we know that the operator coincides with the ones studied in
[
65
]
. In particular, we can use Theorem
4
, in dimension one, which is one of the key estimates of the paper:

Proposition 3.1

(Theorem 4 in
[
65
]
)
.

Let
t_{0}>\frac{1}{2}
and
\zeta\in H^{t_{0}+3}({\mathbb{R}})
be such that (
1.16
) is satisfied. Then for all
0\leq s\leq t_{0}
and
\psi\in\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
, one can approximate the positive Dirichlet-Neumann operator by

\mathrm{Op}(S^{+})\psi(x)=\sqrt{\mu}\mathcal{F}^{-1}\Big(|\xi|\tanh(\sqrt{\mu}t(x,\xi)\hat{\psi}(\xi)\Big)(x),

where we define the “tail”  by the symbol

(3.2)

t(x,\xi)=(1+{\varepsilon}\zeta)\frac{\arctan({\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}\partial_{x}\zeta)}{{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}\partial_{x}\zeta}|\xi|.

Moreover, for
k=0,1
, the operator satisfy

(3.3)

|\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi-\mathrm{Op}(S^{+})\psi|_{H^{s+\frac{k}{2}}}\leq{\varepsilon}\mu^{1-\frac{k}{4}}M(t_{0}+3)|\psi|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}.

In the case of infinite depth, it is pointed out in Remark
17
in
[
65
]
that the tail effects vanish (formally) since the hyperbolic tangent is a bottom effect. In particular, we have the following result that proves this fact.

Proposition 3.2
.

Let
t_{0}\geq 1
and
\zeta\in H^{t_{0}+3}({\mathbb{R}})
. Then for all
0\leq s\leq t_{0}
and
\psi\in\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
, one can approximate the negative Dirichlet-Neumann operator by

S^{-}(\mathrm{D})=-\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|,

where we have the following estimate

(3.4)

|\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi-\mathrm{Op}(S^{-})\psi|_{H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}\leq{\varepsilon}\mu M(t_{0}+3)|\psi|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}.

Remark 3.3
.

The proof can be seen as a modified version of the proof presented in
[
65
]
, but where we need to make two approximations of an elliptic problem depending on the frequencies and weighted estimates to deal with some integrability issues in
\mathcal{S}^{-}={\mathbb{R}}\times[0,\infty)
. The weights and the cut-off functions are adapted to the approximation and deal with separate issues. We also note that the proof in
[
65
]
also extends to two dimensions. In fact, Proposition
3.2
is mainly the result that restricts Theorem
1.7
to one horizontal dimension in this paper. However, we expect the result to be true for
X\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
by modifying the ansatz in the proof and letting
\mathrm{Op}(S^{-})
be given by

\mathrm{Op}(S^{-})\psi(X)=-\sqrt{\mu}\mathcal{F}^{-1}\Big(\sqrt{|\xi|^{2}+{\varepsilon}^{2}\mu\big((|\nabla_{X}\zeta|^{2}|\xi|^{2})-(\nabla_{X}\zeta\cdot\xi)^{2}\big)}\hat{\psi}(\xi)\Big)(X),

for
X,\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
.

Proof.

The proof will be given in several steps, where we first decompose the estimate into two main parts depending on the frequencies. In particular, let
\chi_{1}
be a Fourier multiplier with a smooth symbol and equal to one around zero. Also, let
\chi_{2}=1-\chi_{1}
be the high-frequency part. Then we have by duality

\displaystyle|\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi+\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|\psi|_{H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle=\sup\limits_{|\varphi|_{L^{2}}=1}\Big|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\Lambda^{s+\frac{1}{2}}\big(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi+\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|\psi\big)\chi_{1}(\mathrm{D})\varphi\>\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\Lambda^{s+\frac{1}{2}}\big(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi+\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|\psi\big)\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\varphi\>\mathrm{d}x\Big|

(3.5)

\displaystyle\leq\sup\limits_{|\varphi|_{L^{2}}=1}\Big(|I_{1}(\varphi)|+|I_{2}(\varphi)|\Big).

We will now make two approximations of the elliptic problem (
A.20
), where the estimate on
I_{1}
can be made using the Poisson kernel. On the other hand, the estimate on
I_{2}
requires an approximate solution of (
A.20
) that accounts for the principal part of the elliptic operator and is “well-behaved”  in high frequency on weighted Sobolev spaces.

Step 1.

Approximate solutions for
I_{1}
. We know that
\phi_{\mathrm{app}}^{1}=e^{-z\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|}\psi
solves

(3.6)

\begin{cases}(\mu\partial_{x}^{2}+\partial_{z}^{2})\phi_{\text{app}}^{1}=0\quad\text{in}\quad\mathcal{S}^{-}\\
\phi_{\text{app}}^{1}|_{z=0}=\psi,\end{cases}

and

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\phi_{\mathrm{app}}^{1}|_{z=0}=-\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|\psi.

Now, let
\phi^{-}
be the solution of (
A.20
) and define
u_{1}=\phi^{-}-\phi_{\mathrm{app}}^{1}
. Then
u_{1}
solves

(3.7)

\begin{cases}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}\cdot P(\Sigma^{-})\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u_{1}={\varepsilon}\mu r_{1}\quad\text{in}\quad\mathcal{S}^{-}\\
u_{1}|_{z=0}=0,\quad\lim\limits_{z\rightarrow\infty}|\nabla^{\mu}_{x,z}u_{1}|=0,\end{cases}

where
r_{1}
reads

\displaystyle r_{1}

\displaystyle=\partial_{x}\big((\partial_{x}\zeta)\partial_{z}\phi_{\mathrm{app}}^{1}\big)+(\partial_{x}\zeta)\partial_{x}\partial_{z}\phi_{\mathrm{app}}^{1}-{\varepsilon}(\partial_{x}\zeta)^{2}\partial_{z}^{2}\phi_{\mathrm{app}}^{1}.

Moreover, we have by construction that

\displaystyle\partial_{n}^{P^{-}}u_{1}|_{z=0}=\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi+\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|\psi+{\varepsilon}\mu r_{2},

where the second rest is given by

r_{2}=(\partial_{x}\zeta)\partial_{x}\psi+{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}(\partial_{x}\zeta)^{2}|\mathrm{D}|\psi.

Step 1.1.

Estimate on
I_{1}
. From the previous step, we deduce that

\displaystyle|I_{1}(\varphi)|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}(\Lambda^{s+\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{n}^{P^{-}}u_{1}|_{z=0})\chi_{1}(\mathrm{D})\varphi\>\mathrm{d}x\Big|+{\varepsilon}\mu\Big|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}(\Lambda^{s+\frac{1}{2}}r_{2})\chi_{1}(\mathrm{D})\varphi\>\mathrm{d}x\Big|

\displaystyle=|I_{1}^{1}(\varphi)|+|I_{1}^{2}(\varphi)|.

We will now estimate each piece separately. For the estimate on
|I_{1}^{1}(\varphi)|
, we use the Divergence Theorem to deduce that

\displaystyle I_{1}^{1}(\varphi)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{S}^{-}}P(\Sigma^{-})\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u_{1}\cdot(\Lambda^{s+\frac{1}{2}}\chi_{1}(\mathrm{D})\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}\varphi^{\mathrm{ext_{1}}})\>\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}z+{\varepsilon}\mu\int_{\mathcal{S}^{-}}r_{1}(\Lambda^{s+\frac{1}{2}}\chi_{1}(\mathrm{D})\varphi^{\mathrm{ext_{1}}})\>\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=I_{1}^{1,1}(\varphi)+I_{1}^{1,2}(\varphi).

Here we let
\varphi^{\mathrm{ext_{1}}}
be the extension of
\varphi
onto the upper half-plane defined by

\varphi^{\mathrm{ext_{1}}}(x,z)=\eta(\sqrt{\mu}z)\varphi(x),

where
\eta(s)
is a positive smooth function supported on
[0,1]
. Then we estimate each term separately. For the estimate on
I_{1}^{1,1}
, we first use Hölder inequality and Sobolev embedding to deduce that

(3.8)

\displaystyle|I_{1}^{1,1}(\varphi)|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\mu}(|\eta|_{L^{\infty}}+|\eta^{\prime}|_{L^{\infty}})M\|\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u_{1}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{-})}|\varphi|_{L^{2}}.

To conclude this estimate, we use (
1.40
) and the Divergence Theorem to make the following observation:

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u_{1}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{-})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq M\int_{\mathcal{S}^{-}}P(\Sigma^{-})\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u_{1}\cdot\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u_{1}\>\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}\mu M\Big|\int_{\mathcal{S}^{-}}r_{1}u_{1}\>\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}z\Big|.

Then use the definition of
r_{1}
and integration by parts to distribute the derivatives in a convenient way:

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u_{1}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{-})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}\mu\Big|\int_{\mathcal{S}^{-}}(\partial_{x}\zeta)(\partial_{z}\phi_{\mathrm{app}}^{1})\partial_{x}u_{1}\>\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}z\Big|+{\varepsilon}\mu\Big|\int_{\mathcal{S}^{-}}(\partial_{x}\zeta)(\partial_{x}\phi_{\mathrm{app}}^{1})\partial_{z}u_{1}\>\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}z\Big|

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+{\varepsilon}^{2}\mu\Big|\int_{\mathcal{S}^{-}}(\partial_{x}\zeta)^{2}\partial_{z}\phi_{\mathrm{app}}^{1}\partial_{z}u_{1}\>\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}z\Big|.

In particular, if we apply Hölder’s inequality, Sobolev embedding, and integrate the definition of
\phi_{\mathrm{app}}^{1}
in
z
, we find that

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u_{1}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{-})}

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}\mu M\|e^{-z\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|}|\mathrm{D}|\psi\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{-})}

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}\mu^{\frac{3}{4}}M|\psi|_{\mathring{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}},

and we use it to can contol (
3.8
) by

\displaystyle|I_{1}^{1,1}(\varphi)|

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}\mu M|\psi|_{\mathring{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}|\varphi|_{L^{2}}.

Moreover, the estimate on
I_{1}^{1,2}(\varphi)
contains
r_{1}
, and can be estimated similarly by integrating only in
x
and using the cut-off
\chi_{1}
to obtain that

\displaystyle|I_{1}^{1,2}(\varphi)|

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}\mu\Big(\Big|\int_{\mathcal{S}^{-}}(\partial_{x}\zeta)(\partial_{z}\phi_{\mathrm{app}}^{1})\partial_{x}(\Lambda^{s+\frac{1}{2}}\chi_{1}(\mathrm{D})\varphi^{\mathrm{ext_{1}}})\>\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}z\Big|

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\Big|\int_{\mathcal{S}^{-}}(\partial_{z}\phi_{\mathrm{app}}^{1})\partial_{x}\big((\partial_{x}\zeta)\Lambda^{s+\frac{1}{2}}\chi_{1}(\mathrm{D})\varphi^{\mathrm{ext_{1}}}\big)\>\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}z\Big|

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+{\varepsilon}\mu\Big|\int_{\mathcal{S}^{-}}(\partial_{x}e^{-z\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|}\psi)\partial_{x}\big((\partial_{x}\zeta)^{2}\Lambda^{s+\frac{1}{2}}\chi_{1}(\mathrm{D})\varphi^{\mathrm{ext_{1}}}\big)\>\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}z\Big|\Big)

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}\mu M|\psi|_{\mathring{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}|\varphi|_{L^{2}}.

Finally, we arrive at the estimate of
I_{1}^{2}(\varphi)
. Here we use the definition of
r_{2}
to find that

\displaystyle|I_{1}^{2}(\varphi)|

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}\mu\Big|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}(\partial_{x}\zeta)(\partial_{x}\psi)(\Lambda^{s+\frac{1}{2}}\chi_{1}(\mathrm{D})\varphi)\>\mathrm{d}x\Big|+{\varepsilon}^{2}\mu^{\frac{3}{2}}\Big|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}(\partial_{x}\zeta)^{2}(|\mathrm{D}|\psi)(\Lambda^{s+\frac{1}{2}}\chi_{1}(\mathrm{D})\varphi)\>\mathrm{d}x\Big|

\displaystyle=|I_{1}^{2,1}(\varphi)|+|I_{1}^{2,2}(\varphi)|.

Each term is estimated similarly, and for the estimate of the first term, we recall
\partial_{x}=-\mathcal{H}|\mathrm{D}|
, where
\mathcal{H}
is the Hilbert transform. Consequently, we have from Hölder’s inequality, Sobolev embedding, and commutator estimate (
B.18
) that

\displaystyle|I_{1}^{2,1}(\varphi)|

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}\mu\Big|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}[\partial_{x}\zeta,|\mathrm{D}|^{\frac{1}{2}}]\mathcal{H}|\mathrm{D}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\psi)(\Lambda^{s+\frac{1}{2}}\chi_{1}(\mathrm{D})\varphi)\>\mathrm{d}x\Big|

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+{\varepsilon}\mu\Big|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}(\partial_{x}\zeta)(\mathcal{H}|\mathrm{D}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\psi)(\Lambda^{s+\frac{1}{2}}|\mathrm{D}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\chi_{1}(\mathrm{D})\varphi)\>\mathrm{d}x\Big|

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}\mu M|\psi|_{\mathring{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}|\varphi|_{L^{2}}.

We may now collect all these estimates to conclude this step:

\displaystyle|I_{1}(\varphi)|\leq{\varepsilon}\mu M|\psi|_{\mathring{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}|\varphi|_{L^{2}}.

Step 2.

Approximate solutions for
I_{2}
. By using the approach in
[
63
,
65
]
we can show that the function:

(3.9)

\phi_{\text{app}}^{2}(x,z)=(\text{Op}(L)\psi)(x,z)=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\Big(L(x,\xi,z)\hat{\psi}(\xi)\Big)(x),

where
L
is a symbol given by

L(x,\xi,z)=e^{-z\big(\frac{\sqrt{\mu}|\xi|}{1+{\varepsilon}^{2}\mu(\partial_{x}\zeta)^{2}}-i\frac{{\varepsilon}\mu\partial_{x}\zeta\xi}{1+{\varepsilon}^{2}\mu(\partial_{x}\zeta)^{2}}\big)},

provides a good approximation of the elliptic problem (
A.20
) and, by extension, an approximation of the Neumann condition at the boundary. To see this, we use Definition
1.23
for
P(\Sigma^{-})
, and (
3.9
) to compute
(\partial_{n}^{P^{-}}\phi^{2}_{\text{app}})|_{z=0}
:

\displaystyle(\partial_{n}^{P^{-}}\phi^{2}_{\text{app}})|_{z=0}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{e}_{z}\cdot P(\Sigma^{-})\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}\phi^{2}_{\text{app}}|_{z=0}

\displaystyle=-\varepsilon\mu(\partial_{x}\zeta)\partial_{x}\phi^{2}_{\text{app}}|_{z=0}+\big(1+\varepsilon^{2}\mu(\partial_{x}\zeta)^{2}\big)\partial_{z}\phi^{2}_{\text{app}}|_{z=0}

\displaystyle=-\varepsilon\mu(\partial_{x}\zeta)\mathrm{Op}(L)\partial_{x}\psi|_{z=0}+\big(1+\varepsilon^{2}\mu(\partial_{x}\zeta)^{2}\big)\mathrm{Op}(\partial_{z}L)\psi|_{z=0}

\displaystyle=-\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|\psi.

where used that
\partial_{x}L|_{z=0}=0
. In fact, the approximation
\phi_{\text{app}}^{2}
is constructed from the solution of the following ODE:

(1+{\varepsilon}^{2}\mu(\partial_{x}\zeta)^{2})\partial_{z}^{2}L-2i{\varepsilon}\mu(\partial_{x}\zeta)\xi\partial_{z}L-\mu\xi^{2}L=0,

which corresponds to the principal part of (
A.20
) with “frozen coefficients”. As a result, we have that
u_{2}=\phi^{-}-\phi_{\mathrm{app}}^{2}
solves

(3.10)

\begin{cases}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}\cdot P(\Sigma^{-})\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u_{2}={\varepsilon}\mu r_{3}\quad\text{in}\quad\mathcal{S}^{-}\\
u_{2}|_{z=0}=0,\quad\lim\limits_{z\rightarrow\infty}\omega(z)|\nabla^{\mu}_{x,z}u_{2}|=0,\end{cases}

where we simply take
\omega(z)=e^{-\frac{z}{2}}
and the rest term can be computed explicitly:

\displaystyle r_{3}=2\text{Op}((\partial_{x}\zeta)\partial_{x}\partial_{z}L)\psi-2i\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}}\text{Op}(\partial_{x}L\xi)\psi-\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}}\text{Op}(\partial_{x}^{2}L)\psi+\mathrm{Op}\big((\partial_{x}^{2}\zeta)\partial_{z}L\big)\psi.

One should note that derivatives in
x
on
L
give rise to
{\varepsilon}\mu
and is polynomial in
z
. The polynomial dependence in
z
will require weighted estimates in high frequency to get an estimate for
\psi\in\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
.

Step 2.2.

Estimate on
I_{2}
. By construction and the Divergence Theorem, we have that

\displaystyle I_{2}(\varphi)

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}(\Lambda^{s+\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{n}^{P^{-}}u_{2}|_{z=0})\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\varphi\>\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{S}^{-}}\Lambda^{s+1}P(\Sigma^{-})\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u_{2}\cdot(\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}\varphi^{\mathrm{ext_{2}}})\>\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+{\varepsilon}\mu\int_{\mathcal{S}^{-}}(\Lambda^{s}r_{3})(\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\varphi^{\mathrm{ext_{2}}})\>\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=I_{2}^{1}(\varphi)+I_{2}^{2}(\varphi).

We now need to incorporate a weight in the estimates. To do so, we let
\varphi^{\mathrm{ext_{2}}}
be the extension of
\varphi
onto the upper half-plane defined by

\varphi^{\mathrm{ext_{2}}}(x,z)=(\omega_{\mu}(z))^{2}e^{-z\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|}\varphi(x).

Here we let
\omega_{\mu}(z)=e^{-\frac{\sqrt{\mu}z}{2M}}
. For the estimate on
I_{2}^{1}(\varphi)
we first make the decomposition

\displaystyle I_{2}^{1}(\varphi)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{S}^{-}}\omega_{\mu}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\Lambda^{s+1}P(\Sigma^{-})\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u_{2}\cdot(\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\omega_{\mu})^{-1}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}\varphi^{\mathrm{ext_{2}}})\>\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{S}^{-}}[\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\Lambda^{s+1},P(\Sigma^{-})]\omega_{\mu}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u_{2}\cdot(\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\omega_{\mu})^{-1}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}\varphi^{\mathrm{ext_{2}}})\>\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\int_{\mathcal{S}^{-}}P(\Sigma^{-})\Lambda^{s+1}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\omega_{\mu}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u_{2}\cdot(\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\omega_{\mu})^{-1}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}\varphi^{\mathrm{ext_{2}}})\>\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=I_{2}^{1,1}(\varphi)+I_{2}^{1,2}(\varphi).

For the control of
I_{2}^{1,1}(\varphi)
, we use Cauchy-Schwarz, commutator estimate (
B.16
), and the half-derivative smoothing from the Poisson kernel:

(3.11)

\||\mathrm{D}|^{\frac{1}{2}}e^{-z\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|}\varphi\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{-})}\leq\mu^{-\frac{1}{4}}|\varphi|_{L^{2}},

and the weight estimate:

\|\omega_{\mu}(z)\varphi\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{-})}\leq\mu^{-\frac{1}{4}}M|\varphi|_{L^{2}},

to get that

\displaystyle|I_{2}^{1,1}(\varphi)|

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}M\|\omega_{\mu}\Lambda^{s}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u_{2}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{-})}\|\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\omega_{\mu})^{-1}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}\varphi^{\mathrm{ext_{2}}})\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{-})}

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}M\|\omega_{\mu}\Lambda^{s}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u_{2}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{-})}\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}|\varphi|_{L^{2}}.

We also use the definition
u_{2}=\phi^{-}-\phi_{\mathrm{app}}^{2}
, the decay of the weight, estimates (
A.23
) and the ones provided in Lemma
B.12
to obtain

\displaystyle\|\omega_{\mu}\Lambda^{s}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u_{2}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{-})}

\displaystyle\leq\|\Lambda^{s}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}\phi\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{-})}+\|\frac{1}{z}\Lambda^{s}\mathrm{Op}(\partial_{x}L)\psi\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{-})}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\sqrt{\mu}\|\Lambda^{s}\mathrm{Op}(L)\partial_{x}\psi\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{-})}+\|\Lambda^{s}\mathrm{Op}(\partial_{z}L)\psi\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{-})}

\displaystyle\leq\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}M|\psi|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}.

We therefore have

\displaystyle|I_{2}^{1,1}(\varphi)|\leq{\varepsilon}\mu M|\psi|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}|\varphi|_{L^{2}}.

For the estimate on
I_{2}^{1,2}(\varphi)
, we use Hölder’s inequality and Sobolev embedding to find that

\displaystyle|I_{2}^{1,2}(\varphi)|

\displaystyle\leq\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}M\|\Lambda^{s+1}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\omega_{\mu}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u_{2}\|_{L^{2}(S^{-})}|\varphi|_{L^{2}}.

So we have again reduced the problem to an elliptic estimate. Using the coercivity of
P(\Sigma^{-})
and integration by parts we find that

(3.12)

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle=\|\Lambda^{s+1}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\omega_{\mu}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u_{2}\|_{L^{2}(S^{-})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq M\Big|\int_{\mathcal{S}^{-}}P(\Sigma^{-})\Lambda^{s+1}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\omega_{\mu}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u_{2}\cdot\Lambda^{s+1}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\omega_{\mu}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u_{2}\>\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}z\Big|

\displaystyle\leq M\Big|\int_{\mathcal{S}^{-}}(\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}\cdot P(\Sigma^{-})\Lambda^{s+1}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\omega_{\mu}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u_{2})(\Lambda^{s+1}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\omega_{\mu}u_{2})\>\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}z\Big|

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+M\Big|\int_{\mathcal{S}^{-}}(\mathbf{e}_{z}\cdot P(\Sigma^{-})\Lambda^{s+1}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\omega_{\mu}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u_{2})(\Lambda^{s+1}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\omega^{\prime}_{\mu}u_{2})\>\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}z\Big|

\displaystyle=M\cdot(A_{1}+A_{2}).

Before estimating
A_{1}
, we will decompose it into three pieces:

\displaystyle A_{1}

\displaystyle=\Big|\int_{\mathcal{S}^{-}}(\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}\cdot[P(\Sigma^{-}),\Lambda^{s+1}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})]\omega_{\mu}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u_{2})(\Lambda^{s+1}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\omega_{\mu}u_{2})\>\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}z\Big|

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+{\varepsilon}\mu\Big|\int_{\mathcal{S}^{-}}(\Lambda^{s+1}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\omega_{\mu}r_{3})(\Lambda^{s+1}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\omega_{\mu}u_{2})\>\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}z\Big|

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\Big|\int_{\mathcal{S}^{-}}(\Lambda^{s+1}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})(\omega_{\mu})^{\prime}\mathbf{e}_{z}\cdot P(\Sigma^{-})\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u_{2})(\Lambda^{s+1}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\omega_{\mu}u_{2})\>\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}z\Big|

\displaystyle=A_{1}^{1}+A_{1}^{2}+A_{1}^{3}.

For the estimate on
A_{1}^{1}
, we write out each term explicitly to clearly see the terms we need to treat. In particular, we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}\cdot[P(\Sigma^{-}),\Lambda^{s+1}\chi_{2}

\displaystyle(\mathrm{D})]\omega_{\mu}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u_{2}

\displaystyle=-{\varepsilon}\mu\partial_{x}[\partial_{x}\zeta,\Lambda^{s+1}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})]\omega_{\mu}\partial_{z}u_{2}-{\varepsilon}\mu\partial_{z}[\partial_{x}\zeta,\Lambda^{s+1}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})]\omega_{\mu}\partial_{x}u_{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+{\varepsilon}^{2}\mu\partial_{z}[(\partial_{x}\zeta)^{2},\Lambda^{s+1}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})]\omega_{\mu}\partial_{z}u_{2}.

Then using this decomposition together with Plancherel’s identity, integration by parts, commutator estimate (
B.16
), and the support of
\chi_{2}
, we find that

\displaystyle A_{1}^{1}

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}\mu\Big|\int_{\mathcal{S}^{-}}(\Lambda^{-1}\partial_{x}[\partial_{x}\zeta,\Lambda^{s+1}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})]\omega_{\mu}\partial_{z}u_{2})(\Lambda^{s+2}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\omega_{\mu}u_{2})\>\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}z\Big|

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+{\varepsilon}\mu\Big|\int_{\mathcal{S}^{-}}([\partial_{x}^{2}\zeta,\Lambda^{s+1}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})]\omega_{\mu}\partial_{z}u_{2})(\Lambda^{s+1}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\omega_{\mu}u_{2})\>\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}z\Big|

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+{\varepsilon}\mu\Big|\int_{\mathcal{S}^{-}}([\partial_{x}\zeta,\Lambda^{s+1}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})]\omega_{\mu}\partial_{z}u_{2})(\Lambda^{s+1}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\omega_{\mu}\partial_{x}u_{2})\>\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}z\Big|

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+{\varepsilon}^{2}\mu\Big|\int_{\mathcal{S}^{-}}([\partial_{x}\zeta,\Lambda^{s+1}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})]\omega_{\mu}\partial_{z}u_{2})(\Lambda^{s+1}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\partial_{z}(\omega_{\mu}u_{2}))\>\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}z\Big|

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}\mu M\|\Lambda^{s}\partial_{z}u_{2}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{-})}\|\Lambda^{s+1}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\omega_{\mu}\nabla_{x,z}u_{2}\|_{L^{2}(S^{-})}.

Then to conclude this part, we simply use that
u_{2}=\phi^{-}-\phi_{\mathrm{app}}^{2}
, together with the estimates (
A.23
) and Lemma
B.12
:

\displaystyle A_{1}^{1}

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}\mu^{1+\frac{1}{4}}M|\psi|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}\|\Lambda^{s+1}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\omega_{\mu}\nabla_{x,z}u_{2}\|_{L^{2}(S^{-})}

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}\mu^{\frac{3}{4}}M|\psi|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}\|\Lambda^{s+1}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\omega_{\mu}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u_{2}\|_{L^{2}(S^{-})}.

Next, we make an estimate on
A_{1}^{2}
using the definition of
r_{3}
in the previous step:

\displaystyle A_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2{\varepsilon}\mu\Big|\int_{\mathcal{S}^{-}}(\Lambda^{s}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\omega_{\mu}\text{Op}((\partial_{x}\zeta)\partial_{x}\partial_{z}L)\psi)(\Lambda^{s+2}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\omega_{\mu}u_{2})\>\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}z\Big|

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+2\mu\Big|\int_{\mathcal{S}^{-}}(\Lambda^{s}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\omega_{\mu}\text{Op}(\partial_{x}L)\partial_{x}\psi)(\Lambda^{s+2}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\omega_{\mu}u_{2})\>\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}z\Big|

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+{\varepsilon}\mu\Big|\int_{\mathcal{S}^{-}}(\Lambda^{s}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\omega_{\mu}\mathrm{Op}\big((\partial_{x}^{2}\zeta)\partial_{z}L\big)\psi)(\Lambda^{s+2}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\omega_{\mu}u_{2})\>\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}z\Big|

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\mu\Big|\int_{\mathcal{S}^{-}}(\Lambda^{s}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\omega_{\mu}\text{Op}(\partial_{x}^{2}L)\psi)(\Lambda^{s+2}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\omega_{\mu}u_{2})\>\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}z\Big|

\displaystyle=A_{1}^{2,1}+A_{1}^{2,2}+A_{1}^{2,3}+A_{1}^{2,4}.

The first three terms are easily estimated by Lemma
B.12
, with Remark
B.13
, and the support of
\chi_{2}
where we find that

\displaystyle A_{1}^{2,1}+A_{1}^{2,2}+A_{1}^{2,3}

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}\mu^{1+\frac{1}{4}}M|\psi|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}\|\Lambda^{s+1}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\omega_{\mu}\nabla_{x,z}u_{2}\|_{L^{2}(S^{-})}

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}\mu^{\frac{3}{4}}M|\psi|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}\|\Lambda^{s+1}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\omega_{\mu}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u_{2}\|_{L^{2}(S^{-})}.

For the last term, we use the weight to gain decay in
z
. Then apply the same estimates as above to find that

\displaystyle A_{1}^{2,4}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\mu}\|\Lambda^{s}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\text{Op}(\frac{1}{z}\partial_{x}^{2}L)\psi\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{-})}\|\Lambda^{s+2}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\omega_{\mu}u_{2}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{-})}

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}\mu^{\frac{3}{4}}M(t_{0}+3)|\psi|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}\|\Lambda^{s+1}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\omega_{\mu}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u_{2}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{-})}.

As a result, we have the same estimate on
A_{1}^{2}
, and so we proceed with the estimate on
A_{1}^{3}
. We observe by definition of
P(\Sigma^{-})
that we have one term without
{\varepsilon}
that need special attention:

\displaystyle A_{1}^{3}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{{\varepsilon}\mu^{\frac{3}{2}}}{2M}\Big|\int_{\mathcal{S}^{-}}(\Lambda^{s}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\omega_{\mu}(\partial_{x}\zeta)\partial_{x}u_{2})(\Lambda^{s+2}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\omega_{\mu}u_{2})\>\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}z\Big|

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\frac{{\varepsilon}^{2}\mu^{\frac{3}{2}}}{2M}\Big|\int_{\mathcal{S}^{-}}(\Lambda^{s}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\omega_{\mu}(\partial_{x}\zeta)^{2}\partial_{z}u_{2})(\Lambda^{s+2}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\omega_{\mu}u_{2})\>\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}z\Big|

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\frac{\sqrt{\mu}}{2M}\Big|\int_{\mathcal{S}^{-}}(\Lambda^{s+1}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\omega_{\mu}\partial_{z}u_{2})(\Lambda^{s+1}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\omega_{\mu}u_{2})\>\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}z\Big|

\displaystyle=A_{1}^{3,1}+A_{1}^{3,2}+A_{1}^{3,3}.

Here we see that the first two terms are easily estimated by using the weight to gain decay and then combining it with the estimates in Lemma
B.12
,

\displaystyle A_{1}^{3,1}+A_{1}^{3,2}\leq{\varepsilon}\mu^{\frac{3}{4}}M(t_{0}+1)|\psi|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}\|\Lambda^{s+1}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\omega_{\mu}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u_{2}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{-})}.

Lastly, for
A_{1}^{3,3}
we use integration by parts and the support of
\chi_{2}
to find that

\displaystyle A_{1}^{3,3}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mu}{4M}\Big|\int_{\mathcal{S}^{-}}(\Lambda^{s+1}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\omega_{\mu}u_{2})(\Lambda^{s+1}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\omega_{\mu}u_{2})\>\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}z\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mu}{4M}\|\Lambda^{s+1}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\omega_{\mu}\nabla_{x,z}u_{2}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{-})}^{2},

and therefore can be reabsorbed into
A
. The only remaining estimate is now on
A_{2}
. However, we note that it is similar to
A_{1}^{3}
, and the same estimates apply. In conclusion, we may gather all these estimates and use (
3.12
) to find that

\displaystyle(1-\frac{1}{4})\|\Lambda^{s+1}\chi_{2}(\mathrm{D})\omega_{\mu}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u_{2}\|_{L^{2}(S^{-})}\leq{\varepsilon}\mu^{\frac{3}{4}}M(t_{0}+3)|\psi|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}.

Returning to
I_{2}^{1}
, we obtain the bound

\displaystyle|I_{2}^{1}(\varphi)|\leq{\varepsilon}\mu M(t_{0}+3)|\psi|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}|\varphi|_{L^{2}}.

To conclude this step, it only remains to estimate
I_{2}^{2}(\varphi)
. However, this is just a simple version of
A_{1}^{2}
and the following estimate is easy to deduce

\displaystyle|I_{2}(\varphi)|

\displaystyle\leq|I_{2}^{1}(\varphi)|+|I_{2}^{2}(\varphi)|

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}\mu M(t_{0}+3)|\psi|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}|\varphi|_{L^{2}}.

Step 3.

Conclusion of proof
. We have from Step
1
. and Step
2
. that the result holds:

\displaystyle|\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi+\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|\psi|_{H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup\limits_{|\varphi|_{L^{2}}=1}\Big(|I_{1}(\varphi)|+|I_{2}(\varphi)|\Big)

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}\mu M(t_{0}+3)|\psi|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}.

∎

3.2.
Symbolic analysis of
(\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{-})^{-1}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{+}

The next step in studying the symbolic behaviour of
\mathcal{G}_{\mu}
is to understand the composition of
(\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{-})^{-1}
with
\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{+}
.

Corollary 3.4
.

Let
t_{0}\geq 1
and
\zeta\in H^{t_{0}+3}({\mathbb{R}})
be such that (
1.16
) is satisfied. Then for all
0\leq s\leq t_{0}
and
f\in\mathscr{S}({\mathbb{R}})
one can approximate the operator
(\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{-})^{-1}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{+}
by

\mathrm{Op}\Big(\frac{S^{+}}{S^{-}}\Big)f(x)=-\mathcal{F}^{-1}\big(\tanh(\sqrt{\mu}t(x,\xi))\hat{f}(\xi)\big)(x),

where
t
is defined by (
3.2
). Moreover, there holds,

(3.13)

|(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]f-\mathrm{Op}\Big(\frac{S^{+}}{S^{-}}\Big)f|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}\leq{\varepsilon}\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}M(t_{0}+3)|f|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Remark 3.5
.

From the symmetry of the Dirichlet-Neumann operators
\mathcal{G}^{\pm}
they can be defined on Sobolev spaces of negative order by duality. See Remark
3.17
in
[
66
]
.

Proof.

We define
\tilde{\psi}^{-}=\mathrm{Op}\Big(\frac{S^{+}}{S^{-}}\Big)f
and let
\tilde{\phi}^{-}
be a solution of

\begin{cases}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}\cdot P(\Sigma^{-})\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}\tilde{\phi}^{-}=0\hskip 19.91684pt\qquad\text{in}\quad\mathcal{S}^{-}\\
\tilde{\phi}^{-}=\tilde{\psi}^{-}\hskip 115.23373pt\text{on}\quad z=0.\end{cases}

Then we can make the definition

\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\tilde{\psi}^{-}=\partial_{n}\tilde{\phi}^{-}|_{z=0}.

Now, let
\phi^{-}
be defined by the solution of

(3.14)

\begin{cases}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}\cdot P(\Sigma^{-})\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}\phi^{-}=0\hskip 14.22636pt\qquad\text{in}\quad\mathcal{S}^{-}\\
\partial_{n}^{P^{-}}\phi^{-}=\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]f\hskip 65.44142pt\text{on}\quad z=0.\end{cases}

Then the difference
u=\phi^{-}-\tilde{\phi}^{-}
satisfies

(3.15)

\begin{cases}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}\cdot P(\Sigma^{-})\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u=0\hskip 48.36958pt\qquad\text{in}\quad\mathcal{S}^{-}\\
\partial_{n}^{P^{-}}u=\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]f-\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\tilde{\psi}^{-}\hskip 39.83368pt\text{on}\quad z=0.\end{cases}

It is straightforward to prove that these problems are well-defined where both
\phi^{-}
and
\tilde{\phi}^{-}
satisfy (
2.14
). Consequently, we can consider the variational equation:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{S}^{-}}P(\Sigma^{-})\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}\Lambda^{s}u\cdot\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}\Lambda^{s}u\>\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=-\int_{\{z=0\}}\Lambda^{s}(\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]f-\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\tilde{\psi}^{-})\Lambda^{s}u_{0}\>\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\int_{\mathcal{S}^{-}}[\Lambda^{s},P(\Sigma^{-})]\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u\cdot\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}\Lambda^{s}u\>\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}z,

where
u_{0}=u|_{z=0}
and trace inequality (
2.15
) implies

|u_{0}|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim\mu^{-\frac{1}{4}}\|\Lambda^{s}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{-})}.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

To conclude the proof, we need the following inequality

(3.16)

\displaystyle\Big|\big(\Lambda^{s}(\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]f-\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\tilde{\psi}^{-}),\Lambda^{s}u_{0}\big)_{L^{2}}\Big|\leq{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}M(t_{0}+3)|f|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}|u_{0}|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Assuming the inequality holds, we can argue as in the proof of Proposition
2.4
, Step
2
. to obtain the result.

To prove (
3.16
), we will decompose the left-hand side in several pieces. In particular, we first observe that

\displaystyle\big(\Lambda^{s}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]f,\Lambda^{s}u_{0}\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=\big(\mathrm{Op}\Big(\frac{S^{+}}{|\mathrm{D}|}\Big)^{\ast}\Lambda^{s}f,\Lambda^{s}|\mathrm{D}|u_{0}\big)_{L^{2}}+R_{1}+R_{2},

where the rest is given by

\displaystyle R_{1}

\displaystyle=\big(\big[\Lambda^{s},\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\big]f,\Lambda^{s}u_{0}\big)_{L^{2}},

\displaystyle R_{2}

\displaystyle=\big(\Lambda^{s-\frac{1}{2}}f,\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]-\mathrm{Op}(S^{+})\big)\Lambda^{s}u_{0}\big)_{L^{2}}.

Here we estimate
R_{1}
by Cauchy-Schwarz, (
A.9
), and (
B.4
) to get that

\displaystyle|R_{1}|

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}\mu M|f|_{\dot{H}^{s-\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}|u_{0}|_{\dot{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}M|f|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}|u_{0}|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}.

While for
R_{2}
we use (
3.3
) (with
k=1
) and (
B.4
) to get that

\displaystyle|R_{2}|

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}M(t_{0}+3)|f|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}|u_{0}|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}.

Next, we decompose the remaining part where we get that

\displaystyle\big(\Lambda^{s}\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\tilde{\psi}^{-},\Lambda^{s}u_{0}\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=\big(\mathrm{Op}\Big(\frac{S^{+}}{|\mathrm{D}|}\Big)\Lambda^{s}f,\Lambda^{s}|\mathrm{D}|u_{0}\big)_{L^{2}}+R_{3}+R_{4}+R_{5},

where the rest terms are defined by

\displaystyle R_{3}

\displaystyle=\big(\big[\Lambda^{s},\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\big]\tilde{\psi}^{-},\Lambda^{s}u_{0}\big)_{L^{2}},

\displaystyle R_{4}

\displaystyle=\big(\Lambda^{s-\frac{1}{2}}\tilde{\psi}^{-},\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]-\mathrm{Op}(S^{-})\big)\Lambda^{s}u_{0}\big)_{L^{2}},

\displaystyle R_{5}

\displaystyle=\big(\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}[\Lambda^{s},\mathrm{Op}\big(\frac{S^{+}}{S^{-}}\big)]f,\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{Op}(S^{-})\Lambda^{s}u_{0}\big)_{L^{2}}.

For
R_{3}
, the commutator estimate (
A.26
), and (
B.36
) yields,

\displaystyle|R_{3}|

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}M|\tilde{\psi}^{-}|_{\mathring{H}^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}|u_{0}|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}M|f|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}|u_{0}|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}.

For
R_{4}
, we argue similarly but use Proposition
3.2
with estimate (
3.4
) to get that

\displaystyle|R_{4}|\leq{\varepsilon}\mu M(t_{0}+3)|f|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}|u_{0}|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}.

Finally, for
R_{5}
, we apply Cauchy-Schwarz, the commutator estimate (
B.37
) to get that

\displaystyle|R_{5}|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\mu}|[\Lambda^{s},\mathrm{Op}\big(\frac{S^{+}}{S^{-}}\big)]f|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}|\Lambda^{s-\frac{1}{2}}|\mathrm{D}|u_{0}|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}M|f|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}|u_{0}|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}.

Gathering all these observations, we can estimate the left-hand side of (
3.16
) by

\mathrm{LHS}_{\eqref{claim ineq G+ - G-}}\leq\Big|\big(\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathrm{Op}\big(\frac{S^{+}}{|\mathrm{D}|}\big)^{\ast}-\mathrm{Op}\big(\frac{S^{+}}{|\mathrm{D}|}\big))\Lambda^{s}f,\Lambda^{s-\frac{1}{2}}|\mathrm{D}|u_{0}\big)_{L^{2}}\Big|+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{5}|R_{i}|.

Then to conclude the proof, we use Cauchy-Schwarz, the adjoint estimate (
B.38
) to get that

\mathrm{LHS}_{\eqref{claim ineq G+ - G-}}\leq{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}M(t_{0}+3)|f|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}|u_{0}|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}.

∎

Before we proceed, we also note that under the provisions of Corollary
3.4
one can make an approximation of
(\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{-})^{-1}\partial_{x}
in terms of the Hilbert transform:

Corollary 3.6
.

Let
t_{0}\geq 1
and
\zeta\in H^{t_{0}+3}({\mathbb{R}})
be such that (
1.16
) is satisfied. Then for all
0\leq s\leq t_{0}
and
f\in\mathscr{S}({\mathbb{R}})
there holds,

(3.17)

|(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\partial_{x}f-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu}}\mathcal{H}f|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}\leq{\varepsilon}\mu^{-\frac{1}{4}}M(t_{0}+3)|f|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}.

Proof.

The proof is the same as Corollary
3.4
, where we note that
S^{+}
is traded for
\partial_{x}
in the formula
\mathrm{Op}(\frac{\partial_{x}}{S^{-}})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu}}\mathcal{H}
. In particular, now
\tilde{\psi}=\mathrm{Op}(\frac{\partial_{x}}{S^{-}})f
and the proof follows from:

(3.18)

\displaystyle\Big|\big(\Lambda^{s}(\partial_{x}f-\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\tilde{\psi}^{-}),\Lambda^{s}u_{0}\big)_{L^{2}}\Big|\leq{\varepsilon}M(t_{0}+3)|f|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}|u_{0}|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Then using the same notation as in the previous proof, we note that in this case we have

\displaystyle\big(\Lambda^{s}(\partial_{x}f-\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\tilde{\psi}^{-}),\Lambda^{s}u_{0}\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=\big(\big[\Lambda^{s},\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\big]\tilde{\psi}^{-},\Lambda^{s}u_{0}\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\big(\Lambda^{s-\frac{1}{2}}\tilde{\psi}^{-},\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]-\mathrm{Op}(S^{-})\big)\Lambda^{s}u_{0}\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=R_{3}+R_{4},

where
R_{1}=R_{2}=R_{5}=0
and

|R_{3}|+|R_{4}|\leq{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}M(t_{0}+3)|\tilde{\psi}^{-}|_{\mathring{H}^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}|u_{0}|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}\leq{\varepsilon}M(t_{0}+3)|f|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}|u_{0}|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}.

Adding the estimates concludes the proof.
∎

3.3.
Symbolic analysis of
(\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{-})^{-1}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}

Next we will study the symbolic behaviour of
(\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{-})^{-1}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}
. To do so, we recall that we may write
\mathcal{G}_{\mu}=\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}(\mathcal{J}_{\mu})^{-1}
where the symbolic behaviour of
\mathcal{J}_{\mu}
is captured by the symbol

(3.19)

S_{J}=1-\gamma\frac{S^{+}}{S^{-}}.

Corollary 3.7
.

Let
t_{0}\geq 1
and
\zeta\in H^{t_{0}+3}({\mathbb{R}})
be such that (
1.16
) is satisfied. Then for all
0\leq s\leq t_{0}
and
f\in\mathscr{S}({\mathbb{R}})
one can approximate the operator
(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]
by

\mathrm{Op}\Big(\frac{S^{+}}{S^{-}S_{J}}\Big)f(x)=-\mathcal{F}^{-1}\Big(\frac{\tanh(\sqrt{\mu}t(x,\xi))}{1+\gamma\tanh(\sqrt{\mu}t(x,\xi))}\hat{f}(\xi)\Big)(x),

where
t
is defined by (
3.2
). Moreover, there holds,

(3.20)

|(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]f-\mathrm{Op}\Big(\frac{S^{+}}{S^{-}S_{J}}\Big)f|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}\leq{\varepsilon}M(t_{0}+3)|f|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Proof.

We will give the proof in three steps. First, we will prove that
S_{J}
provides a good symbolic description of
\mathcal{J}_{\mu}
. Then we will show that
1/S_{J}
describes the inverse, and lastly, we combine each step with the previous results of this section to prove (
3.20
).

Step 1.
We can approximate
\mathcal{J}_{\mu}
with
\mathrm{Op}(S_{J})
. Indeed, by definition and estimate (
3.13
) we get that

(3.21)

\displaystyle|\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]f-\mathrm{Op}(S_{J})f|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle=\gamma|(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]f-\mathrm{Op}\big(\frac{S^{+}}{S^{-}}\big)f|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}M(t_{0}+3)|f|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Step 2.
We can approximate
(\mathcal{J}_{\mu})^{-1}
with
\mathrm{Op}(1/S_{J})
. To prove this fact, we simply employ Lemma
2.10
with inequality (
2.24
) to deduce that

\displaystyle|(\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}f-\mathrm{Op}(1/S_{J})f|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle=|(\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\big(1-\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\mathrm{Op}(1/S_{J})\big)f|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq M|\big(1-\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\mathrm{Op}(1/S_{J})\big)f|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq M\big(|\big(1-\mathrm{Op}(S_{J})\mathrm{Op}(1/S_{J})\big)f|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+|\big(\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]-\mathrm{Op}(S_{J})\big)\mathrm{Op}(1/S_{J})f|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle=:R_{1}+R_{2}.

Here
R_{1}
is estimated by (
B.40
):

\displaystyle R_{1}\leq\mu^{-\frac{k}{4}}|\big(1-\mathrm{Op}(S_{J})\mathrm{Op}(1/S_{J})\big)f|_{H^{s+1-\frac{k}{2}}}\leq{\varepsilon}\mu^{-\frac{k}{4}}M|f|_{H^{s-\frac{k}{2}}}.

While
R_{2}
we use the estimate in Step 1. and (
B.39
) to obtain

\displaystyle R_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mu^{-\frac{1}{4}}|\big(\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]-\mathrm{Op}(S_{J})\big)\mathrm{Op}(1/S_{J})f|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\ \leq{\varepsilon}M(t_{0}+3)|\mathrm{Op}(1/S_{J})f|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}M(t_{0}+3)|f|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

As a result, we have the following estimate

(3.22)

\displaystyle|(\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}f-\mathrm{Op}(1/S_{J})f|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}\leq{\varepsilon}\mu^{-\frac{k}{4}}M(t_{0}+3)|f|_{H^{s-\frac{k}{2}}}.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Step 3.
Estimate (
3.20
) holds true. To prove this, we first use the definition
\mathcal{G}_{\mu}=\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{+}(\mathcal{J}_{\mu})^{-1}
to make the decomposition:

\displaystyle\mathrm{LHS}_{\eqref{Est G-invG}}

\displaystyle:=|(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}f-\mathrm{Op}\Big(\frac{S^{+}}{S^{-}S_{J}}\Big)f|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq|(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\big((\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}-\mathrm{Op}\Big(\frac{1}{S_{J}}\Big)\big)f|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+|(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\mathrm{Op}\Big(\frac{1}{S_{J}}\Big)f-\mathrm{Op}\Big(\frac{S^{+}}{S^{-}S_{J}}\Big)f|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle=:R_{3}+R_{4}.

For
R_{3}
we use Proposition
2.4
with inequality (
2.11
) and then Step 2. to get that

\displaystyle R_{3}\leq\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}M|\big((\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}-\mathrm{Op}\Big(\frac{1}{S_{J}}\Big)\big)f|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}\leq{\varepsilon}\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}M(t_{0}+3)|f|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

For
R_{4}
, we decompose it further:

\displaystyle R_{4}

\displaystyle=|\Big((\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]-\mathrm{Op}\Big(\frac{S^{+}}{S^{-}}\Big)\Big)\mathrm{Op}\Big(\frac{1}{S_{J}}\Big)f|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+|\Big(\mathrm{Op}\Big(\frac{S^{+}}{S^{-}}\Big)\mathrm{Op}\Big(\frac{1}{S_{J}}\Big)-\mathrm{Op}\Big(\frac{S^{+}}{S^{-}S_{J}}\Big)\Big)f|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle=:R_{5}+R_{6}.

For
R_{5}
we use (
3.13
) and (
B.39
) to get that

\displaystyle R_{5}\leq{\varepsilon}\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}M(t_{0}+3)|\mathrm{Op}\Big(\frac{1}{S_{J}}\Big)f|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}\leq{\varepsilon}\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}M(t_{0}+3)|f|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

While for
R_{6}
we simply employ estimate (
B.48
) which yields

\displaystyle R_{6}

\displaystyle={\varepsilon}M|f|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}.

Gathering all these estimates concludes the proof.

∎

3.4.
Symbolic description of
\mathcal{G}_{\mu}

For the next result, we will give a simple approximation of
\mathcal{G}_{\mu}
. We will allow for a loss of derivatives in the estimate. However, the loss in regularity is translated into precision with respect to the small parameters.

Corollary 3.8
.

Let
t_{0}\geq 1
and
\zeta\in H^{t_{0}+2}({\mathbb{R}})
be such that (
1.16
) is satisfied. Then for all
0\leq s\leq t_{0}
and
\psi\in\dot{H}^{s+\frac{3}{2}}_{\mu}({\mathbb{R}})
one can approximate the operator
\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]
by the Fourier multiplier
\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[0]
defined by

\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[0]=\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|\frac{\tanh(\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|)}{1+\gamma\tanh(\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|)},

and it satisfies the estimate

(3.23)

|\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi-\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[0]\psi|_{H^{s}}\leq{\varepsilon}\mu M|\psi|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{3}{2}}}.

Proof.

For the proof of inequality (
3.23
), we let
\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[0]=\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[0](\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[0])^{-1}
and make the following decomposition

\displaystyle|\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi-\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{+}[0](\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[0])^{-1}\psi|_{H^{s}}

\displaystyle\leq|\big(\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]-\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{+}[0]\big)(\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\psi|_{H^{s}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+|\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{+}[0]\big((\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}-(\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[0])^{-1}\big)\psi|_{H^{s}}

\displaystyle=B_{1}+B_{2}.

For the estimate on
B_{1}
, we use the classical small amplitude expansion of
\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}
provided by Proposition
3.44
(with
n=0
,
k=1
,
s^{\prime}=s+\frac{1}{2}
) in
[
66
]
, and then (
2.24
):

\displaystyle B_{1}\leq{\varepsilon}\mu M|\psi|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{3}{2}}}.

For an estimate on
B_{2}
, we first observe that

\sqrt{\mu}|\xi|\tanh(\sqrt{\mu}|\xi|)\leq\mu\frac{|\xi|^{2}}{(1+\sqrt{\mu}|\xi|)^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

We may therefore use the product rule, combined with (
2.24
) and the shape derivative formulas (
A.60
) and (
A.61
) to find that

\displaystyle B_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mu|\partial_{x}\big((\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}(1-\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[0])^{-1})\psi\big)|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq\mu M\Big(|\mathrm{d}_{\zeta}(\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}(\partial_{x}\zeta)(1-\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[0])^{-1})\psi|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+|\mathrm{d}_{\zeta}\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](\partial_{x}\zeta)(\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[0])^{-1}\psi|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}+|\partial_{x}\psi|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}\mu M|\psi|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{3}{2}}}.

∎

3.5.
Symbolic analysis of
(\mathcal{J}_{\mu})^{-1}(\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{-})^{-1}\partial_{x}

We end this section with the study of an operator that appears in the quasilinearisation of the equations. In particular, we will need the symbolic description for the energy estimates later (see inequality (
5.11
)). The operator is well-defined by the first point in Corollary
A.17
, and can be approximated by a first order operator with a skew-symmetric principal symbol.

Corollary 3.9
.

Let
t_{0}\geq 1
and
\zeta\in H^{t_{0}+3}({\mathbb{R}})
be such that (
1.16
) is satisfied. Then for all
0\leq s\leq t_{0}
and
f\in H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
, one can approximate the operator
\mathfrak{P}^{2}(\mathcal{J}_{\mu})^{-1}(\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{-})^{-1}\partial_{x}
by

\mathrm{Op}\Big(\frac{\mathfrak{P}^{2}}{S_{J}S^{-}}\Big)\partial_{x}f(x)=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu}}\mathcal{F}^{-1}\Big(\frac{|\xi|(1+\sqrt{\mu}|\xi|)^{-1}}{1+\gamma\tanh(\sqrt{\mu}t(x,\xi))}i\xi\hat{f}(\xi)\Big)(x),

and for
k=0,1
, there holds,

(3.24)

|\big[\mathfrak{P}^{2}(\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}(\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{-}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}-\mathrm{Op}(\frac{\mathfrak{P}^{2}}{S_{J}S^{-}})\big]\partial_{x}f|_{H^{s+\frac{k}{2}}}\leq{\varepsilon}\mu^{-1-\frac{k}{4}}M(t_{0}+3)|(1+\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|)^{\frac{1}{2}}f|_{H^{s}}.

Proof.

We first decompose the main operator into three pieces:

(3.25)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{P}^{2}(\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}(\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{-}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\partial_{x}f

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Op}(\frac{\mathfrak{P}^{2}}{S_{J}S^{-}})\partial_{x}f+R_{1}+R_{2},

where we define
f^{\sharp}=(\mathcal{J}_{\mu})^{-1}(\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{-})^{-1}\partial_{x}f

R_{1}=\Big(1-\mathrm{Op}(\frac{\mathfrak{P}^{2}}{S_{J}S^{-}})\mathrm{Op}(\frac{S_{J}S^{-}}{\mathfrak{P}^{2}})\Big)\mathfrak{P}^{2}f^{\sharp},

and

R_{2}=\mathrm{Op}(\frac{\mathfrak{P}^{2}}{S_{J}S^{-}})\big(\mathrm{Op}(S^{-}S_{J})-\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\big)f^{\sharp}.

For the estimate on
R_{1}
we use (
B.50
) to get,

\displaystyle|R_{1}|_{H^{s+\frac{k}{2}}}\leq{\varepsilon}\mu^{-\frac{k}{4}}M|\mathfrak{P}f^{\sharp}|_{H^{s}}.

Then apply (
2.24
) and(
A.34
) to find that

(3.26)

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{P}f^{\sharp}|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=|(\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}(\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{-}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\partial_{x}f|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq\mu^{-\frac{1}{4}}M|(\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{-}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\partial_{x}f|_{\mathring{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq\mu^{-\frac{3}{4}}M|f|_{\mathring{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}},

which yields the following estimate on
R_{1}
:

\displaystyle|R_{1}|_{H^{s+\frac{k}{2}}}\leq{\varepsilon}\mu^{-1-\frac{k}{4}}M|(1+\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|)^{\frac{1}{2}}f|_{H^{s}}.

For the estimate on
R_{2}
we would like to employ the symbolic description of
\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{\pm}
. However, since estimate (
3.4
) arrives on
\mathring{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
, we need to carefully decompose this term so that we can estimate
f^{\sharp}\in\dot{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}({\mathbb{R}})
. First, we observe that

\displaystyle\big(\mathrm{Op}(S^{-}S_{J})-\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\big)f^{\sharp}

\displaystyle=\Big(\big(\mathrm{Op}(S^{-})-\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\big)\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\Big)f^{\sharp}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\big(\mathrm{Op}(S^{-}S_{J})-\mathrm{Op}(S^{-})\mathrm{Op}(S_{J})\big)f^{\sharp}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\Big(\mathrm{Op}(S^{-})\big(\mathrm{Op}(S_{J})-\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\big)\Big)f^{\sharp}

\displaystyle=r_{1}+r_{2}+r_{3}.

Let
R_{2}^{i}=\Lambda^{\frac{k}{2}}\mathrm{Op}(\frac{\mathfrak{P}^{2}}{S_{J}S^{-}})r_{i}
with
i=1,2,3
. Then for the contribution of
R_{2}^{1}
, we first use estimates (
B.39
) and (
B.6
) to find that

\displaystyle|R_{2}^{1}|_{H^{s}}

\displaystyle\leq\mu^{-\frac{3}{4}-\frac{k}{4}}M(t_{0}+3)|r_{1}|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}.

Then apply Proposition
3.2
, the definition of
f^{\sharp}
, and argue as in the proof of estimate (
3.26
) to deduce,

\displaystyle|R_{2}^{1}|_{H^{s}}

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}\mu^{\frac{1}{4}-\frac{k}{4}}M(t_{0}+3)|(\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{-}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\partial_{x}f|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}\mu^{-1}M(t_{0}+3)|(1+\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|)^{\frac{1}{2}}f|_{H^{s}}.

For
R_{2}^{2}
, we apply estimates (
B.39
), (
B.6
), (
B.49
), and (
3.26
):

\displaystyle|R_{2}^{2}|_{H^{s}}\leq{\varepsilon}\mu^{-1}M|(1+\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|)^{\frac{1}{2}}f|_{H^{s}}.

Lastly, for
R_{2}^{3}
, we will decompose it further:

\displaystyle R_{2}^{3}

\displaystyle=-\gamma\Lambda^{\frac{k}{2}}\mathrm{Op}\big(\frac{\mathfrak{P}^{2}}{S_{J}S^{-}}\big)\Big(\mathrm{Op}(S^{-})\mathrm{Op}(\frac{S^{+}}{S^{-}})-\mathrm{Op}(S^{+})\Big)f^{\sharp}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-\gamma\Lambda^{\frac{k}{2}}\mathrm{Op}\big(\frac{\mathfrak{P}^{2}}{S_{J}S^{-}}\big)\Big(\mathrm{Op}(S^{+})-\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\Big)f^{\sharp}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-\gamma\Lambda^{\frac{k}{2}}\mathrm{Op}\big(\frac{\mathfrak{P}^{2}}{S_{J}S^{-}}\big)\Big(\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{-}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]-\mathrm{Op}(S^{-})\Big)(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]f^{\sharp}

\displaystyle=R_{2}^{3,1}+R_{2}^{3,2}+R_{2}^{3,3}.

For
R_{2}^{3,1}
, we use (
B.39
), (
B.6
), (
B.49
), and (
3.26
) to find that

|R_{2}^{3,1}|_{H^{s}}\leq{\varepsilon}\mu^{-1}M|(1+\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|)^{\frac{1}{2}}f|_{H^{s}}.

For
R_{2}^{3,2}
, we use (
B.39
), (
B.6
), then estimate (
3.3
) to find that

|R_{2}^{3,2}|_{H^{s}}\leq{\varepsilon}\mu^{1-\frac{k}{4}}M(t_{0}+3)|(1+\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|)^{\frac{1}{2}}f|_{H^{s}}

For
R_{2}^{3,3}
, we use estimates (
B.39
), (
B.6
), (
3.4
), (
2.11
), (
3.26
) to find that

\displaystyle|R_{2}^{3,3}|_{H^{s}}

\displaystyle\leq\mu^{-1}M|\Big(\mathrm{Op}(S^{-})-\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{-}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\Big)(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]f^{\sharp}|_{H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}M(t_{0}+3)|(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]f^{\sharp}|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}M(t_{0}+3)|f^{\sharp}|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}\mu^{-1}M(t_{0}+3)|(1+\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|)^{\frac{1}{2}}f|_{H^{s}}.

Gathering all these estimates implies

\displaystyle|R_{2}|_{H^{s}}\leq{\varepsilon}\mu^{-1-\frac{k}{4}}M(t_{0}+3)|(1+\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|)^{\frac{1}{2}}f|_{H^{s}},

and adding the estimates for
R_{1}
and
R_{2}
completes the proof.

∎

4.
Quasilinearization of the internal water wave system

In this section, we will put the internal water waves system (
1.15
) in a quasilinear form. This is done by applying time and space derivatives to the system and taking care of the principal terms. In particular, one needs shape derivative formulas for the Dirichlet-Neumann operator (
2.1
), where we prove in Lemma
A.22
that

\mathrm{d}_{\zeta}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](h)\psi=-{\varepsilon}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\big(h(\underline{w}^{+}-\gamma\underline{w}^{-})\big)-{\varepsilon}\mu\mathcal{I}[\mathbf{U}]h,

where
\underline{w}^{\pm}
is defined below together with the operator
\mathcal{I}[\mathbf{U}]h
. In fact, the operator
\mathcal{I}[\mathbf{U}]h
is one of the main quantities that we need to understand, and is where we will use the symbolic descriptions from the previous section. Before stating the main result, we formally introduce the main operators involved, and that will be studied in detail later.

Definition 4.1
.

Let the functions
\psi^{\pm}
serve as Dirichlet data for the elliptic problems (
1.7
) and (
1.5
), then we define the horizontal components of the velocities at the surface by

\underline{w}^{\pm}=\frac{\mathcal{G}^{\pm}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi^{+}+{\varepsilon}\mu\partial_{x}\zeta\partial_{x}\psi^{\pm}}{1+{\varepsilon}^{2}\mu(\partial_{x}\zeta)^{2}}.

We define the vertical component of the velocity at the surface by

\underline{V}^{\pm}=\partial_{x}\psi^{\pm}-{\varepsilon}\underline{w}^{\pm}\partial_{x}\zeta,

and

[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]=\underline{V}^{+}-\underline{V}^{-}.

We refer to Corollary
A.18
for the precise definition.
Moreover, we define the quantity related to the Rayleigh-Taylor criterion by

(4.1)

\mathfrak{a}=\Big(1+{\varepsilon}\big((\partial_{t}+{\varepsilon}\underline{V}^{+}\partial_{x})\underline{w}^{+}-\gamma(\partial_{t}+{\varepsilon}\underline{V}^{-}\partial_{x})\underline{w}^{-}\big)\Big),

and a quantity related to the presence of surface tension:

\mathcal{K}[{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}\partial_{x}\zeta]\bullet=(1+{\varepsilon}^{2}\mu(\partial_{x}\zeta)^{2})^{-\frac{3}{2}}\bullet.

From these quantities, we let
\mathbf{U}=(\zeta,\psi)^{T}
and define the linear operator,
\mathcal{I}[\mathbf{U}]
, of order one by,

(4.2)

\mathcal{I}[\mathbf{U}]\bullet=\partial_{x}(\bullet\underline{V}^{+})+\gamma\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\partial_{x}\Big(\bullet[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]\Big),

and its adjoint reads,

(4.3)

\mathcal{I}[\mathbf{U}]^{\ast}\bullet=-\underline{V}^{+}\partial_{x}\bullet-\gamma[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]\partial_{x}\big((\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\bullet\big).

Moreover, we define the instability operator:

(4.4)

\mathfrak{Ins}[\mathbf{U}]\bullet=\mathfrak{a}\bullet-(1-\gamma)\gamma{\varepsilon}^{2}\mu[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]\mathfrak{E}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\big(\bullet[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]\big)-\mathrm{bo}^{-1}\partial_{x}\mathcal{K}[{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}\partial_{x}\zeta]\partial_{x}\bullet,

where

(4.5)

\mathfrak{E}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\bullet=\partial_{x}\circ(\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\circ\partial_{x}\bullet.

Then to put the internal water waves system in matrix form, it is convenient to introduce the notation

\mathcal{A}[U]=\begin{pmatrix}0&-\frac{1}{\mu}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\\
\mathfrak{Ins}[\mathbf{U}]&0\end{pmatrix},\qquad\mathcal{B}[\mathbf{U}]=\begin{pmatrix}{\varepsilon}\mathcal{I}[\mathbf{U}]&0\\
0&-{\varepsilon}\mathcal{I}[\mathbf{U}]^{\ast}\end{pmatrix},

where
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
corresponds to the principal part of the system. To account for surface tension, one also needs to track the dependence in the sub-principal part, which is defined for
\alpha=(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})\in{\mathbb{N}}^{2}
:

\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}[U]=\begin{pmatrix}0&-\frac{1}{\mu}\mathcal{G}_{\mu,(\alpha)}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\\
\mathrm{bo}^{-1}\mathcal{K}_{(\alpha)}[{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}\partial_{x}\zeta]&0\end{pmatrix},

where we let
(\partial_{1},\partial_{2})=(\partial_{x},\partial_{t})
,
F=(f_{1},f_{2})
to define

\mathcal{G}_{\mu,(\alpha)}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]F=\sum\limits_{j=1}^{2}\ \alpha_{j}\mathrm{d}_{\zeta}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](\partial_{j}\zeta)f_{j},

and we have:

\mathcal{K}_{(\alpha)}[\partial_{x}\zeta]F=-\partial_{x}\Big(\sum\limits_{j=1}^{2}\mathrm{d}_{\zeta}\mathcal{K}[\partial_{x}\partial_{j}\zeta]\partial_{x}f_{j}+\mathcal{K}[\partial_{x}f_{j}]\partial_{x}\partial_{j}\zeta\Big).

With these formulas, we can state the main result of the section.

Proposition 4.2
.

Let
T>0
,
t_{0}\geq 1
and
N\in{\mathbb{N}}
be such that
N\geq 5
. Furthermore, let
\mathbf{U}=(\zeta,\psi)^{T}\in\mathscr{E}_{\mathrm{bo},T}^{N}
be such that (
1.16
) is satisfied on
[0,T]
. Also, for any
\alpha=(\alpha^{1},\alpha^{2})\in{\mathbb{N}}^{2}
,
\check{\alpha}^{j}=\alpha-\mathbf{e}_{j}
, with
1\leq|\alpha|\leq N
we define
\partial_{x,t}^{\alpha}=\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{1}}\partial_{t}^{\alpha_{2}}
, and let
\underline{w}^{\pm}
be defined as in (
A.41
). Moreover, define
\underline{w}
by

\displaystyle\underline{w}

\displaystyle=\underline{w}^{+}-\gamma\underline{w}^{-},

and

\zeta_{(\alpha)}=\partial_{x,t}^{\alpha}\zeta,\quad\psi_{(\alpha)}=\partial_{x,t}^{\alpha}\psi-{\varepsilon}\underline{w}\partial_{x,t}^{\alpha}\zeta,\quad\psi_{\langle\check{\alpha}\rangle}=(\psi_{(\check{\alpha}^{1})},\psi_{(\check{\alpha}^{2})})^{T}.

Then for
\mathbf{U}_{(\alpha)}=(\zeta_{(\alpha)},\psi_{(\alpha)})^{T}
and
\mathbf{U}_{\langle\check{\alpha}\rangle}=(\zeta_{(\alpha)},\psi_{\langle\check{\alpha}\rangle})^{T}
, there holds

(4.6)

\displaystyle\text{if}\>\>1\leq|\alpha|<N:

\displaystyle\quad\hskip 116.65646pt\partial_{t}\mathbf{U}_{(\alpha)}+\mathcal{A}[\mathbf{U}]\mathbf{U}_{(\alpha)}={\varepsilon}(R_{\alpha},S_{\alpha})^{T},

(4.7)

\displaystyle\text{if}\qquad\>|\alpha|=N:

\displaystyle\quad\partial_{t}\mathbf{U}_{(\alpha)}+\mathcal{A}[\mathbf{U}]\mathbf{U}_{(\alpha)}+\mathcal{B}[\mathbf{U}]\mathbf{U}_{(\alpha)}+\mathcal{C}[\mathbf{U}]\mathbf{U}_{\langle\check{\alpha}\rangle}={\varepsilon}(R_{\alpha},S_{\alpha})^{T}

The rest functions satisfy the estimates

(4.8)

|R_{\alpha}|^{2}_{H^{1}_{\mathrm{bo}}}+|S_{\alpha}|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}\leq C\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U})\big(1+{\varepsilon}^{2}\sqrt{\mu}|[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}|\zeta|^{2}_{<N+\frac{1}{2}>}\big),

for some
C(M,\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}))>0
nondecreasing function of its argument and where
|\zeta|_{<N+\frac{1}{2}>}
is defined by

|\zeta|_{<N+\frac{1}{2}>}=\sum\limits_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{2},|\alpha|=N}|\partial_{x,t}^{\alpha}\zeta|_{\mathring{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

For the proof, we need to carefully track the dependencies in the small parameters. However, this part is very similar to the one in
[
65
]
and can therefore be considered a technical point. We give the details and point out the differences in the Appendix
\mathrm{A}
, Section
A.5
. Now, before we proceed with the energy estimates, we need to give a rigorous meaning to the operators given in Definition
4.1
. This will be done in separate subsections.

4.0.1.
Properties of
\mathcal{I}[\mathbf{U}]\bullet

First, we study the operator
\mathcal{I}[\mathbf{U}]\bullet
which appears later in the shape derivative formulas for
\mathcal{G}_{\mu}
.

Proposition 4.3
.

Let
t_{0}\geq 1
and
\mathbf{U}=(\zeta,\psi)^{T}
, with
\zeta\in H^{t_{0}+3}({\mathbb{R}})
satisfying (
1.16
) and
\psi\in\dot{H}^{t_{0}+2}({\mathbb{R}})
. Then we may define the operator

\mathcal{I}[\mathbf{U}]\bullet=\partial_{x}(\bullet\underline{V}^{+})+\gamma\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\partial_{x}\Big(\bullet[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]\Big),

and it satisfies the following properties:

1.

For all
0\leq s\leq t_{0}
and
f\in H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
there holds,

(4.9)

|\mathcal{I}[\mathbf{U}]f|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}\leq M|f|_{H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}|\partial_{x}\psi|_{H^{t_{0}+\frac{1}{2}}},

and

(4.10)

|\mathcal{I}[\mathbf{U}]f|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}\leq M|f|_{H^{t_{0}+\frac{1}{2}}}|\partial_{x}\psi|_{H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}.

2.

Let
\mathfrak{a}=1+\mathfrak{b}
, with
\mathfrak{b}\in H^{t_{0}+1}({\mathbb{R}})
, and for all
f\in L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})
there holds,

(4.11)

\big(\mathfrak{a}\mathcal{I}[\mathbf{U}]f,f\big)_{L^{2}}\leq M(t_{0}+3)(1+|\mathfrak{b}|_{H^{t_{0}+1}})|\partial_{x}\psi|_{H^{t_{0}+1}}|f|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

3.

Let
K\in H^{t_{0}+1}({\mathbb{R}})
, then for all
f\in H^{1}({\mathbb{R}})
there holds,

(4.12)

\big(\partial_{x}(K\partial_{x}\mathcal{I}[\mathbf{U}]f),f\big)_{L^{2}}\leq M(t_{0}+3)|K|_{H^{t_{0}+1}}|\partial_{x}\psi|_{H^{t_{0}+1}}|f|^{2}_{H^{1}}.

4.

For all
f\in\dot{H}_{\mu}^{\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
,
g\in H^{\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
, one has

(4.13)

\big(\mathcal{I}[\mathbf{U}]^{\ast}f,g\big)_{L^{2}}\leq M|\partial_{x}\psi|_{H^{t_{0}+\frac{1}{2}}}|f|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}(|g|_{L^{2}}+\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}|g|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}).

Proof.

We prove each point in separate steps.

Step 1.
To prove the first point, we observe that the first part of
\mathcal{I}[\mathbf{U}]f
is estimated by the product estimate (
B.14
) and (
A.45
):

\displaystyle|\partial_{x}(f\underline{V}^{+})|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq|f|_{H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}|\underline{V}^{+}|_{H^{\max\{s+\frac{1}{2},t_{0}\}}}

\displaystyle\leq M|f|_{H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}|\partial_{x}\psi|_{H^{t_{0}+\frac{1}{2}}}.

To estimate the remaining part we first estimate
\mathcal{G}_{\mu}
with (
2.2
) to find that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\big(\partial_{x}(f[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!])\big)|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}\leq\mu^{\frac{3}{4}}|(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\big(\partial_{x}(f[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!])\big)|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}.

Then use (
A.34
), the product estimate (
B.14
), and (
A.45
) to see that

\displaystyle\mu^{\frac{3}{4}}|(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\big(\partial_{x}(f[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!])\big)|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}|(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\big(\partial_{x}(f[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!])\big)|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq|f[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]|_{H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq M|f|_{H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}|\partial_{x}\psi|_{H^{t_{0}+\frac{1}{2}}}.

For the proof of (
4.10
), it is proved similarly where we only need to modify the part when we apply the product estimate.

Step 2.
In this estimate, we note that the instability operator is a first-order differential operator acting on
f\mapsto\mathcal{I}[\mathbf{U}]f
. But the principal symbol is skew-symmetric. Indeed, for the first part, we use integration by parts

\displaystyle\big(\mathfrak{a}\partial_{x}(f\underline{V}^{+}),f\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=\big(\mathfrak{a}(\partial_{x}\underline{V}^{+})f,f\big)_{L^{2}}-\frac{1}{2}\big(\big(\partial_{x}(\mathfrak{a}\underline{V}^{+})\big)f,f\big)_{L^{2}}.

Then Hölder’s inequality, the Sobolev embedding
H^{t_{0}}({\mathbb{R}})\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}})
, the product estimate (
B.14
), and (
A.44
) gives

\displaystyle|\big(\mathfrak{a}\partial_{x}(f\underline{V}^{+}),f\big)_{L^{2}}|

\displaystyle=(1+|\mathfrak{b}|_{H^{t_{0}+1}})|\underline{V}^{+}|_{H^{t_{0}+1}}|f|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq(1+|\mathfrak{b}|_{H^{t_{0}+1}})|\partial_{x}\psi|_{H^{t_{0}+1}}|f|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

For the second part, we use integration by parts together with the fact that
\mathcal{G}_{\mu}
and
\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{-}
are symmetric to make the following decomposition

\displaystyle\big(\mathfrak{a}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\big(\partial_{x}(f[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!])\big),f\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=-\big(f[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!],\partial_{x}\Big((\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](\mathfrak{a}f)\Big)\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=-\big(f[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!],\partial_{x}\Big((\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]-\mathrm{Op}\Big(\frac{S^{+}}{S^{-}S_{J}}\Big)\Big)(\mathfrak{a}f)\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\big(\partial_{x}(f[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]),\mathrm{Op}\Big(\frac{S^{+}}{S^{-}S_{J}}\Big)(\mathfrak{a}f)\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=:R_{1}+R_{2}.

For
R_{1}
we use Cauchy-Schwarz and estimate (
3.20
) to deduce that

\displaystyle|R_{1}|

\displaystyle\leq|f[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]|_{L^{2}}|((\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]-\mathrm{Op}\Big(\frac{S^{+}}{S^{-}S_{J}}\Big))(\mathfrak{a}f)|_{\mathring{H}^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}M(t_{0}+3)|f[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]|_{L^{2}}|\mathfrak{a}f|_{L^{2}}.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Then use the product estimate (
B.14
) and (
A.45
) to get that

|R_{1}|\leq{\varepsilon}M(t_{0}+3)(1+|\mathfrak{b}|_{H^{t_{0}}})|\partial_{x}\psi|_{H^{t_{0}}}|f|^{2}_{L^{2}}.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

For
R_{2}
, we will make two different decomposition where we first split it into several parts:

\displaystyle R_{2}

\displaystyle=\big(\Big(\mathrm{Op}\Big(\frac{S^{+}}{S^{-}S_{J}}\Big)^{\ast}-\mathrm{Op}\Big(\frac{S^{+}}{S^{-}S_{J}}\Big)\Big)\partial_{x}(f[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]),(\mathfrak{a}f)\big)_{L^{2}}+\big(\mathrm{Op}\Big(\frac{S^{+}}{S^{-}S_{J}}\Big)(f\partial_{x}[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]),(\mathfrak{a}f)\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\big([\mathrm{Op}\Big(\frac{S^{+}}{S^{-}S_{J}}\Big),[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]]\partial_{x}f,(\mathfrak{a}f)\big)_{L^{2}}+\big([\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]\mathrm{Op}\Big(\frac{S^{+}}{S^{-}S_{J}}\Big)\partial_{x}f,(\mathfrak{a}f)\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=R_{2}^{1}+R_{2}^{2}+R_{2}^{3}+R_{2}^{4}.

For
R_{2}^{1}
we use estimate (
B.47
), for
R_{2}^{2}
we use (
B.44
), for
R_{2}^{3}
we use (
B.46
), combined with (
B.14
) and (
A.45
) we get that

|R_{2}^{1}|+|R_{2}^{2}|+|R_{2}^{3}|\leq M(1+|\mathfrak{b}|_{H^{t_{0}}})|\partial_{x}\psi|_{H^{t_{0}+1}}|f|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

On the other hand, to cancel
R_{2}^{4}
we can use integration by parts on
R_{2}
. Indeed, we obtain that

\displaystyle R_{2}

\displaystyle=-\big((f[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]),\mathrm{Op}\Big(\partial_{x}\frac{S^{+}}{S^{-}S_{J}}\Big)(\mathfrak{a}f)\big)_{L^{2}}-\big((f[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]),\mathrm{Op}\Big(\frac{S^{+}}{S^{-}S_{J}}\Big)(f\partial_{x}\mathfrak{b})\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-\big((f[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]),[\mathrm{Op}\Big(\frac{S^{+}}{S^{-}S_{J}}\Big),\mathfrak{b}]\partial_{x}f\big)_{L^{2}}-\big((f[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]),\mathfrak{a}\mathrm{Op}\Big(\frac{S^{+}}{S^{-}S_{J}}\Big)\partial_{x}f\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=R_{2}^{5}+R_{2}^{6}+R_{2}^{7}+R_{2}^{8}.

Here we estimate
R_{2}^{5},R_{2}^{6},R_{2}^{7}
as above where we also use estimate (
B.45
) for
R_{2}^{5}
. Adding these two decompositions implies

2|R_{2}|\leq\big|\sum\limits_{j=1}^{8}R_{2}^{j}\big|\leq M(1+|\mathfrak{b}|_{H^{t_{0}+1}})|\partial_{x}\psi|_{H^{t_{0}+1}}|f|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

Step 3.
Since
K
is symmetric and
f\mapsto\mathcal{I}[\mathbf{U}]f
is skew-symmetric, we can absorb one derivative by integrating by parts as we did in the previous step.

Step 4.
From the definition (
4.3
) we have that

\displaystyle\big(\mathcal{I}[\mathbf{U}]^{\ast}f,g\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=-\big(\underline{V}^{+}\partial_{x}f,g\big)_{L^{2}}-\gamma\big([\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]\partial_{x}\big((\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]f\big),g\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=A_{1}+\gamma A_{2}.

For the first term, we introduce a commutator, then apply Hölder’s inequality, Sobolev embedding, estimate (
A.45
), and (
B.19
) to find that

\displaystyle|A_{1}|

\displaystyle\leq|[\underline{V}^{+},(1+\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|)^{\frac{1}{2}}]\frac{\partial_{x}}{(1+\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|)^{\frac{1}{2}}}f|_{L^{2}}|g|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+|\underline{V}^{+}|_{L^{\infty}}|\frac{\partial_{x}}{(1+\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|)^{\frac{1}{2}}}f||(1+\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|)^{\frac{1}{2}}g|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq M|\partial_{x}\psi|_{H^{t_{0}+\frac{1}{2}}}|f|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}(|g|_{L^{2}}+\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}|g|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}).

For the second term, we instead use commutator estimate (
B.18
) and (
2.11
) to obtain

\displaystyle|A_{2}|

\displaystyle\leq|\big[[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!],|\mathrm{D}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\big]\mathcal{H}|\mathrm{D}|^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]f|_{L^{2}}|g|_{L^{2}}+|[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]|_{L^{\infty}}|(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]f|_{\mathring{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}|g|_{\mathring{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq M\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}|\partial_{x}\psi|_{H^{t_{0}+\frac{1}{2}}}|f|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}(|g|_{L^{2}}+|g|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}).

∎

4.1.
Properties of
\mathfrak{Ins}[\mathbf{U}]\bullet
.

The instability operator is defined in terms of
\mathfrak{E}_{\mu}
which is given by

\mathfrak{E}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\bullet=\partial_{x}\circ(\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\circ\partial_{x}\bullet.

Meaning we first need to study its properties, which is the topic of the next Proposition.

Proposition 4.4
.

Let
t_{0}\geq 1
and
\zeta\in H^{t_{0}+2}({\mathbb{R}})
be such that (
1.16
) is satisfied. Then we have the following results:

1.

There exist a constant
c\leq M
such that for all
f\in H^{\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
there holds,

(4.14)

0\leq\big(\mathfrak{E}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]f,f\big)_{L^{2}}\leq\frac{c}{\mu}|(1+\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|)^{\frac{1}{2}}f|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

2.

If we suppose further that
\zeta
is time dependent and satisfies (
1.16
) uniformly in time, then for all
f\in H^{\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
there holds,

(4.15)

|\big(\big[\partial_{t},\mathfrak{E}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\big]f,f\big)_{L^{2}}|\leq\frac{{\varepsilon}}{\mu}M|\partial_{t}\zeta|_{H^{t_{0}+1}}|(1+\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|)^{\frac{1}{2}}f|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

3.

If we suppose further that
\zeta\in H^{t_{0}+3}({\mathbb{R}})
. Then for all
f\in H^{\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
and
g\in H^{-\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
there holds,

(4.16)

|\big(f,\big(\mathfrak{E}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]^{\ast}-\mathfrak{E}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\big)g\big)_{L^{2}}|\leq{\varepsilon}\mu^{-\frac{3}{4}}M(t_{0}+3)|f|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}|g|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}}.

Proof.

We give the proof in three separate points.

Step 1.
For the proof of the first point, we deduce the positivity by integrating parts:

\displaystyle\big(\mathfrak{E}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]f,f\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=-\big((\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\partial_{x}f,\partial_{x}f\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=-\big(g,(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]g\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq 0,

where we defined
g
by

g=(\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\partial_{x}f,

and used that
\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}\mathcal{J}_{\mu}=\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}-\gamma\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}
is negative.

For the upper bound, we use Plancherel’s identity, and Cauchy-Schwarz inequality with the estimates (
2.24
) and (
A.34
) to find that

\displaystyle|\big(\mathfrak{E}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]f,f\big)_{L^{2}}|

\displaystyle\leq|(\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\partial_{x}f|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}|(1+\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|)^{\frac{1}{2}}f|_{L^{2}}
