\displaystyle\leq\mu^{-\frac{1}{4}}M|(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\partial_{x}f|_{\mathring{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}|(1+\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|)^{\frac{1}{2}}f|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\mu^{-\frac{3}{4}}M|f|_{\mathring{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}|(1+\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|)^{\frac{1}{2}}f|_{L^{2}}.

Step 2.
By direct computations, we need to control the following two terms:

\displaystyle\big[\partial_{t},\mathfrak{E}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\big]f

\displaystyle=\partial_{x}\circ(\mathrm{d}_{\zeta}(\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}(\partial_{t}\zeta))\circ(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\circ\partial_{x}f

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\partial_{x}\circ(\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\circ\mathrm{d}_{\zeta}(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}(\partial_{t}\zeta)\circ\partial_{x}f

\displaystyle=f_{1}+f_{2}.

For the contribution of the first term, we use Plancherel’s identity, Cauchy-Schwarz, and estimates (
A.61
) and (
A.34
):

\displaystyle|\big(f_{1},f\big)_{L^{2}}|

\displaystyle\leq|\mathrm{d}_{\zeta}(\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}(\partial_{t}\zeta)(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\circ\partial_{x}f|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}|(1+\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|)^{\frac{1}{2}}f|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}\mu^{-\frac{1}{4}}M|\partial_{t}\zeta|_{H^{t_{0}+1}}|(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\partial_{x}f|_{\mathring{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}|(1+\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|)^{\frac{1}{2}}f|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}\mu^{-1}M|\partial_{t}\zeta|_{H^{t_{0}+1}}|(1+\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|)^{\frac{1}{2}}f|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

For the estimate on
f_{2}
we use we use Plancherel’s identity, Cauchy-Schwarz, (
2.24
), (
A.37
), and then (
A.34
) to obtain

\displaystyle|\big(f_{2},f\big)_{L^{2}}|

\displaystyle\leq\mu^{-\frac{1}{4}}|(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\circ\mathrm{d}_{\zeta}\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](\partial_{t}\zeta)\circ(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\circ\partial_{x}f|_{\mathring{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}|(1+\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|)^{\frac{1}{2}}f|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}\mu^{-1}M|\partial_{t}\zeta|_{H^{t_{0}+1}}|(1+\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|)^{\frac{1}{2}}f|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Step 3.
We will split the left hand side of (
4.16
) into two parts and trade the differential operators with corresponding PDOs. In particular, for the first term we observe that

\displaystyle\big(f,\mathfrak{E}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]^{\ast}g\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=\big(\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{x}(\mathcal{J}_{\mu})^{-1}\Big((\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{-})^{-1}\partial_{x}-\mathrm{Op}(\frac{\partial_{x}}{S^{-}})\Big)f,\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}g\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\big(\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{x}\Big((\mathcal{J}_{\mu})^{-1}-\mathrm{Op}(\frac{1}{S_{J}})\Big)\mathrm{Op}(\frac{\partial_{x}}{S^{-}})f,\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}g\big)_{L^{2}}+\big(\partial_{x}\mathrm{Op}(\frac{1}{S_{J}})\mathrm{Op}(\frac{\partial_{x}}{S^{-}})f,g\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=A_{1}+A_{2}+A_{3}.

Here
A_{1}
is estimated by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, (
2.24
), (
3.17
), to find that

\displaystyle|A_{1}|\leq|(\mathcal{J}_{\mu})^{-1}\Big((\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{-})^{-1}\partial_{x}-\mathrm{Op}(\frac{\partial_{x}}{S^{-}})\Big)f|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{3}{2}}_{\mu}}|g|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}}\leq{\varepsilon}\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}M(t_{0}+3)|f|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}|g|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}},

while
A_{2}
is estimated similarly using (
3.22
) with
s=k=1
to find that

|A_{2}|\leq{\varepsilon}\mu^{-\frac{3}{4}}M(t_{0}+3)|f|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}|g|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}}.

Lastly, the contribution will cancel up to a smoothing estimate with the remaining terms

\displaystyle-\big(f,\mathfrak{E}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]g\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=\big(\mathfrak{P}^{-1}\partial_{x}f,\mathfrak{P}(\mathcal{J}_{\mu})^{-1}\Big((\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{-})^{-1}\partial_{x}-\mathrm{Op}(\frac{\partial_{x}}{S^{-}})\Big)g\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\big(\mathfrak{P}^{-1}\partial_{x}f,\mathfrak{P}\Big((\mathcal{J}_{\mu})^{-1}-\mathrm{Op}(\frac{1}{S_{J}})\Big)\mathrm{Op}(\frac{\partial_{x}}{S^{-}})g\big)_{L^{2}}+\big(\partial_{x}f,\mathrm{Op}(\frac{1}{S_{J}})\mathrm{Op}(\frac{\partial_{x}}{S^{-}})g\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=B_{1}+B_{2}+B_{3},

where
|B_{1}|+|B_{2}|
is estimated as
|A_{1}|+|A_{2}|
to find that

|B_{1}|+|B_{2}|\leq{\varepsilon}\mu^{-\frac{3}{4}}M(t_{0}+3)|f|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}|g|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}}.

On the other hand,
B_{3}
can be compensated with
A_{3}
by noting that

\displaystyle B_{3}

\displaystyle=-\big(\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{Op}(\frac{\partial_{x}}{S^{-}})\mathrm{Op}(\frac{1}{S_{J}})^{\ast}\partial_{x}f,\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}g\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=-\big(\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{Op}(\frac{\partial_{x}}{S^{-}})\Big(\mathrm{Op}(\frac{1}{S_{J}})^{\ast}-\mathrm{Op}(\frac{1}{S_{J}})\Big)\partial_{x}f,\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}g\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-\big(\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\big[\mathrm{Op}(\frac{\partial_{x}}{S^{-}}),\mathrm{Op}(\frac{1}{S_{J}})\big]\partial_{x}f,\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}g\big)_{L^{2}}-\big(\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\big[\mathrm{Op}(\frac{1}{S_{J}}),\partial_{x}\big]\mathrm{Op}(\frac{\partial_{x}}{S^{-}})f,\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}g\big)_{L^{2}}-A_{3}

\displaystyle=B_{3}^{1}+B_{3}^{2}+B_{3}^{3}-A_{3},

where
|B_{3}^{1}|+|B_{3}^{2}|+|B_{3}^{3}|
is estimated using PDO estimates (
B.41
), (
B.43
), and (
B.42
) respectively, which yields the following bound

|B_{3}^{1}|+|B_{3}^{2}|+|B_{3}^{3}|\leq{\varepsilon}\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}M|f|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}|g|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}}.

Adding all these estimates implies the final result.

\displaystyle|\big(f,\big(\mathfrak{E}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]^{\ast}-\mathfrak{E}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\big)g\big)_{L^{2}}|\leq{\varepsilon}\mu^{-\frac{3}{4}}M(t_{0}+3)|f|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}|g|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}}.

∎

Remark 4.5
.

In the first point, we can define the smallest constant
c\leq M
such that (
4.14
) holds by

(4.17)

\mathfrak{e}(\zeta)=\sup\limits_{f\in H^{\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}}),f\neq 0}\mu\frac{\big((\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\partial_{x}f,\partial_{x}f\big)_{L^{2}}}{|1+\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|^{\frac{1}{2}}f|_{L^{2}}^{2}}.

Also, it is evident from the proof that for
f,g\in H^{\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
that there holds

(4.18)

\big(\mathfrak{E}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]f,g\big)_{L^{2}}\leq\frac{c}{\mu}|(1+\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|)^{\frac{1}{2}}f|_{L^{2}}|(1+\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|)^{\frac{1}{2}}g|_{L^{2}}.

For the next result, we will treat the properties of the instability operator. In particular, we will show that under the stability criterion, we can have a coercivity-type estimate. This is essential for the well-posedness theory to work and relies on the surface tension parameter through the Bond number
\mathrm{bo}^{-1}>0
. To be clear, we restate the stability criterion in the introduction:

0<\mathfrak{d}(\mathbf{U}):=\inf\limits_{{\mathbb{R}}}\mathfrak{a}-\Upsilon\mathfrak{c}(\zeta)|[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]|_{L^{\infty}}^{4},

where

\Upsilon=\frac{\mathrm{bo}}{2}(1-\gamma)^{2}\gamma^{2}{\varepsilon}^{4}\mu

and
\mathfrak{a}
is given by (
4.20
),
\mathfrak{e}
is given in (
4.17
), and we define

\displaystyle\mathfrak{c}(\zeta)=\mathfrak{e}(\zeta)^{2}(1+{\varepsilon}^{2}\mu|\partial_{x}\zeta|_{L^{\infty}}^{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}.

Proposition 4.6
.

Let
{\varepsilon},\mu,\mathrm{bo}^{-1}\in(0,1)
such that
\varepsilon^{2}\mu\leq\mathrm{bo}^{-1}
,
T>0
,
t_{0}\geq 1
,
\mathcal{E}^{\lceil t_{0}+2\rceil}(\mathbf{U})
be defined by (
1.18
), and
\mathbf{U}=(\zeta,\psi)\in\mathscr{E}^{\lceil t_{0}+2\rceil}_{\mathrm{bo},T}
be such that the non-cavitation condition (
1.16
) holds and satisfies the stability criterion (
1.19
) on
[0,T]
. Then we have the following set of inequalities on the same time interval:

1.

For all
u\in H^{1}_{\gamma,\mathrm{bo}}({\mathbb{R}})
and
\mathfrak{Ins}(\mathbf{U})
defined by (
4.4
) one has

(4.19)

\big(u,\mathfrak{Ins}(\mathbf{U})u\big)_{L^{2}}\leq C\big(\mathcal{E}^{\lceil t_{0}+2\rceil}(\mathbf{U})\big)|u|_{H^{1}_{\mathrm{bo}}}^{2},

where
C>0
is a nondecreasing function of its argument.

2.

For
a(\mathbf{U})
defined by

(4.20)

a(\mathbf{U})=(1-\gamma)\gamma{\varepsilon}^{2}\mathfrak{e}(\zeta)|[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]|_{L^{\infty}}^{2},

and there is some
C_{1}>0
and
b(\mathbf{U})
defined by

(4.21)

b(\mathbf{U})=(1-\gamma)\gamma{\varepsilon}^{2}\sqrt{\mu}C_{1}\mathfrak{e}(\zeta)|[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]|_{H^{t_{0}+1}}^{2},

such that for
\mathfrak{d}
defined by (
1.19
) there holds,

(4.22)

\min\big\{\mathfrak{d}(\mathbf{U}),\frac{1}{M}\big\}|u|_{H^{1}_{\mathrm{bo}}}^{2}\leq\big(u,\mathfrak{Ins}(\mathbf{U})u\big)_{L^{2}}+a(\mathbf{U})|u|_{L^{2}}^{2}+b(\mathbf{U})|u|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

3.

Lastly, there is a control on
u\in\mathring{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
through the inequality

(4.23)

(1-\gamma)\gamma{\varepsilon}^{2}\sqrt{\mu}\mathfrak{e}(\zeta)\max\limits_{|\alpha|\leq 1}|\partial^{\alpha}_{x,t}[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}|u|^{2}\leq\varepsilon C\big(\mathcal{E}^{\lceil t_{0}+2\rceil}(\mathbf{U})\big)\Big(|u|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\mathrm{bo}^{-1}|\partial_{x}u|_{L^{2}}^{2}\Big).

where
C>0
is a nondecreasing function of its argument.

Proof.

The proof is similar to the one of Lemma
11
in
[
65
]
. However, we give a short proof for the convenience of the reader to track the constants that are responsible for the definitions above. We divide the proof into three steps

Step 1.
For the proof of (
4.19
), we have to deal with the terms:

\displaystyle\big(u,\mathfrak{Ins}(\mathbf{U})u\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=\big(u,\mathfrak{a}u\big)_{L^{2}}-(1-\gamma)\gamma{\varepsilon}^{2}\mu\big(u,[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]\mathfrak{E}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\big(u[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]\big)\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-\mathrm{bo}^{-1}\big(u,\partial_{x}\mathcal{K}[{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}\partial_{x}\zeta]\partial_{x}u\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}.

For
I_{1}
, we have that

(4.24)

\big(\inf\limits_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}}\mathfrak{a}\big)|u|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq I_{1}\leq C\big(\mathcal{E}^{\lceil t_{0}+2\rceil}(\mathbf{U})\big)|u|_{L^{2}}^{2},

where the upper bound is a consequence of the Hölder inequality and estimate (
A.47
). For the estimate on
I_{2}
, we employ (
4.14
) to find that

\displaystyle-(1-\gamma)\gamma{\varepsilon}^{2}\mathfrak{e}(\zeta)|(1+\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|)^{\frac{1}{2}}(u[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!])|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq I_{2}\leq 0.

For the estimate on
I_{3}
, we have by integration by parts that

(4.25)

\mathrm{bo}^{-1}(1+{\varepsilon}^{2}\mu|\partial_{x}\zeta|^{2}_{L^{\infty}})^{-\frac{3}{2}}|\partial_{x}u|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq I_{3}\leq\mathrm{bo}^{-1}|\partial_{x}u|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

So the upper bound in (
4.19
) is proved.

Step 2.
For the proof of (
4.22
), we need to work on the lower bound in
I_{2}
. First, we can replace the lower bound using the commutator estimate (
B.16
) and Hölder’s inequality to find that

\displaystyle|\langle\sqrt{\mu}\mathrm{D}\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}(u[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!])|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\sqrt{\mu}|[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]|_{H^{t_{0}+1}}^{2}|u|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}+\sqrt{\mu}|[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}||\mathrm{D}|^{\frac{1}{2}}u|_{L^{2}}^{2}

(4.26)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+|[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}|u|_{L^{2}}^{2},

for some constant
C_{1}>0
. Then we may define
b(\mathbf{U})
by (
4.21
) and use the expression for
a(\mathbf{U})
to find that

\displaystyle I_{2}\geq-b(\mathbf{U})|u|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}-a(\mathbf{U})\big(|u|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\sqrt{\mu}|u|^{2}_{\mathring{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\big).

Adding all these estimates, one finds that

\displaystyle\mathrm{RHS}_{\eqref{lower bound ins}}:

\displaystyle=a(\mathbf{U})|u|_{L^{2}}^{2}+b(\mathbf{U})|u|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}}+\big(u,\mathfrak{Ins}(\mathbf{U})u\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=a(\mathbf{U})|u|_{L^{2}}^{2}+b(\mathbf{U})|u|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}+I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}

\displaystyle\geq\big(\inf\limits_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}}\mathfrak{a}\big)|u|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\mathrm{bo}^{-1}(1+{\varepsilon}^{2}\mu|\partial_{x}\zeta|^{2}_{L^{\infty}})^{-\frac{3}{2}}|\partial_{x}u|_{L^{2}}^{2}-a(\mathbf{U})\sqrt{\mu}|u|^{2}_{\mathring{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}},

where the last term is controlled using interpolation and Young’s inequality:

\displaystyle\sqrt{\mu}a(\mathbf{U})|u|^{2}_{\mathring{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\mu}a(\mathbf{U})|u|_{L^{2}}|\partial_{x}u|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathrm{bo}\mu}{2}(1+{\varepsilon}^{2}\mu|\partial_{x}\zeta|^{2}_{L^{\infty}})^{\frac{3}{2}}a(\mathbf{U})^{2}|u|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2\mathrm{bo}}(1+{\varepsilon}^{2}\mu|\partial_{x}\zeta|^{2}_{L^{\infty}})^{-\frac{3}{2}}|\partial_{x}u|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Consequently, we find that

\displaystyle\mathrm{RHS}_{\eqref{lower bound ins}}

\displaystyle\geq\Big(\inf\limits_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}}\mathfrak{a}-\frac{\mathrm{bo}\mu}{2}(1+{\varepsilon}^{2}\mu|\partial_{x}\zeta|^{2}_{L^{\infty}})^{\frac{3}{2}}a(\mathbf{U})^{2}\Big)|u|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2\mathrm{bo}}(1+{\varepsilon}^{2}\mu|\partial_{x}\zeta|^{2}_{L^{\infty}})^{-\frac{3}{2}}|\partial_{x}u|_{L^{2}}^{2},

\displaystyle\geq\Big(\inf\limits_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}}\mathfrak{a}-\Upsilon\mathfrak{c}(\zeta)|[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]|_{L^{\infty}}^{4}\Big)|u|_{L^{2}}^{2}+(M\mathrm{bo})^{-1}|\partial_{x}u|_{L^{2}}^{2},

where we used Sobolev embedding and defined the quantities

\Upsilon\cdot\mathfrak{c}(\zeta):=\frac{\mathrm{bo}}{2}(1-\gamma)^{2}\gamma^{2}{\varepsilon}^{4}\mu\cdot\mathfrak{e}^{2}(\zeta)(1+{\varepsilon}^{2}\mu|\partial_{x}\zeta|^{2}_{L^{\infty}})^{\frac{3}{2}},

in accordance with Definition
1.4
.

Step 3.
To prove the third point, we use interpolation, Sobolev embedding, (
A.45
), (
A.50
), and that
\varepsilon^{2}\mu\leq\mathrm{bo}^{-1}
to obtain

\displaystyle\mathrm{LHS}_{\eqref{zeta half norm}}

\displaystyle=(1-\gamma)\gamma{\varepsilon}^{2}\sqrt{\mu}\mathfrak{e}(\zeta)\max\limits_{|\alpha|\leq 1}|\partial^{\alpha}_{x,t}[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}|u|^{2}_{\mathring{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\Big(\varepsilon^{-2}\Upsilon\mathfrak{c}(\zeta)\max\limits_{|\alpha|\leq 1}|\partial^{\alpha}_{x,t}[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]|_{L^{\infty}}^{4}|u|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\mathrm{bo}^{-1}(1+\varepsilon^{2}\mu|\partial_{x}\zeta|_{L^{\infty}})^{-\frac{3}{2}}|\partial_{x}u|_{L^{2}}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\Big(C\big(\mathcal{E}^{\lceil t_{0}+2\rceil}(\mathbf{U})\big)|u|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\mathrm{bo}^{-1}|\partial_{x}u|_{L^{2}}^{2}\Big).

∎

Remark 4.7
.

Here we made the restriction
\varepsilon^{2}\mu\leq\mathrm{bo}^{-1}
to prove the third point. Alternatively, we could have made stronger version of the stability criterion (
1.19
):

(4.27)

\inf\limits_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}}\mathfrak{a}-\varepsilon^{-2}\Upsilon\mathfrak{c}(\zeta)\max\limits_{|\alpha|\leq 1}|\partial^{\alpha}_{x,t}[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]|_{L^{\infty}}^{4}>0.

In this case, we could have used it to prove (
4.23
) without any restriction on the small parameters. However, in order to use this criterion later in the proof of Theorem
1.7
we would need to make the restriction
\varepsilon\mu\leq\mathrm{bo}^{-1}
(see Remark
6.1
). Also, due to a technical point in the energy estimate we need the restrction
{\varepsilon}^{2}\leq\mathrm{bo}^{-1}
(see Remark
5.3
).

5.
A priori estimates

We are now in the position to derive energy estimates for the internal water waves system (
1.15
). To define a natural energy to the system, we distinguish between the cases
\alpha=0
and
1\leq|\alpha|\leq N.
For
\alpha=0
, we have enough regularity on the data to control the solutions with the energy:

(5.1)

E^{0}(\mathbf{U})=|\Lambda^{t_{0}+\frac{5}{2}}\zeta|_{H^{1}_{\mathrm{bo}}}^{2}+\frac{1}{\mu}\big(\Lambda^{t_{0}+\frac{5}{2}}\psi,\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[0]\Lambda^{t_{0}+\frac{5}{2}}\psi\big)_{L^{2}},

where
\mathcal{G}[0]\psi
is defined by formula (
2.9
):

\mathcal{G}[0]\psi=\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|\frac{\mathrm{tanh}(\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|)}{1+\gamma\mathrm{tanh}(\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|)}\psi.

For
1\leq|\alpha|\leq N
, we use Proposition
4.2
and exploit its quaslinear structure to define a suitable symmetrizer. In particular, we will need to cancel specific terms in the energy estimates, and it will be done by introducing the symmetrizer:

(5.2)

Q(\mathbf{U})=Q^{(1)}(\mathbf{U})+Q^{(2)}(\mathbf{U})=\begin{pmatrix}\mathfrak{Ins}[\mathbf{U}]&0\\
0&\frac{1}{\mu}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}a(\mathbf{U})+b(\mathbf{U})\Lambda^{-1}&0\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}.

where
a(U)
and
b(U)
are defined by (
4.20
) and (
4.21
). With this symmetrizer at hand, we define energies by

(5.3)

E_{\alpha}(\mathbf{U})=\big(\mathbf{U}_{(\alpha)},Q(\mathbf{U})\mathbf{U}_{(\alpha)}\big)_{L^{2}}.

Finally, taking the sum over all
\alpha
will be the main quantity used to control the principal part of (
1.15
):

(5.4)

E^{k}(\mathbf{U})=E^{0}(\mathbf{U})+\sum\limits_{1\leq|\alpha|\leq k}E_{\alpha}(\mathbf{U}).

Before proceeding, we make three comments on the choice of the energy.

Remark 5.1
.

space

1.

The term
Q^{(1)}(\mathbf{U})
in (
5.2
), is chosen specifically to cancel the principal part:

\big(\mathcal{A}[\mathbf{U}]\mathbf{U}_{(\alpha)},Q^{(1)}(\mathbf{U})\mathbf{U}_{(\alpha)}\big)_{L^{2}}=0,

which appears naturally in the energy estimates. See estimate of
B_{2}^{1}
in the proof of Proposition
5.2
below.

2.

The role of the term
Q^{(2)}(\mathbf{U})
is to make the energy equivalent to the energy norm. In particular, for (
5.4
) to be coercive, we need
Q^{(2)}(\mathbf{U})
and the stability criterion (
1.19
) to have a lower bound on
\mathfrak{Ins}[\mathbf{U}]
in the energy space.

3.

To close the energy estimates, we actually need to modify (
5.4
). This is because
\mathfrak{Ins}[\mathbf{U}]
is a second-order operator and therefore makes a contribution to the sub-principal part of the equation (when
|\alpha|=N
). In particular, we correct the energy to cancel the terms

\big(\mathcal{C}[\mathbf{U}]\mathbf{U}_{\langle\check{\alpha}\rangle},Q^{(1)}(\mathbf{U})\mathbf{U}_{(\alpha)}\big)_{L^{2}},

which will appear in the a priori estimates. To do so, we define the quantity

\tilde{Q}(\mathbf{U})=\begin{pmatrix}\mathrm{bo}^{-1}\mathcal{K}_{(\alpha)}[{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}\partial_{x}\zeta]&0\\
0&\frac{1}{\mu}\mathcal{G}_{\mu,(\alpha)}\end{pmatrix},

where we define the modified energy for the internal water waves equation by

(5.5)

E_{{\text{\tiny IWW}}}^{N}(\mathbf{U})=E^{N}(\mathbf{U})+C_{2}CE^{N-1}(\mathbf{U})+\sum\limits_{|\alpha|=N}\big(\mathbf{U}_{(\alpha)},\tilde{Q}(\mathbf{U})\mathbf{U}_{\langle\check{\alpha}\rangle}\big)_{L^{2}},

for some constants
C,C_{2}>0
to be fixed in the proof.

Proposition 5.2
.

Let
{\varepsilon},\mu,\mathrm{bo}^{-1}\in(0,1)
, such that
\varepsilon^{2}\leq\mathrm{bo}^{-1}
,
t_{0}=1
,
N\geq 5
, and let
\mathbf{U}=(\zeta,\psi)^{T}\in\mathscr{E}_{\mathrm{bo},\gamma}^{N,t_{0}}
be a solution to (
1.15
) on a time interval
[0,T]
for some
T>0
. Assume that
\mathbf{U}
satisfies the non-cavitation condition (
1.16
) and the stability criterion (
1.19
) on
[0,T]
. Then, for the modified energy defined by (
5.5
) there is a nondecreasing function of its argument
C=C(\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}),h_{\min}^{-1},(\mathfrak{d}(\mathbf{U}))^{-1})>0
such that,

(5.6)

\frac{d}{dt}E_{{\text{\tiny IWW}}}^{N}(\mathbf{U})\leq{\varepsilon}CE_{{\text{\tiny IWW}}}^{N}(\mathbf{U}),

for all
0<t<T
. Furthermore, for the energy (
5.4
) is equivalent to

\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U})=|\partial_{x}\psi|_{H^{t_{0}+2}}^{2}+\sum\limits_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{2},|\alpha|\leq N}|\zeta_{(\alpha)}|_{H^{1}_{\mathrm{bo}}}^{2}+|\psi_{(\alpha)}|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}.

In particular, there holds,

(5.7)

\frac{1}{C}\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U})\leq E^{N}(\mathbf{U})\leq C\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}),

and

(5.8)

\frac{1}{C}\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U})\leq E_{{\text{\tiny IWW}}}^{N}(\mathbf{U})\leq C\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}),

for all
0<t<T
.

Proof.

We first prove (
5.7
) and (
5.8
) in two separate steps before turning to the proof of (
5.6
).

Proof of (
5.7
).
First consider the case
k=0
, where we only deal with space derivatives. Let
(\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[0])^{\frac{1}{2}}
be the square root of the symbol associated to
\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[0]
and then use Plancherel’s identity to see that

\frac{1}{\mu}\big(\Lambda^{\frac{7}{2}}\psi,\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[0]\Lambda^{\frac{7}{2}}\psi\big)_{L^{2}}=\frac{1}{\mu}|(\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[0])^{\frac{1}{2}}\psi|_{H^{\frac{7}{2}}}^{2}.

Then use (
B.3
) and the definition (
5.1
) to obtain that

|\Lambda^{\frac{7}{2}}\zeta|_{H^{1}_{\mathrm{bo}}}^{2}+\frac{1}{C}|\partial_{x}\psi|_{H^{3}}^{2}\leq E^{0}(\mathbf{U})\leq C|\psi|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{4}}^{2}+\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}).

The lower bound will be absorbed in
\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U})
when summing over all
k
. While for the upper bound, we relate
\psi
with the definition of
\psi_{(\alpha)}=\partial_{x,t}^{\alpha}\psi-\varepsilon\underline{w}\zeta_{(\alpha)}
through estimate (
A.46
):

\ |\psi|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{(N-\frac{1}{2})+\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}\leq M\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}).

For the case
k=N
, we use the definition of the individual energies (
5.3
) to find that

\displaystyle E_{\alpha}(\mathbf{U})

\displaystyle=\big(\zeta_{(\alpha)},\mathfrak{Ins}[U]\zeta_{(\alpha)}\big)_{L^{2}}+\big(\zeta_{(\alpha)},(a(\mathbf{U})+b(\mathbf{U})\Lambda^{-1})\zeta_{(\alpha)}\big)_{L^{2}}+\frac{1}{\mu}\big(\psi_{(\alpha)},\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi_{(\alpha)}\big)_{L^{2}}.

The first two terms are estimated by (
4.22
) and (
4.19
). While the last term is controlled by (
2.6
) and (
2.8
), which implies the result

\frac{1}{C}\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U})\leq E^{N}(\mathbf{U})\leq C\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}).

Proof of (
5.8
).
Next, we prove (
5.8
). To estimate the additional term appearing in (
5.5
) when
|\alpha|=N
, we first observe that (suppressing the argument in
\zeta
):

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mu}|\big(\psi_{(\alpha)},\mathcal{G}_{{\mu},(\alpha)}\psi_{\langle\check{\alpha}\rangle}\big)_{L^{2}}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\mu}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{2}\alpha_{j}\Big(|\big(\psi_{(\alpha)},\mathrm{d}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{+}(\partial_{j}\zeta)(\mathcal{J}_{\mu})^{-1}\psi_{(\check{\alpha}^{j})}\big)_{L^{2}}|

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+|\big(\psi_{(\alpha)},\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{+}(\mathcal{J}_{\mu})^{-1}(\mathrm{d}\mathcal{J}_{\mu}(\partial_{j}\zeta))(\mathcal{J}_{\mu})^{-1}\psi_{(\check{\alpha}^{j})}\big)_{L^{2}}|\Big),

where
\check{\alpha}^{j}=\alpha-\mathbf{e}_{j}
and
\mathcal{G}_{{\mu},(\alpha)}
is given in Definition
4.1
. For the first term we use (
A.16
) with (
2.24
), while the for the second term we also use (
A.6
), and (
A.61
) to find that

(5.9)

\frac{1}{\mu}|\big(\psi_{(\alpha)},\mathcal{G}_{{\mu},(\alpha)}\psi_{\langle\check{\alpha}\rangle}\big)_{L^{2}}|\leq{\varepsilon}M|\psi_{(\alpha)}|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{2}|\psi_{(\check{\alpha}^{j})}|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}.

Then since the surface tension term can be treated directly by integration by parts, we use Young’s inequality to find that

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{|\alpha|=N}|\big(\mathbf{U}_{(\alpha)},\tilde{Q}(\mathbf{U})\mathbf{U}_{\langle\check{\alpha}\rangle}\big)_{L^{2}}|

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{|\alpha|=N}\mathrm{bo}^{-1}|\big(\zeta_{(\alpha)},\mathcal{K}_{(\alpha)}\zeta_{\langle\check{\alpha}\rangle}\big)_{L^{2}}|+\frac{1}{\mu}|\big(\psi_{(\alpha)},\mathcal{G}_{{\mu},(\alpha)}\psi_{\langle\check{\alpha}\rangle}\big)_{L^{2}}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2C_{2}}\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U})+\frac{C_{2}}{2}\mathcal{E}^{N-1}(\mathbf{U}),

for any
C_{2}>0
. Then let
C_{2}\geq C
and use (
5.7
) with the definition of the modified energy (
5.5
) to see that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}E^{N}(\mathbf{U})\leq E_{IWW}^{N}(\mathbf{U})\leq C_{2}CE^{N}(\mathbf{U}).

Lastly, we will prove energy estimate (
5.6
) by considering two cases.

An energy estimate in the case
k=0
.
We first use (
1.15
) to make the decomposition

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\zeta

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mu}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[0]\psi+{\varepsilon}\mathcal{N}_{1}(\mathbf{U})

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\psi

\displaystyle=-\big(1-\mathrm{bo}^{-1}\partial_{x}^{2}\big)\zeta+{\varepsilon}\mathcal{N}_{2}(\mathbf{U}),

where

\mathcal{N}_{1}(\mathbf{U})=\frac{1}{\mu{\varepsilon}}(\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]-\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[0])\psi,

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{2}(\mathbf{U})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}\mathrm{bo}}\partial_{x}\Big(\big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+{\varepsilon}^{2}\mu(\partial_{x}\zeta)^{2}}}-1\big)\partial_{x}\zeta\Big)-\frac{1}{2}\big((\partial_{x}\psi^{+})^{2}-\gamma(\partial_{x}\psi^{-})^{2}\big)-\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{U}).

The decomposition emphasizes the terms of order
{\varepsilon}
, while the linear terms are canceled by our choice of the energy:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}E^{0}(U)

\displaystyle=\big(\Lambda^{\frac{7}{2}}\partial_{t}\zeta,\big(1-\mathrm{bo}^{-1}\partial_{x}^{2}\big)\Lambda^{\frac{7}{2}}\zeta\big)_{L^{2}}+\frac{1}{\mu}\big(\Lambda^{\frac{7}{2}}\partial_{t}\psi,\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[0]\Lambda^{\frac{7}{2}}\psi\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle={\varepsilon}\big(\Lambda^{\frac{7}{2}}\mathcal{N}_{1}(\mathbf{U}),\big(1-\mathrm{bo}^{-1}\partial_{x}^{2}\big)\Lambda^{\frac{7}{2}}\zeta\big)_{L^{2}}+\frac{{\varepsilon}}{\mu}\big(\Lambda^{\frac{7}{2}}\mathcal{N}_{2}(\mathbf{U}),\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[0]\Lambda^{\frac{7}{2}}\psi\big)_{L^{2}}.

\displaystyle=A_{1}+A_{2}.

For the estimate on
A_{1}
, we use Plancherel’s identity, Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, and (
3.23
) with
s=t_{0}=3
to find that

\displaystyle|A_{1}|

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}|\mathcal{N}_{1}(\mathbf{U})|_{H^{3}}|\Lambda^{5}\zeta|_{H_{\gamma,\mathrm{bo}}^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}M(5)|\psi|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{\frac{9}{2}}}|\Lambda^{5}\zeta|_{H^{1}_{\gamma,\mathrm{bo}}}.

Then to estimate
\psi
, we use that
|\psi|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{\frac{9}{2}}}\leq|\psi|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{(N-1)+\frac{1}{2}}}
together with (
A.46
). Combining these estimates implies

\displaystyle|A_{1}|\leq{\varepsilon}C(\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}))\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}).

For the estimate on
A_{2}
, we integrate by parts and use the algebra property of
H^{\frac{7}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
to find that

\displaystyle|A_{2}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{{\varepsilon}}{\mu}\mathrm{bo}^{-1}|\Lambda^{5}\zeta|_{H^{1}}|\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[0]\psi|_{H^{3}}+\frac{{\varepsilon}}{\mu}(|\partial_{x}\psi^{+}|^{2}_{{H^{4}}}+|\partial_{x}\psi^{-}|^{2}_{{H^{4}}}+|\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{U})|_{H^{4}})|\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[0]\psi|_{H^{3}},

where we recall the definition

\mathcal{N}[{\varepsilon}\zeta,\psi]=\frac{1}{2\mu}\frac{\gamma(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi^{-}+{\varepsilon}\mu\partial_{x}\zeta\partial_{x}\psi^{-})^{2}-(\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi^{+}+{\varepsilon}\mu\partial_{x}\zeta\partial_{x}\psi^{+})^{2}}{(1+\varepsilon^{2}\mu(\partial_{x}\zeta)^{2})}.

To conclude, we use (
B.2
) to control
\frac{1}{\mu}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[0]\psi
together with (
A.46
) in the first term:

\frac{\mathrm{bo}^{-1}}{\mu}|\Lambda^{5}\zeta|_{H^{1}}|\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[0]\psi|_{H^{3}}\lesssim|\Lambda^{5}\zeta|_{H^{1}_{\mathrm{bo}}}|\psi|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{9}{2}}_{\mu}}\leq\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}).

For the second term, we use Corollary
A.18
and estimates (
A.40
) with (
A.46
) to get that

|\partial_{x}\psi^{+}|^{2}_{{H^{4}}}+|\partial_{x}\psi^{-}|_{{H^{4}}}^{2}\leq M|\partial_{x}\psi|_{H^{4}}^{2}\leq M|\psi|_{\dot{H}^{5}_{\mu}}^{2}\leq M\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}).

Lastly, to deal with the terms in
\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{U})
we use the algebra property of
{H^{4}}({\mathbb{R}})
, estimate (
A.42
) to obtain

\displaystyle|\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{U})|_{{H^{4}}}\leq M(\frac{\gamma}{\mu}|\underline{w}^{-}|^{2}_{{H^{4}}}+\frac{1}{\mu}|\underline{w}^{+}|_{{H^{4}}}^{2})\leq M|\psi|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{5}}^{2}\leq M\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}),

Gathering all these estimates, we find that

(5.10)

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}E^{0}(U)\leq{\varepsilon}C(\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}))\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}).

An energy estimate in the case
1\leq k\leq N
.
We first derive an estimate on each
E_{\alpha}(\mathbf{U})
defined by (
5.3
) for
1\leq|\alpha|\leq N
. To do so, we use that
Q(\mathbf{U})
is symmetric up to a lower order term to find that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}E_{\alpha}(\mathbf{U})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\big(\mathbf{U}_{(\alpha)},\big(\partial_{t}Q(\mathbf{U})\big)\mathbf{U}_{(\alpha)}\big)_{L^{2}}+\big(\partial_{t}\mathbf{U}_{(\alpha)},Q(\mathbf{U})\mathbf{U}_{(\alpha)}\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\frac{1}{2}\big(\mathbf{U}_{(\alpha)},\big(Q(\mathbf{U})-Q(\mathbf{U})^{\ast}\big)\partial_{t}\mathbf{U}_{(\alpha)}\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=B_{1}+B_{2}+B_{3},

where
B_{3}
is the contribution of the lower order term and reads

\displaystyle B_{3}=-\frac{1}{2}(1-\gamma)\gamma{\varepsilon}^{2}\mu\Big((\zeta_{(\alpha)}[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!],\big(\mathfrak{E}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]-\mathfrak{E}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]^{\ast}\big)\big(\partial_{t}\zeta_{(\alpha)}[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]\big)\Big)_{L^{2}}.

Control of
B_{1}
.
By definition of
Q(\mathbf{U})
given by (
5.2
), we find that

\displaystyle B_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\big([\partial_{t},\mathfrak{Ins}(\mathbf{U})]\zeta_{(\alpha)},\zeta_{(\alpha)}\big)_{L^{2}}+\frac{1}{2\mu}\big([\partial_{t},\mathcal{G}]\psi_{(\alpha)},\psi_{(\alpha)}\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\frac{1}{2}\big((\partial_{t}a)\zeta_{(\alpha)},\zeta_{(\alpha)}\big)_{L^{2}}+\frac{1}{2}\big((\partial_{t}b)\zeta_{(\alpha)},\Lambda^{-1}\zeta_{(\alpha)}\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=B_{1}^{1}+B_{1}^{2}+B_{1}^{3}+B_{1}^{4}.

For the estimate on
B_{1}^{1}
, we have by definition and integration by parts that

\displaystyle B_{1}^{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\big(\zeta_{(\alpha)},\big(\partial_{t}\mathfrak{a}\big)\zeta_{(\alpha)}\big)_{L^{2}}+\frac{1}{2\mathrm{bo}}\big(\partial_{x}\zeta_{(\alpha)},\big[\partial_{t},\mathcal{K}[{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}\partial_{x}\zeta]\big]\partial_{x}\zeta_{(\alpha)}\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-(1-\gamma)\gamma{\varepsilon}^{2}\mu\big(\big(\partial_{t}[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]\big)\zeta_{(\alpha)},\mathfrak{E}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\big(\zeta_{(\alpha)}[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]\big)\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-\frac{1}{2}(1-\gamma)\gamma{\varepsilon}^{2}\mu\big([\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]\zeta_{(\alpha)},\big[\partial_{t},\mathfrak{E}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\big]\big(\zeta_{(\alpha)}[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]\big)\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=B_{1}^{1,1}+B_{1}^{1,2}+B_{1}^{1,3}+B_{1}^{1,4}.

For the estimate on
B_{1}^{1,1}
, we have an estimate on
\partial_{t}\mathfrak{a}
in
L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}})
given by (
A.48
). In particular, the Hölder inequality implies that

\displaystyle|B_{1}^{1,1}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}|\partial_{t}\mathfrak{a}|_{H^{s}}|\zeta_{(\alpha)}|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon C\big(\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U})\big)\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}),

where we used that
t_{0}+3\leq N
.

For the estimate on
B_{1}^{1,2}
, it only depends on
\partial_{t}\mathcal{K}[{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}\partial_{x}\zeta]
which is in
L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}})
. Indeed, it follows from an estimate on
\partial_{x}\partial_{t}\zeta
, where we use the Sobolev embedding:

\displaystyle|\partial_{x}\partial_{t}\zeta|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\leq|\partial_{t}\zeta|_{H^{2}}^{2}\leq\sum\limits_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{2},|\alpha|\leq 3}|\zeta_{(\alpha)}|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}).

As a result, we obtain by Hölder’s inequality and the definition of the energy that

\displaystyle|B_{1}^{1,2}|\leq{\varepsilon}C\big(\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U})\big)\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}).

For the estimate on
B_{1}^{1,3}
, we use estimates (
4.18
), (
4.1
), (
A.45
), and (
A.50
) to find that

\displaystyle B_{1}^{1,3}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1-\gamma)\gamma\mathfrak{e}(\zeta){\varepsilon}^{2}|(1+\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|)^{\frac{1}{2}}(\zeta_{(\alpha)}[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!])|_{L^{2}}|(1+\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|)^{\frac{1}{2}}(\zeta_{(\alpha)}\partial_{t}[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!])|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}C\big(\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U})\big)\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U})+(1-\gamma)\gamma\mathfrak{e}(\zeta){\varepsilon}^{2}\sqrt{\mu}\max\limits_{|\alpha|\leq 1}|\partial_{x,t}^{\alpha}[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}|\zeta_{(\alpha)}|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}C\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}).

Likewise, the estimate on
B_{1}^{1,4}
we simply use (
4.15
) instead of (
4.14
) to obtain the same bound and we deduce that

\displaystyle|B_{1}^{1}|\leq{\varepsilon}C\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}).

Clearly, the estimates on
B_{1}^{3}
and
B_{1}^{4}
is estimated similarly to
B_{1}^{1,1}
, while we estimate
B_{1}^{2}
we have by direct computations that

\displaystyle|B_{1}^{2}|\leq\frac{1}{2\mu}\big([\partial_{t},\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{+}](\mathcal{J}_{\mu})^{-1}\psi_{(\alpha)},\psi_{(\alpha)}\big)_{L^{2}}+\frac{1}{2\mu}\big(\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{+}(\mathcal{J}_{\mu})^{-1}(\mathrm{d}\mathcal{J}_{\mu}(\partial_{t}\zeta))(\mathcal{J}_{\mu})^{-1}\psi_{(\alpha)},\psi_{(\alpha)}\big)_{L^{2}}.

For the first term we use Proposition
3.6
of
[
9
]
with (
2.24
), while the for the second term we also use (
A.6
), (
2.24
), and then (
A.60
) to find that

\displaystyle|B_{1}^{2}|\leq{\varepsilon}C\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}).

Collecting each estimate so far gives

\displaystyle|B_{1}|\leq{\varepsilon}C\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}).

Control of
B_{2}
.
We use system (
4.7
) to find that

\displaystyle B_{2}

\displaystyle=-\big(\mathcal{A}[\mathbf{U}]\mathbf{U}_{(\alpha)},Q(\mathbf{U})\mathbf{U}_{(\alpha)}\big)_{L^{2}}-\big(\mathcal{B}[\mathbf{U}]\mathbf{U}_{(\alpha)},Q(\mathbf{U})\mathbf{U}_{(\alpha)}\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-\big(\mathcal{C}[\mathbf{U}]\mathbf{U}_{\langle\check{\alpha}\rangle},Q(\mathbf{U})\mathbf{U}_{(\alpha)}\big)_{L^{2}}+{\varepsilon}\big((R_{\alpha},S_{\alpha})^{T},Q(\mathbf{U})\mathbf{U}_{(\alpha)}\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=B_{2}^{1}+B_{2}^{2}+B_{2}^{3}+B_{2}^{4}.

Control of
B_{2}^{1}
.
From Definition
4.1
and (
5.2
) we have that

\big(\mathcal{A}[\mathbf{U}]\mathbf{U}_{(\alpha)},Q^{(1)}(\mathbf{U})\mathbf{U}_{(\alpha)}\big)_{L^{2}}=0.

We may therefore decompose
B_{2}^{1}
into two pieces:

\displaystyle B_{2}^{1}

\displaystyle=-\big(\mathcal{A}[\mathbf{U}]\mathbf{U}_{(\alpha)},Q_{2}(\mathbf{U})\mathbf{U}_{(\alpha)}\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=a(\mathbf{U})\big(\frac{1}{\mu}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}\psi_{(\alpha)},\zeta_{(\alpha)}\big)_{L^{2}}+b(\mathbf{U})\big(\frac{1}{\mu}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}\psi_{(\alpha)},\Lambda^{-1}\zeta_{(\alpha)}\big)_{L^{2}}.

Both terms are estimated directly by (
2.8
), (
A.45
) combined with Sobolev embedding to estimate
a(\mathbf{U})
, to find that

\displaystyle|B_{2}^{1}|

\displaystyle\leq M|\psi_{(\alpha)}|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}\big(a(\mathbf{U})|\zeta_{(\alpha)}|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}+b(\mathbf{U})|\Lambda^{-1}\zeta_{(\alpha)}|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C|\psi_{(\alpha)}|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}\big({\varepsilon}^{2}|\partial_{x}\zeta_{(\alpha)}|_{L^{2}}+b(\mathbf{U})|\zeta_{(\alpha)}|_{L^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}C\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}),

where we used that
{\varepsilon}\leq\mathrm{bo}^{-\frac{1}{2}}
.

Control of
B_{2}^{2}
.
We first decompose
B_{2}^{2}
into four parts

\displaystyle B_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle={\varepsilon}\big(\mathcal{I}[\mathbf{U}]\zeta_{(\alpha)},\mathfrak{Ins}[\mathbf{U}]\zeta_{(\alpha)}\big)_{L^{2}}-\frac{{\varepsilon}}{\mu}\big(\mathcal{I}^{\ast}[\mathbf{U}]\psi_{(\alpha)},\mathcal{G}_{\mu}\psi_{(\alpha)}\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+{\varepsilon}a(\mathbf{U})\big(\mathcal{I}[\mathbf{U}]\zeta_{(\alpha)},\zeta_{(\alpha)}\big)_{L^{2}}+{\varepsilon}b(\mathbf{U})\big(\mathcal{I}[\mathbf{U}]\zeta_{(\alpha)},\Lambda^{-1}\zeta_{(\alpha)}\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=B_{2}^{2,1}+B_{2}^{2,2}+B_{2}^{2,3}+B_{2}^{2,4}.

We first observe that the two last terms are easily treated with an estimate of the type (
4.11
) to get that

\displaystyle|B_{2}^{2,3}|+|B_{2}^{2,4}|

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}C\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}).

For the remaining two terms, we will need to work some more.

Control of
B_{2}^{2,1}
.
Clearly, by (
4.23
) it is enough to prove that

(5.11)

\displaystyle|B_{2}^{2,1}|\leq\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U})\big(1+{\varepsilon}^{2}\sqrt{\mu}|[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]|_{W^{1,\infty}}^{2}|\zeta|^{2}_{<N+\frac{1}{2}>}\big).

Since the quantities involved in this depend on
(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu})^{-1}
, we need to adapt the proof of
[
65
]
. However, the proof relies on the symbolic expression of
(\mathcal{J}_{\mu})^{-1}(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu})^{-1}\partial_{x}
, and this estimate is provided in Corollary
3.9
which has the same outcome as the one in
[
65
]
. In particular, we refer the reader to the proof of estimate
(5.15)
in this paper.

Control of
B_{2}^{2,2}
.
This estimate is similar to estimate
(5.16)
in
[
65
]
, but we give the details here to account for the difference in
\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}
. In particular, we have by definition (
4.3
), that we must provide an estimate on the terms

\displaystyle B_{2}^{2,2}

\displaystyle=\frac{{\varepsilon}}{\mu}\big(\underline{V}^{+}\partial_{x}\psi_{(\alpha)},\mathcal{G}_{\mu}\psi_{(\alpha)}\big)_{L^{2}}+\gamma\frac{{\varepsilon}}{\mu}\big([\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]\partial_{x}\big((\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu})^{-1}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}\psi_{(\alpha)}\big),\mathcal{G}_{\mu}\psi_{(\alpha)}\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=B_{2}^{2,2,1}+B_{2}^{2,2,2}.

We let
g=(\mathcal{J}_{\mu})^{-1}\psi_{(\alpha)}
and use the definition
\mathcal{G}_{\mu}=\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{+}(\mathcal{J}_{\mu})^{-1}
to find that

\displaystyle B_{2}^{2,2,1}

\displaystyle=\frac{{\varepsilon}}{\mu}\big(\underline{V}^{+}\partial_{x}\mathcal{J}_{\mu}g,\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}g\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{{\varepsilon}}{\mu}\big(\underline{V}^{+}\partial_{x}g,\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}g\big)_{L^{2}}-\gamma\frac{{\varepsilon}}{\mu}\big(\underline{V}^{+}\partial_{x}(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu})^{-1}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}g,\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}g\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=B_{2}^{2,2,1,1}+B_{2}^{2,2,1,2}.

For the estimate on
B_{2}^{2,2,1,1}
, we use (
A.10
), combined with Sobolev embedding, (
2.24
), and (
A.45
) to obtain,

\displaystyle|B_{2}^{2,2,1,1}|

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}M|\underline{V}^{+}|_{W^{1,\infty}}|g|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}M|\underline{V}^{+}|_{H^{2}}|\psi_{(\alpha)}|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}C\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}).

For the estimate on
B_{2}^{2,2,1,2}
, we let
\tilde{g}=(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu})^{-1}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}\psi_{(\alpha)}
and use (
A.27
). It is then straightforward to obtain the desired bound using also (
2.11
):

\displaystyle|B_{2}^{2,2,1,1}|

\displaystyle\leq\gamma\frac{{\varepsilon}}{\mu}|\big(\underline{V}^{+}\partial_{x}\tilde{g},\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}\tilde{g}\big)_{L^{2}}|

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}M|\underline{V}^{+}|_{W^{1,\infty}}|\tilde{g}|_{\mathring{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}C\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}).

Lastly, for the estimate on
B_{2}^{2,2,2}
we let
g^{\sharp}=(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu})^{-1}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}\psi_{(\alpha)}
, and again use (
A.27
) with (
2.4
) and (
2.24
):

\displaystyle|B_{2}^{2,2,2}|

\displaystyle=\gamma\frac{{\varepsilon}}{\mu}|\big([\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]\partial_{x}g^{\sharp},\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}g^{\sharp}\big)_{L^{2}}|

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}M|(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu})^{-1}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}\mathcal{J}_{\mu}^{-1}\psi_{(\alpha)}|_{\mathring{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}C\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}).

From these estimates, we conclude the bound on
B_{2}^{2}
where we find that

\displaystyle|B_{2}^{2}|\leq{\varepsilon}C\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}).

Control of
B_{2}^{3}
.
To get an estimate of order
{\varepsilon}
we need to cancel the following term (when
k=N
):

\displaystyle B_{2}^{3,1}:=\big(\mathcal{C}[\mathbf{U}]\mathbf{U}_{\langle\check{\alpha}\rangle},Q^{(1)}(\mathbf{U})\mathbf{U}_{(\alpha)}\big)_{L^{2}}=-\big(\mathcal{A}[\mathbf{U}]\mathbf{U}_{(\alpha)},\tilde{Q}(\mathbf{U})\mathbf{U}_{\langle\check{\alpha}\rangle}\big)_{L^{2}}.

We will cancel
B_{2}^{3,1}
later by introducing
\tilde{Q}
into the energy (see Remark
5.1
). Therefore, we may decompose
(B_{2}^{3}-B_{2}^{3,1})
into two parts:

\displaystyle(B_{2}^{3}-B_{2}^{3,1})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mu}a(\mathbf{U})\big(\mathcal{G}_{\mu,(\alpha)}\psi_{\langle\check{\alpha}\rangle},\zeta_{(\alpha)}\big)_{L^{2}}+\frac{1}{\mu}b(\mathbf{U})\big(\mathcal{G}_{\mu,(\alpha)}\psi_{\langle\check{\alpha}\rangle},\Lambda^{-1}\zeta_{(\alpha)}\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=B_{2}^{3,2}+B_{2}^{3,3}.

However, these terms are easily dealt with using estimates on
\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}
and
(\mathcal{J}_{\mu})^{-1}
(similar to (
5.9
)) and that
{\varepsilon}\leq\mathrm{bo}^{-\frac{1}{2}}
to obtain

\displaystyle|B_{2}^{3,2}|+|B_{2}^{3,3}|\leq{\varepsilon}C\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}).

Control of
B_{2}^{4}
.
For
B_{2}^{4}
we have to estimate the following terms,

\displaystyle B_{2}^{4}

\displaystyle={\varepsilon}\big(R_{\alpha},\mathfrak{Ins}[\mathbf{U}]\zeta_{(\alpha)}\big)_{L^{2}}+\frac{{\varepsilon}}{\mu}\big(S_{\alpha},\mathcal{G}_{\mu}\psi_{(\alpha)}\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+{\varepsilon}a(\mathbf{U})\big(R_{\alpha},\zeta_{(\alpha)}\big)_{L^{2}}+{\varepsilon}b(\mathbf{U})\big(R_{\alpha},\Lambda^{-1}\zeta_{(\alpha)}\big)_{L^{2}}.

For the first term, we use (
4.19
) and (
4.8
) to obtain,

\displaystyle{\varepsilon}|\big(R_{\alpha},\mathfrak{Ins}[\mathbf{U}]\zeta_{(\alpha)}\big)_{L^{2}}|\leq{\varepsilon}C\mathcal{E}^{1}(\mathbf{U})|R_{\alpha}|_{H^{1}_{\mathrm{bo}}}\leq{\varepsilon}C\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}).

For the second term is treated by (
2.8
), (
4.8
), and (
4.23
) to get

\displaystyle\frac{{\varepsilon}}{\mu}|\big(S_{\alpha},\mathcal{G}_{\mu}\psi_{(\alpha)}\big)_{L^{2}}|\leq{\varepsilon}M|S_{\alpha}|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}|\psi_{(\alpha)}|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\leq{\varepsilon}C\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}).

The remaining two estimates satisfy the same upper bound, and so gathering all these estimates, we find that

(5.12)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}E_{\alpha}(\mathbf{U})-B_{1}^{3,1}\leq{\varepsilon}C\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}).

As pointed out above, we need to cancel
B_{2}^{3,1}
and is done by modifying the energy by

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big(\mathbf{U}_{(\alpha)},\tilde{Q}(\mathbf{U})\mathbf{U}_{\langle\check{\alpha}\rangle}\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\big(\mathbf{U}_{(\alpha)},\big(\partial_{t}\tilde{Q}(\mathbf{U})\big)\mathbf{U}_{\langle\check{\alpha}\rangle}\big)_{L^{2}}-\big(\mathcal{A}[\mathbf{U}]\mathbf{U}_{(\alpha)},\tilde{Q}(\mathbf{U})\mathbf{U}_{\langle\check{\alpha}\rangle}\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-\big(\mathcal{B}[\mathbf{U}]\mathbf{U}_{(\alpha)},\tilde{Q}(\mathbf{U})\mathbf{U}_{\langle\check{\alpha}\rangle}\big)_{L^{2}}-\big(\mathcal{C}[\mathbf{U}]\mathbf{U}_{\langle\check{\alpha}\rangle},\tilde{Q}(\mathbf{U})\mathbf{U}_{\langle\check{\alpha}\rangle}\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+{\varepsilon}\big((R_{\alpha},S_{\alpha})^{T},\tilde{Q}(\mathbf{U})\mathbf{U}_{\langle\check{\alpha}\rangle}\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=\tilde{B}_{1}+\tilde{B}_{2}+\tilde{B}_{3}+\tilde{B}_{4}+\tilde{B}_{5}.

Where we observe that
\tilde{B}_{2}=-B_{2}^{3,1}
and gives the desired cancellation (when
k=N
), while the remaining terms are treated as above and satisfy

|\tilde{B}_{1}|+|\tilde{B}_{3}|+|\tilde{B}_{4}|+|\tilde{B}_{5}|\leq{\varepsilon}C\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}).

We therefore find that

(5.13)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\Big(E_{\alpha}(\mathbf{U})+\big(\mathbf{U}_{(\alpha)},\tilde{Q}(\mathbf{U})\mathbf{U}_{\langle\check{\alpha}\rangle}\big)_{L^{2}}\Big)\leq{\varepsilon}C\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U})+|B_{3}|,

where we recall that
\tilde{Q}=0
when
|\alpha|<N
.

Control of
B_{3}
.
For the estimate on
B_{3}
, we use (
4.16
), (
B.14
), (
A.45
),
{\varepsilon}^{2}\leq\mathrm{bo}^{-1}
, to find that

\displaystyle|B_{3}|

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}^{3}\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}M(t_{0}+3)|\zeta_{(\alpha)}[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}|\partial_{t}\zeta_{(\alpha)}[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}^{2}\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}C(\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}))|\zeta_{(\alpha)}|_{H^{1}_{\mathrm{bo}}}|\partial_{t}\zeta_{(\alpha)}|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}}.

Now for the control of
\partial_{t}\zeta_{(\alpha)}
, we use equation (
4.7
) to see that

\displaystyle|\partial_{t}\zeta_{(\alpha)}|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\mu}|\mathcal{G}_{\mu}\psi_{(\alpha)}|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}}+\frac{1}{\mu}|\mathcal{G}_{\mu,(\alpha)}\psi_{(\alpha)}|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}}+{\varepsilon}|\mathcal{I}[\mathbf{U}]\zeta_{(\alpha)}|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}}+{\varepsilon}|R_{\alpha}|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}},

where
R_{\alpha}
satisfies (
4.8
). Then to conclude this estimate, we simply employ (
2.2
), (
4.9
), and (
4.8
) to deduce that

|B_{3}|\leq{\varepsilon}C\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}).

Proof of estimate (
5.6
).
To conclude, we use (
5.10
) combined with (
5.13
) where we sum over
0\leq|\alpha|\leq N
and then apply estimate (
5.8
) to find that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}E_{{\text{\tiny IWW}}}^{N}(\mathbf{U})\leq{\varepsilon}CE_{{\text{\tiny IWW}}}^{N}(\mathbf{U}).

∎

Remark 5.3
.

The restiction
\varepsilon^{2}\leq\mathrm{bo}^{-1}
stems from the estimate on
B_{2}^{1}
and
B_{3}
.

6.
Proof of Theorem
1.7

The strategy for the proof of Theorem
1.7
is classical, and we refer the reader to
[
66
]
Chapter
4
for a detailed proof in the case of the water waves equations. See also Chapter
9
in the same book in the case of the water waves equations with surface tension. We will now give the main steps involved.

Proof.

The first step is to regularize the internal water waves equations (
1.15
), and use the Fixed Point Theorem to deduce the existence of a solution. In fact, the solution is smooth. However, the existence time depends on the regularization parameter and shrinks to zero when taking the limit. To extend the existence time, one applies Proposition
5.2
and then a compactness argument to deduce a limit. Note however, this requires us to verify the stability criteria and the non-cavitatation condition on a long-time scale where we merely assume the non-cavitation condition (
1.16
), and the stability criterion (
1.19
) for the initial data
5
5

5

This is an abuse of notation, note that terms involving time derivatives are not initially prescribed. But they can be relplaced with spatial derivatives using shape derivative formulas and the equation for
\partial_{t}\zeta
and
\partial_{t}\psi
.
. For clarity, we recall (
1.19
) (in the case of the non-cavitation condition see for instance Lemma 5.3 in
[
84
]
):

0<\mathfrak{d}(\mathbf{U}_{0})=\inf\mathfrak{a}|_{t=0}-\Upsilon\mathfrak{c}(\zeta|_{t=0})|[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}|_{t=0}]\!]|_{L^{\infty}}^{4}.

To verify the stability criterion on a long time scale follows classically from a boot-strap argument and

\sup\limits_{t\in[0,\frac{T}{\varepsilon}]}\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U})\leq C\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}_{0}),

if we can verify the inequality

(6.1)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}(\mathbf{U})(t)\geq\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{d}(\mathbf{U}_{0}),

for any
t\in[0,\frac{T}{\varepsilon}]
where
T=C^{-1}>0
with
C=C(\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}_{0}),{h^{-1}_{\min}},\mathfrak{d}(\mathbf{U}_{0})^{-1})
. To prove (
6.1
), we use (
A.48
) to estimate
\partial_{t}\mathfrak{a}
, (
4.15
) to estimate
\mathfrak{c}(\partial_{t}\zeta)
, and for the estimates on
\underline{V}^{\pm}
we use the Sobolev embedding and (
A.50
) to find that

|\partial_{t}\mathfrak{d}(\mathbf{U})(t)|_{L^{\infty}}\leq(\varepsilon+\Upsilon)C\big(\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U})(t)\big),

for all
t\in[0,\frac{T}{\varepsilon}]
. From this estimate we may employ the Fundamental Theorem of Calculus to deduce that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}(\mathbf{U})(t)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{d}(\mathbf{U}_{0})-\int_{0}^{t}(\partial_{t}\mathfrak{d}(\mathbf{U}))(s)\>\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\geq\mathfrak{d}(\mathbf{U}_{0})-\frac{T}{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon+\Upsilon)C,

which implies (
6.1
) by taking
T
small enough, depending only on
C
, and
\Upsilon\sim\varepsilon^{4}\mu\mathrm{bo}\lesssim\varepsilon
which is ensured by the assumption that
\varepsilon^{2}\leq\mathrm{bo}^{-1}
.

For uniqueness, one needs to have an estimate of the difference between two solutions, which can be proved with estimates similar to the ones used for the proof of Proposition
5.2
.
∎

Remark 6.1
.

Here we needed to make the restriction
\Upsilon\lesssim\varepsilon
Also, note if we have imposed the stronger criterion (
4.27
) we would have
\varepsilon^{-2}\Upsilon\lesssim\varepsilon
and implies
\varepsilon\mu\leq\mathrm{bo}^{-1}
.

7.
Proof of Theorem
1.15

Since the long-time well-posedness of the unidirectional model (
1.27
) is classical (see Remark
1.17
) we only give the proof for (
1.26
). The strategy of the proof is the same as for (
1.15
). It relies on the energy method, where we need to find a suitable symmetrizer for the system. For simplicity, we define

u=\mathrm{t}(\mathrm{D})v\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbf{U}=(\zeta,u)^{T},

using it to write (
1.26
) on the compact form:

(7.1)

\partial_{t}\mathbf{U}+\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{U})\mathbf{U}=\mathbf{0},

with

(7.2)

\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{U})=\begin{pmatrix}{\varepsilon}\mathrm{t}(\mathrm{D})\big(u\partial_{x}\bullet\big)&\partial_{x}+{\varepsilon}\mathrm{t}(\mathrm{D})\big(\zeta\partial_{x}\bullet\big)\\
\mathrm{k}(\mathrm{D})\partial_{x}&{\varepsilon}\mathrm{t}(\mathrm{D})\big(u\partial_{x}\bullet\big)\end{pmatrix},

To define an energy, we introduce the symmetrizer

(7.3)

\mathcal{Q}(\mathbf{U})=\begin{pmatrix}(1-\mathrm{bo}^{-1}\partial_{x}^{2})&0\\
0&\mathrm{t}^{-1}(\mathrm{D})+\varepsilon\zeta\end{pmatrix}.

Then the energy associated with
X^{s}_{\mu,\mathrm{bo}}({\mathbb{R}})
, given in Definition
1.14
, for the weakly dispersive Benjamin system (
1.26
) can be written as

E_{\text{\tiny wBs}}^{s}(\mathbf{U})=\big(\Lambda^{s}\mathbf{U},\mathcal{Q}(\mathbf{U})\Lambda^{s}\mathbf{U}\big)_{L^{2}}.

We will use this energy to deduce a bound on the solutions of (
7.1
). As noted in the proof Theorem
1.7
, this is not enough. One also needs a uniqueness type estimate to establish the well-posedness with the energy method. This estimate is derived similarly, but since we will need it for the proof of the full justification, we also state it in the next result. For convenience, we let two solutions of (
7.1
) be given by
\mathbf{U}_{1}=(\zeta_{1},u_{1})^{T}
and
\mathbf{U}_{2}=(\zeta_{2},u_{2})^{T}
where we define
(\eta,w)=(\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{2},u_{1}-u_{2})
. Then
\mathbf{W}=(\eta,w)^{T}
solves

(7.4)

\partial_{t}\mathbf{W}+\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{U}_{1})\mathbf{W}=\mathbf{F},

with
\mathcal{M}
defined as in (
7.2
) and the source term is given by

(7.5)

\mathbf{F}=-\big(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{U}_{1})-\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{U}_{2})\big)\mathbf{U}_{2}.

The energy associated to (
7.4
) is given in terms of the symmetrizer
\mathcal{Q}(\mathbf{U}_{1})
defined in (
7.3
) and reads

(7.6)

\tilde{E}^{s}_{\text{\tiny wBs}}(\mathbf{W}):=\big(\Lambda^{s}\mathbf{W},\mathcal{Q}(\mathbf{U}_{1})\Lambda^{s}\mathbf{W}\big)_{L^{2}}.

From these energies, we have the following
a priori
estimate and an estimate of the difference between two solutions.

Proposition 7.1
.

Let
{\varepsilon},\mu,\mathrm{bo}^{-1}\in(0,1)
,
s>\frac{3}{2}
, and
(\zeta,u)\in C([0,T];X^{s}_{\mu,\mathrm{bo}}({\mathbb{R}}))
be a solution to (
7.1
) on a time interval
[0,T]
for some
T>0
. Moreover, assume that (
1.16
) holds for
\zeta
uniformly in time and let
K(s)=C_{1}(h_{\min}^{-1},|(\zeta,u)|_{X^{s}_{\mu,\mathrm{bo}}})>0
be a nondecreasing function of its argument. Then, for the energy given in Definition
5.4
, there holds,

1.

For all
0<t<T
, we have that

(7.7)

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}E^{s}_{\text{\tiny wBs}}(\mathbf{U})\leq{\varepsilon}K(s)E_{s}(\mathbf{U}).

2.

For all
0<t<T
, there exist
C_{2}>0
such that

(7.8)

(K(s))^{-1}|(\zeta,u)|^{2}_{X^{s}_{\mu,\mathrm{bo}}}\leq E^{s}_{\text{\tiny wBs}}(\mathbf{U})\leq K(s)|(\zeta,u)|^{2}_{X^{s}_{\mu,\mathrm{bo}}}.

Moreover, let
(\zeta_{1},u_{1}),(\zeta_{2},u_{2})\in C([0,T];X^{s}_{\mu,\mathrm{bo}}({\mathbb{R}}))
be solutions to (
7.1
) on a time interval
[0,T]
and satisfying the condition (
1.16
) uniformly in time. Define the difference to be
\mathbf{W}=(\eta,w)=(\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{2},u_{1}-u_{2})
and for
i=1,2
let
\tilde{K}(s)=C_{2}(h_{\min}^{-1},|(\zeta_{i},v_{i})|_{X^{s}_{\mu,\mathrm{bo}}})>0
be a nondecreasing function of its argument. Then, for the energy defined by (
7.6
), there holds

3.

For all
0<t<T
, we have that

(7.9)

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\tilde{E}^{0}_{\text{\tiny wBs}}(\mathbf{W})\leq{\varepsilon}\tilde{K}(s)\tilde{E}^{0}_{\text{\tiny wBs}}(\mathbf{W}).

4.

For all
0<t<T
, we have that

(7.10)

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\tilde{E}^{s}_{\text{\tiny wBs}}(\mathbf{W})\leq{\varepsilon}\tilde{K}(s+1)\tilde{E}^{s}_{\text{\tiny wBs}}(\mathbf{W}).

5.

For all
0<t<T
and
r\geq 0
, there exist
C_{3}(h_{\min}^{-1},|\zeta|_{L^{\infty}})>0
nondecreasing function of its argument such that

(7.11)

C_{3}^{-1}|(\eta,w)|^{2}_{X^{r}_{\mu,\mathrm{bo}}}\leq\tilde{E}^{r}_{\text{\tiny wBs}}(\mathbf{W})\leq C_{3}|(\eta,w)|^{2}_{X^{r}_{\mu,\mathrm{bo}}}.

Remark 7.2
.

In estimate (
7.10
), we observe that there is a loss of derivative. This is not sufficient for the proof of the continuous dependence with respect to initial data. In fact, one would have to refine this estimate using a Bona-Smith argument
[
20
]
. Since the system is almost identical to one in
[
84
]
we only detail the main differences. With this in mind, we simply allow for this rough estimate and use it to deduce the convergence estimate when comparing its solution with the ones of the internal water waves system.

Proof of Proposition
7.1
.

The proof of point
3-5
is similar to the proof of the first two points. We will therefore only prove (
7.7
) and (
7.8
).

We first prove estimate (
7.8
). By definition, we have that

\displaystyle E^{s}_{\text{\tiny wBs}}(\mathbf{U})=|\Lambda^{s}\zeta|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\mathrm{bo}^{-1}|\Lambda^{s}\partial_{x}\zeta|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\big(\Lambda^{s}u,(\mathrm{t}^{-1}(\mathrm{D})+{\varepsilon}\zeta)\Lambda^{s}u\big)_{L^{2}}.

Then, as a result of the non-cavitation condition (
1.16
) and (
B.13
) we find that

\displaystyle\big(\Lambda^{s}u,(\mathrm{t}^{-1}(\mathrm{D})+{\varepsilon}\zeta)\Lambda^{s}u\big)_{L^{2}}\geq\frac{h_{0}}{2}|u|_{H^{s}}^{2}+C\sqrt{\mu}|u|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}^{2},

for any
h_{0}\in(0,1-h_{\min})
and some
C>0
. The reverse inequality is a consequence of (
B.13
), Hölder’s inequality, and the Sobolev embedding with
s>\frac{1}{2}
:

\displaystyle E^{s}_{\text{\tiny wBs}}(\mathbf{U})

\displaystyle\leq|\Lambda^{s}\zeta|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\mathrm{bo}^{-1}|\Lambda^{s}\partial_{x}\zeta|^{2}_{L^{2}}+|\Lambda^{s}\mathrm{t}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\mathrm{D})u|_{H^{s}}^{2}+{\varepsilon}|\zeta|_{L^{\infty}}|\Lambda^{s}u|_{H^{s}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(|\zeta|_{H^{s+1}_{\mathrm{bo}}}^{2}+|\Lambda^{s}u|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\sqrt{\mu}|u|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}).

Next, we prove (
7.7
). By using (
7.1
) and the fact that
\mathcal{Q}(\mathbf{U})
is self-adjoint, we compute

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}E^{s}_{\text{\tiny wBs}}(\mathbf{U})

\displaystyle=-\big(\Lambda^{s}\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{U})\mathbf{U},\mathcal{Q}(\mathbf{U})\Lambda^{s}\mathbf{U}\big)_{L^{2}}+\frac{1}{2}\big(\Lambda^{s}\mathbf{U},(\partial_{t}\mathcal{Q}(\mathbf{U}))\Lambda^{s}\mathbf{U}\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=-{\varepsilon}\big(\Lambda^{s}(u\partial_{x}\zeta),\mathrm{k}(\mathrm{D})\Lambda^{s}\zeta\big)_{L^{2}}-\big(\Lambda^{s}(\partial_{x}u+{\varepsilon}\mathrm{t}(\mathrm{D})(\zeta\partial_{x}u)),(1-\mathrm{bo}^{-1}\partial_{x}^{2})\Lambda^{s}\zeta\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-\big(\Lambda^{s}\mathrm{k}(\mathrm{D})\partial_{x}\zeta,(\mathrm{t}^{-1}(\mathrm{D})+\varepsilon\zeta)\Lambda^{s}u\big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-{\varepsilon}\big(\Lambda^{s}\mathrm{t}(\mathrm{D})(u\partial_{x}u),(\mathrm{t}^{-1}(\mathrm{D})+\varepsilon\zeta)\Lambda^{s}u\big)_{L^{2}}+\frac{1}{2}\big(\Lambda^{s}u,(\partial_{t}\zeta)\Lambda^{s}u\big)_{L^{2}}.

We now note that the terms are similar to the ones in the proof of Proposition 3.4. in
[
84
]
. The only difference is the presence of the zero order differential operator
\sqrt{\mathrm{I}}(\mathrm{D})
in the definition of
\sqrt{\mathrm{k}}(\mathrm{D})
and
\sqrt{\mathrm{t}}(\mathrm{D})
. However, the final estimates still remain the same. In particular, the proof of Proposition 3.4 in
[
84
]
relies on

\displaystyle|\sqrt{\mathrm{k}}(\mathrm{D})f|_{H^{s}}

\displaystyle\leq C|f|_{H^{s+1}_{\mathrm{bo}}},

\displaystyle|\sqrt{\mathrm{t}}(\mathrm{D})f|_{H^{s}}

\displaystyle\leq C|f|_{H^{s}},

\displaystyle|[\Lambda^{s}\sqrt{\mathrm{k}}(\mathrm{D}),f]\partial_{x}g|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C|f|_{H^{s+1}_{\mathrm{bo}}}|g|_{H^{s+1}_{\mathrm{bo}}},

\displaystyle|[\Lambda^{s}\sqrt{\mathrm{t}}(\mathrm{D}),f]\partial_{x}g|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C|f|_{H^{s}}|g|_{H^{s}},

\displaystyle|\partial_{x}[\sqrt{\mathrm{t}}(\mathrm{D}),f]g|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C|f|_{H^{t_{0}+1}}|g|_{L^{2}},

which are provided in Propositions
B.2
and
B.5
in the Appendix. From these estimates one can follow
[
84
]
line by line to deduce the final result.

∎

Proof of Theorem
1.15
.

The proof is an application of the energy method where we use the estimates in Proposition
7.1
and Remark
7.2
. We refer the reader to
[
84
]
, Theorem 1.11. for a similar proof.
∎

8.
Proof of Theorem
1.18

For the derivation of (
1.1
), we follow the strategy given in
[
19
]
, where we formulated (
1.15
) in terms of the
(\zeta,v)
solving system (
1.24
). The convenience of this formulation is apparent since we can use the shallow water expansion of
\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{+}
derived by Emerald in
[
33
]
:

Proposition 8.1
.

Let
t_{0}\geq 1
,
s\geq 0
, and
\zeta\in H^{s+3}(\mathbb{R})
such that it satisfies (
1.16
). Then for all
\psi^{+}\in\dot{H}^{s+4}(\mathbb{R})
there holds,

(8.1)

|\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{+}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi^{+}-\partial_{x}\big(-\mu(1+{\varepsilon}\zeta)\mathrm{T}(\mathrm{D})\partial_{x}\psi^{+}\big)|_{H^{s}}\leq\mu^{2}{\varepsilon}M(s+3)|\partial_{x}\psi^{+}|_{H^{s+3}},

where
\mathrm{T}(\mathrm{D})=\frac{\tanh(\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|)}{\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|}
.

Finally, the last ingredient before we turn to the proof of Theorem
1.18
is an expansion of the interface operator defined by (
1.21
).

Proposition 8.2
.

Let
t_{0}\geq 1
,
s\geq 0
, and
\zeta\in H^{s+3}(\mathbb{R})
such that it satisfies (
1.16
). Then for all
\psi^{+}\in\dot{H}^{s+4}(\mathbb{R})
there holds,

(8.2)

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbf{H}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi^{+}-\big(-\tanh(\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|)\partial_{x}\psi^{+}\big)\Big|_{H^{s}}\leq{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}M(s+3)|\partial_{x}\psi^{+}|_{H^{s+3}}.

Remark 8.3
.

space

•

The expansion of
\mathbf{H}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]
is a fully dispersive version of the infinite depth expansion mentioned in
[
19
]
(see Remark
20
of this paper).

•

This expansion can be extended to higher dimensions and may be a useful tool to derive other systems and equations from the internal water waves equations in the case one layer is of infinite depth.

Proof of Proposition
8.2
.

The proof is divided into three steps and relies on making an approximate solution of
\Phi^{-}
solving (
1.22
).

Step 1.

Approximate solution for (
1.22
)
. First we make the transformation
\phi^{-}=\Phi^{-}\circ\Sigma^{-}
defined on
\mathcal{S}^{-}
:

\begin{cases}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}\cdot P(\Sigma^{-})\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}\phi^{-}=0\quad\text{in}\quad\mathcal{S}^{-}\\
\partial_{n}^{P^{-}}\phi^{-}|_{z=0}=\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi^{+},\quad\lim\limits_{z\rightarrow\infty}|\nabla^{\mu}_{x,z}\phi^{-}|=0,\end{cases}

where we recall from estimate (
2.19
) in the proof of Proposition
2.4
that

\|\Lambda^{s}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}\phi^{-}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{-})}\leq\sqrt{\mu}M(s+2)|\psi^{+}|_{\dot{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}.

To construct an approximation, we can verify that

\displaystyle\phi_{\mathrm{app}}^{-}=-\tanh(\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|)\mathrm{e}^{-z\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|}\psi^{+},

solves

(8.3)

\begin{cases}(\mu\partial_{x}^{2}+\partial_{z}^{2})\phi_{\mathrm{app}}^{-}=0,\\
\partial_{z}\phi_{\mathrm{app}}^{-}\>{}_{|_{z=0}}=-\mu\partial_{x}^{2}\mathrm{T}(\mathrm{D})\psi^{+},\end{cases}

and satisfies the decay estimates (
2.14
) by using Plancherel’s identity. Defining the difference
u=\phi^{-}-\phi_{\mathrm{app}}^{-}
, then we have by construction that

(8.4)

\begin{cases}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}\cdot P(\Sigma^{-})\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u={\varepsilon}\mu r_{1}\quad\text{in}\quad\mathcal{S}^{-}\\
\partial_{n}^{P^{-}}u|_{z=0}=\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi^{+}+\mu\partial_{x}^{2}\mathrm{T}(\mathrm{D})\psi^{+}+{\varepsilon}\mu r_{2},\quad\lim\limits_{z\rightarrow\infty}|\nabla^{\mu}_{x,z}u|=0,\end{cases}

where
r_{1}
reads

\displaystyle r_{1}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}\mu}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}\cdot P(\Sigma^{-})\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}\phi_{\mathrm{app}}^{-}

\displaystyle=\partial_{x}\big((\partial_{x}\zeta)\partial_{z}\phi_{\mathrm{app}}^{-}\big)+\partial_{z}\Big((\partial_{x}\zeta)\partial_{x}\phi_{\mathrm{app}}^{-}-{\varepsilon}(\partial_{x}\zeta)^{2}\partial_{z}\phi_{\mathrm{app}}^{-}\Big),

and the second rest is given by

\displaystyle r_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}\mu}\Big(\mathbf{e}_{z}\cdot P(\Sigma^{-})\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}\phi_{\mathrm{app}}^{-}-\partial_{z}\phi_{\mathrm{app}}^{-}\Big)|_{z=0}

\displaystyle=(\partial_{x}\zeta)\partial_{x}\tanh(\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|)\psi^{+}+{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}(\partial_{x}\zeta)^{2}|\mathrm{D}|\tanh(\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|)\psi^{+}.

Step 2.

Estimate on
\mathrm{LHS}_{\eqref{exp H}}
. By construction we have that

\mathbf{H}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi^{+}-\big(-\tanh(\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|)\partial_{x}\psi^{+}\big)=\partial_{x}u_{0},

where we let
u_{0}:=u|_{z=0}
. Then by trace estimate (
2.15
) we obtain the bound

(8.5)

\displaystyle|\partial_{x}u_{0}|_{H^{s}}\leq||\mathrm{D}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda^{s}u_{0}|_{\mathring{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\leq\mu^{-\frac{1}{4}}\||\mathrm{D}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda^{s}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{-})}.

To conclude, we need an estimate on the right hand side of this inequality.

Step 3.

Estimate on
|\mathrm{D}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda^{s}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u
in
L^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{-})
.
We let
\mathrm{m}(\mathrm{D})=|\mathrm{D}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda^{s}
and observe by the coercivity of
P(\Sigma^{-})
, given by (
1.40
), that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+M}\|\mathrm{m}(\mathrm{D})\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{-})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathcal{S}^{-}}P(\Sigma^{-})\mathrm{m}(\mathrm{D})\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u\cdot\mathrm{m}(\mathrm{D})\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u\>\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{dz}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{S}^{-}}\mathrm{m}(\mathrm{D})P(\Sigma^{-})\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u\cdot\mathrm{m}(\mathrm{D})\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u\>\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{dz}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\int_{\mathcal{S}^{-}}[\mathrm{m}(\mathrm{D}),P(\Sigma^{-})]\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u\cdot\mathrm{m}(\mathrm{D})\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u\>\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{dz}

\displaystyle=I_{1}+I_{2}.

For the estimate of
I_{1}
, we use the Divergence Theorem together with (
8.4
) to obtain the relation

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}(\mathrm{m}(\mathrm{D})\partial_{n}^{P^{-}}u|_{z=0})\mathrm{m}(\mathrm{D})u_{0}\>\mathrm{d}x-\varepsilon\mu\int_{\mathcal{S}^{-}}\mathrm{m}(\mathrm{D})r_{1}\>\mathrm{m}(\mathrm{D})u\>\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=I_{1}^{1}+I_{1}^{2}.

To estimate
I_{1}^{1}
, we use Plancherel’s identity, Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, trace estimate (
2.15
), estimate (
8.1
) to approximate
\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}
, and basic product estimates to treat
r_{2}
:
6
6

6

The term
I_{1}^{1}
gives the main restriction on the precision of the parameters and the regularity of
(\zeta,\psi^{+})
.

\displaystyle|I_{1}^{1}|

\displaystyle=\Big|\int(\Lambda^{s}\partial_{n}^{P^{-}}u|_{z=0})|\mathrm{D}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{m}(\mathrm{D})u_{0}\>\mathrm{d}x\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\big(|\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi^{+}+\mu\partial_{x}^{2}\mathrm{T}(\mathrm{D})\psi^{+}|_{H^{s}}+{\varepsilon}\mu|r_{2}|_{H^{s}}\big)|\mathrm{m}(\mathrm{D})u_{0}|_{\mathring{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}\mu^{\frac{3}{4}}M(s+3)|\partial_{x}\psi^{+}|_{H^{s+3}}\|\mathrm{m}(\mathrm{D})\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{-})}.

To estimate
I_{1}^{2}
, we use the definition of
r_{1}
, Plancherel’s identity and the Hilbert transform identity
\partial_{x}=-\mathcal{H}|\mathrm{D}|
to make the decomposition:

\displaystyle I_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\mu\int_{\mathcal{S}^{-}}\Lambda^{s}\mathcal{H}|\mathrm{D}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\big((\partial_{x}\zeta)\partial_{z}\phi_{\mathrm{app}}^{-}\big)\>|\mathrm{D}|^{1}\mathrm{m}(\mathrm{D})u\>\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-\varepsilon\mu\int_{\mathcal{S}^{-}}\mathrm{m}(\mathrm{D})\partial_{z}\Big((\partial_{x}\zeta)\partial_{x}\phi_{\mathrm{app}}^{-}-{\varepsilon}(\partial_{x}\zeta)^{2}\partial_{z}\phi_{\mathrm{app}}^{-}\Big)\>\mathrm{m}(\mathrm{D})u\>\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=I_{1}^{2,1}+I_{1}^{2,2}.

To estimate
I_{1}^{2,1}
, we use Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, the definition
\partial_{z}\phi_{\mathrm{app}}^{-}
to gain
\sqrt{\mu}
and the smoothing estimate:

\displaystyle\|\tanh(\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|)|\mathrm{D}|e^{-z\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|}\psi^{+}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{-})}

\displaystyle\leq\mu^{-\frac{1}{4}}||\mathrm{D}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\tanh(\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|)\psi^{+}|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}||\mathrm{D}|\frac{\tanh(\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|)}{\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|}\psi^{+}|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}

(8.6)

\displaystyle\leq\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}|\partial_{x}\psi^{+}|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}},

to deduce the bound

\displaystyle|I_{2}^{1}|\leq\varepsilon\mu M(s+2)|\partial_{x}\psi^{+}|_{H^{s+1}}\|\mathrm{m}(\mathrm{D})\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{-})}.

On the other hand, to estimate
I_{1}^{2,2}
we also integrate by parts and the trace estimate (
2.15
) to find that

\displaystyle|I_{2}^{2}|

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\mu\Big|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\Lambda^{s}\Big((\partial_{x}\zeta)\partial_{x}\phi_{\mathrm{app}}^{-}-{\varepsilon}(\partial_{x}\zeta)^{2}\partial_{z}\phi_{\mathrm{app}}^{-}\Big)|_{z=0}|\mathrm{D}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{m}(\mathrm{D})u_{0}\>\mathrm{d}x\Big|

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\varepsilon\mu\Big|\int_{\mathcal{S}^{-}}|\mathrm{D}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda^{s}\Big((\partial_{x}\zeta)\partial_{x}\phi_{\mathrm{app}}^{-}-{\varepsilon}(\partial_{x}\zeta)^{2}\partial_{z}\phi_{\mathrm{app}}^{-}\Big)\mathrm{m}(\mathrm{D})\partial_{z}u\>\mathrm{d}x\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{3}{4}}M(s+2)|\partial_{x}\psi^{+}|_{H^{s+1}}\|\mathrm{m}(\mathrm{D})\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{-})}.

Gathering these estimates yields the following bound on
I_{1}
:

|I_{1}|\leq\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{3}{4}}M(s+3)|\partial_{x}\psi^{+}|_{H^{s+1}}\|\mathrm{m}(\mathrm{D})\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{-})}.

Lastly, to estimate
I_{2}
we use Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, (
2.19
), (
8.6
), and that the nonzero terms in the commutator
[\mathrm{m}(\mathrm{D}),P(\Sigma^{-})]
is of order
\varepsilon\sqrt{\mu}
:

\displaystyle|I_{2}|

\displaystyle\leq\|[\mathrm{m}(\mathrm{D}),P(\Sigma^{-})]\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{-})}\|\mathrm{m}(\mathrm{D})\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{-})}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\sqrt{\mu}M(s+2)\big(\|\Lambda^{s+\frac{1}{2}}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}\phi^{-}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{-})}+\|\Lambda^{s+\frac{1}{2}}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}\phi^{-}_{\mathrm{app}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{-})}\big)\|\mathrm{m}(\mathrm{D})\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{-})}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\mu M(s+3)|\partial_{x}\psi^{+}|_{H^{s+2}}\|\mathrm{m}(\mathrm{D})\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{-})},

which implies that

\displaystyle\||\mathrm{D}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda^{s}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}u\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{-})}\leq\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{3}{4}}M(s+3)|\partial_{x}\psi^{+}|_{H^{s+3}},

and from estimate (
8.5
) we make the conclusion:

|\partial_{x}u_{0}|_{H^{s}}\leq\varepsilon\sqrt{\mu}M(s+3)|\partial_{x}\psi^{+}|_{H^{s+3}}.

∎

Having these expansions at hand, we can turn to the main proofs of the section.

Proof of Theorem
1.18
.

We first observe that we can have bound on
v=\partial_{x}\psi
in terms of the intial data. Indeed, using the definition of
\psi_{(\alpha)}
with (
B.14
) and (
A.42
) yields,

\displaystyle|\partial_{x}\psi|_{H^{N-\frac{1}{2}}}\leq|\psi|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{N+\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{2}\>:\>|\alpha|\leq N}|\psi_{(\alpha)}|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{\frac{1}{2}}}+{\varepsilon}|\underline{w}\zeta_{(\alpha)}|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{2}\>:\>|\alpha|\leq N}|\psi_{(\alpha)}|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{\frac{1}{2}}}+M|\psi|_{\dot{H}^{2}}|\zeta_{(\alpha)}|_{H^{1}_{\mathrm{bo}}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U})).

since
\varepsilon\leq\mathrm{bo}^{-\frac{1}{2}}
. Then Theorem
1.7
with estimate (
1.20
) implies

\sup_{t\in[0,{\varepsilon}^{-1}T]}\big(|\zeta|_{H^{N+1}_{\mathrm{bo}}}^{2}+|v|_{H^{N-\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}\big)\leq C(\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}_{0}),{h^{-1}_{\min}},\mathfrak{d}(\mathbf{U}_{0})^{-1}).

With this estimate in mind, we now give the proof in two steps where we first derive (
1.
) in the sense of consistency.

Step 1.
To derive (
1.26
) we let
C:=C(\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}_{0}),{h^{-1}_{\min}},\mathfrak{d}(\mathbf{U}_{0})^{-1})
and
R
be some generic function satisfying

(8.7)

|R|_{H^{N-5}}\leq C.

Then we can simplify (
1.24
). In particular, for the second equation in (
1.15
), we first estimate the nonlinear terms. For
\mathcal{N}[{\varepsilon}\zeta,\psi^{\pm}]
we apply estimates (
B.14
), (
8.1
), and (
1.20
) to obtain,

|\mathcal{N}[{\varepsilon}\zeta,\psi^{\pm}]|_{H^{N-5}}\leq\mu C(\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}))\leq\mu C,

and for the term involving surface tension, we observe that

\displaystyle\Big|\partial_{x}\bigg(\partial_{x}\zeta\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+{\varepsilon}^{2}\mu(\partial_{x}\zeta)^{2}}}-1\Big)\bigg)\Big|_{H^{N-5}}\leq\varepsilon^{2}\mu C(\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}))\leq\varepsilon^{2}\mu C.

While the first equation in (
1.24
), we use (
8.1
) to deduce that

(8.8)

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\zeta+\partial_{x}^{2}\mathrm{T}(\mathrm{D})\psi^{+}+{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}(\zeta\partial_{x}\mathrm{T}(\mathrm{D})\psi^{+})={\varepsilon}\mu R\\
\partial_{t}v+\big(1-\mathrm{bo}^{-1}\partial_{x}^{2}\big)\partial_{x}\zeta+\frac{{\varepsilon}}{2}\partial_{x}\big((\partial_{x}\psi^{+})^{2}-\gamma(\mathbf{H}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi^{+})^{2}\big)=\mu{\varepsilon}R.\end{cases}

Then to relate the equation to
v
, we observe by (
8.2
):

\displaystyle\partial_{x}\psi^{+}

\displaystyle=\gamma\mathbf{H}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi^{+}+v

\displaystyle=-\gamma\tanh(\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|)\partial_{x}\psi^{+}+v+{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}R,

so that

\partial_{x}\psi^{+}=(1+\gamma\tanh(\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|))^{-1}v+{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}R.

From this relation, we can express the first equation in terms of
\zeta
and
v
by using the expansions of
\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}
given by (
8.1
),
\mathrm{T}(\mathrm{D})
given by (
B.7
),
\mathrm{I}(\mathrm{D})=(1+\gamma\tanh(\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|))^{-1}
given by (
B.8
), and recalled here:

(8.9)

|(\mathrm{T}(\mathrm{D})-1)f|_{H^{s}}\leq\mu C|\partial_{x}^{2}f|_{H^{s}},\quad|(\mathrm{I}(\mathrm{D})-1)f|_{H^{s}}\leq\sqrt{\mu}C|\partial_{x}f|_{H^{s}}.

Indeed, from these observations there holds,

\displaystyle\partial_{x}^{2}\mathrm{T}(\mathrm{D})\psi^{+}+{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}(\zeta\partial_{x}\mathrm{T}(\mathrm{D})\psi^{+})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{I}(\mathrm{D})\mathrm{T}(\mathrm{D})\partial_{x}v+{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}(\zeta v)+{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}R.

For the second equation, we have that

(\partial_{x}\psi^{+})^{2}=v^{2}+\sqrt{\mu}R,

and

\displaystyle(\mathbf{H}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi^{+})^{2}

\displaystyle=\mu R.

To summarize the approximations so far, we have the following system:

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\zeta+\mathrm{I}(\mathrm{D})\mathrm{T}(\mathrm{D})\partial_{x}v+{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}(\zeta v)={\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}R\\
\partial_{t}v+\big(1-\mathrm{bo}^{-1}\partial_{x}^{2}\big)\partial_{x}\zeta+{\varepsilon}v\partial_{x}v={\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}R.\end{cases}

Next, we ease the notation by defining

\mathrm{t}(\mathrm{D})=\mathrm{I}(\mathrm{D})\mathrm{T}(\mathrm{D}),\quad\mathrm{k}(\mathrm{D})=\mathrm{t}(\mathrm{D})\big(1-\mathrm{bo}^{-1}\partial_{x}^{2}\big),

and

u=\mathrm{t}(\mathrm{D})v.

Then use (
8.9
) to find the desired system

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\zeta+\partial_{x}u+{\varepsilon}\mathrm{t}(\mathrm{D})\partial_{x}(\zeta u)={\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}R\\
\partial_{t}u+\mathrm{k}(\mathrm{D})\partial_{x}\zeta+\frac{{\varepsilon}}{2}\mathrm{t}(\mathrm{D})\partial_{x}(u)^{2}={\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}R.\end{cases}

Step 2.
To derive (
1.27
) we can make an approximation
u^{\text{\tiny wB}}
in terms of
\zeta^{\text{\tiny wB}}
at order
\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon({\varepsilon}+\sqrt{\mu}+\mathrm{bo}^{-1}))
solving (
1.27
). Then we will show that this solution is consistent with the weakly dispersive Benjamin system (
1.26
). The proof is given in two steps, where we first make formal computations and then prove the consistency once we have constructed
u^{\text{\tiny wB}}
.

Step 2.1.

Formal derivation:

To construct the lowest order approximation of
u^{\text{\tiny wB}}
in terms of the solution
\zeta^{\text{\tiny wB}}
, we first consider system (
1.26
) at order
{\varepsilon}
:

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\zeta^{\text{\tiny wB}}+\partial_{x}u^{\text{\tiny wB}}=\mathcal{O}({\varepsilon})\\
\partial_{t}u^{\text{\tiny wB}}+\mathrm{k}(\mathrm{D})\partial_{x}\zeta^{\text{\tiny wB}}=\mathcal{O}({\varepsilon}).\end{cases}

We observe
u^{\text{\tiny wB}}
is equal to
\sqrt{\mathrm{k}}(\mathrm{D})\zeta^{\text{\tiny wB}}
at first order. Then having an approximation at first order we can now make a correction at higher order:

u^{\text{\tiny wB}}=\sqrt{\mathrm{k}}(\mathrm{D})\zeta^{\text{\tiny wB}}+{\varepsilon}A,

for some function
A
depending on the solution
\zeta^{\text{\tiny wB}}
. In particular, we construct
A
by plugging the ansatz into (
1.26
) and using (
B.11
):

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\zeta^{\text{\tiny wB}}+\sqrt{\mathrm{k}}(\mathrm{D})\partial_{x}\zeta^{\text{\tiny wB}}+{\varepsilon}\big(\partial_{x}A+\partial_{x}((\zeta^{\text{\tiny wB}})^{2})\big)=\mathcal{O}\big(\varepsilon(\varepsilon+\sqrt{\mu}+\mathrm{bo}^{-1})\big)\\
\partial_{t}\zeta^{\text{\tiny wB}}+\sqrt{\mathrm{k}}(\mathrm{D})\partial_{x}\zeta^{\text{\tiny wB}}+{\varepsilon}\big(\partial_{t}A+\zeta^{\text{\tiny wB}}\partial_{x}\zeta^{\text{\tiny wB}}\big)=\mathcal{O}\big(\varepsilon(\varepsilon+\sqrt{\mu}+\mathrm{bo}^{-1})\big).\end{cases}

Now using the nonlocal transport equations to see that
\partial_{x}A=-\partial_{t}A
(up to an order
\mathcal{O}({\varepsilon}+\sqrt{\mu})
), we find that

2\partial_{x}A=-\zeta^{\text{\tiny wB}}\partial_{x}\zeta^{\text{\tiny wB}}.

We therefore let
v^{\text{\tiny wB}}
be given by

(8.10)

v^{\text{\tiny wB}}=\mathrm{t}^{-1}(\mathrm{D})u^{\text{\tiny wB}}=\mathrm{t}^{-1}(\mathrm{D})\big(\sqrt{\mathrm{k}}(\mathrm{D})\zeta^{\text{\tiny wB}}-\frac{{\varepsilon}}{4}(\zeta^{\text{\tiny wB}})^{2}\big).

Step 2.2.

Rigorous derivation:
For any
\zeta_{0}\in H^{N+1}_{\mathrm{bo}}({\mathbb{R}})
we have that

v_{0}=\mathrm{t}^{-1}(\mathrm{D})\big(\sqrt{\mathrm{k}}(\mathrm{D})\zeta_{0}-\frac{{\varepsilon}}{4}(\zeta_{0})^{2}\big)\in H^{N-\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}}).

Indeed, using Plancherel’s identity, the algebra property of
H^{N-\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
, we observe that

\displaystyle|v_{0}|_{H^{N-\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq|\zeta_{0}|_{H^{N+\frac{1}{2}}_{\mathrm{bo}}}+{\varepsilon}\Big|\frac{\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|}{\tanh(\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|)}\zeta_{0}^{2}\Big|_{H^{N-\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim|\zeta_{0}|_{H^{N+\frac{1}{2}}_{\mathrm{bo}}}+{\varepsilon}|\zeta_{0}|_{H^{N-\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}+{\varepsilon}|\partial_{x}(\zeta_{0}^{2})|_{H^{N-\frac{1}{2}}}

(8.11)

\displaystyle\leq|\zeta_{0}|_{H^{N+1}_{\mathrm{bo}}}^{2},

where we used that
{\varepsilon}\leq\mathrm{bo}^{-\frac{1}{2}}
. Moreover, there is a time
T_{1}>0
and a unique solution
7
7

7

See Remark
1.17
.

\zeta^{\text{\tiny wB}}\in C\big([0,{\varepsilon}^{-1}T_{1}]\>:\>H^{N+1}_{\mathrm{bo}}({\mathbb{R}})\big)
associated to
\zeta_{0}
solving the equation

\partial_{t}\zeta^{\text{\tiny wB}}+\sqrt{\mathrm{k}}(\mathrm{D})\partial_{x}\zeta^{\text{\tiny wB}}+\frac{3{\varepsilon}}{2}\zeta^{\text{\tiny wB}}\partial_{x}\zeta^{\text{\tiny wB}}=0

and satisfying

(8.12)

\sup\limits_{t\in[0,{\varepsilon}^{-1}T]}|\zeta^{\text{\tiny{wB}}}|_{H^{N+1}_{\mathrm{bo}}}\leq|\zeta_{0}|_{H^{N+1}_{\mathrm{bo}}}.

Moreover, we can define
v^{\text{\tiny wB}}\in C\big([0,{\varepsilon}^{-1}T_{1}]\>:\>H^{N-\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})\big)
by (
8.10
) and
u^{\text{\tiny wB}}=\mathrm{t}(\mathrm{D})v^{\text{\tiny wB}}
in the same space. Then by using estimate (
B.11
) we deduce from (
8.12
) that

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\zeta^{\text{\tiny wB}}+\partial_{x}u^{\text{\tiny wB}}+{\varepsilon}\mathrm{t}(\mathrm{D})\partial_{x}(\zeta^{\text{\tiny wB}}u^{\text{\tiny wB}})=\varepsilon(\varepsilon+\sqrt{\mu}+\mathrm{bo}^{-1})R\\
\partial_{t}u^{\text{\tiny wB}}+\sqrt{\mathrm{k}}(\mathrm{D})\partial_{x}\zeta^{\text{\tiny wB}}+\frac{{\varepsilon}}{2}\mathrm{t}(\mathrm{D})\partial_{{x}}\big(u^{\text{\tiny wB}}\big)^{2}=\varepsilon(\varepsilon+\sqrt{\mu}+\mathrm{bo}^{-1})R,\end{cases}

for
R
satisfying (
8.7
) and concludes the proof of this step.

Step 3.
For the consistency result in the case of the Benjamin equation we use (
B.12
):

\Big|\big(\sqrt{k}(\mathrm{D})-(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|-\frac{\partial_{x}^{2}}{2\mathrm{bo}})\big)f\Big|_{H^{s}}\lesssim(\mu+\frac{\sqrt{\mu}}{\mathrm{bo}})|\partial_{x}^{2}f|_{H^{s+1}}.

Step 4.
For the consistency result in the case of the BO equation we also neglect the surface tension effects.

∎

9.
Proof of Theorem
1.20
and Corollary
1.22

Proof of Theorem
1.20
.

First, we let
N\geq 8
and take initial data
(\zeta_{0},\psi_{0})
satisfying the assumptions of Theorem
1.7
. Then using (
1.30
), we can define the solutions of the internal water waves equations (
1.24
) with variables

(\zeta,v)\in C([0,{\varepsilon}^{-1}T_{1}];H^{N+1}_{\mathrm{bo}}({\mathbb{R}})\times H^{N-\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})),

from the data
(\zeta_{0},\partial_{x}\psi_{0})\in H^{N+1}_{\mathrm{bo}}({\mathbb{R}})\times H^{N-\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
for some
T_{1}>0
. Next, we use Theorem
1.18
and the matrix notation (
7.2
) to say that on the same time interval the functions

u=\mathrm{t}(\mathrm{D})v\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbf{U}=(\zeta,u)^{T},

solves

\partial_{t}\mathbf{U}+\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{U})\mathbf{U}=\varepsilon\sqrt{\mu}(R,R)^{T},

for any
t\in[0,{\varepsilon}^{-1}T_{1}]
