and where the rest satisfies

(9.1)

|R|_{H^{N-5}}\leq C,

for
C=C(\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}_{0}),{h^{-1}_{\min}},\mathfrak{d}(\mathbf{U}_{0})^{-1})>0
nondecreasing function of its argument. We will now use this to prove estimates (
1.32
), (
1.33
), (
1.34
), and (
1.35
) in four separate steps.

Step 1.

Proof of estimate (
1.32
)
. We take the same data
(\zeta_{0},v_{0})
with
v_{0}=\partial_{x}\psi_{0}
, we by Plancherel’s identity we observe that

|u_{0}|_{H^{N-\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}+\sqrt{\mu}|u_{0}|_{\mathring{H}^{N}}^{2}=|\mathrm{t}(\mathrm{D})v_{0}|_{H^{N-\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}+\sqrt{\mu}||\mathrm{D}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{t}(\mathrm{D})v_{0}|_{H^{N-\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}\leq|v_{0}|_{H^{N-\frac{1}{2}}}^{2},

and (
1.30
) implies
(\zeta_{0},u_{0})\in X_{\mu,\mathrm{bo}}^{N-\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
. We may therefore apply Theorem
1.15
to deduce the existence of
T_{2}>0
such that

\mathbf{U}^{\text{\tiny wBs}}=(\zeta^{\text{\tiny wBs}},u^{\text{\tiny wBs}})\in C([0,{\varepsilon}^{-1}T_{2}];X_{\mu,\mathrm{bo}}^{N-\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})),

solves system (
7.1
):

\partial_{t}\mathbf{U}^{\text{\tiny wBs}}+\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{U}^{\text{\tiny wBs}})\mathbf{U}^{\text{\tiny wBs}}=\mathbf{0},

for any
t\in[0,{\varepsilon}^{-1}T_{2}]
. We may now take the difference between the two solutions

\mathbf{W}=(\eta,w)^{T}=\mathbf{U}-\mathbf{U}^{\text{\tiny wBs}},

to find that

\partial_{t}\mathbf{W}+\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{U})\mathbf{W}=\mathbf{F}+\varepsilon\sqrt{\mu}(R,R)^{T},

for any
t\in[0,{\varepsilon}^{-1}\min\{T_{1},T_{2}\}]
where
\mathbf{F}
is defined by (
7.5
). Then using the estimate (
7.10
) with
N-6>\frac{3}{2}
and adding the rest term we find that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\tilde{E}_{\text{\tiny{wBs}}}^{N-6}(\mathbf{W})

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\sqrt{\mu}|\big(\mathcal{Q}(\mathbf{U}^{\text{\tiny wBs}})\Lambda^{N-6}(R,R)^{T},\Lambda^{N-6}\mathbf{W}\big)_{L^{2}}|+\tilde{K}(N-5)\tilde{E}_{\text{\tiny{wBs}}}^{N-6}(\mathbf{W}).

where the energy is defined by (
7.6
). Furthermore, by definition of
\mathcal{Q}(\mathbf{U}_{1})
, the Hölder inequality, the Sobolev embedding, (
7.11
), and (
9.1
) we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\tilde{E}_{\text{\tiny{wBs}}}^{N-6}(\mathbf{W})

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\sqrt{\mu}|R|_{H^{N-5}}(\tilde{E}_{\text{\tiny{wBs}}}^{N-6}(\mathbf{W}))^{\frac{1}{2}}+{\varepsilon}\tilde{K}(N-5)\tilde{E}_{\text{\tiny{wBs}}}^{N-6}(\mathbf{W})

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\sqrt{\mu}C(\tilde{E}_{\text{\tiny{wBs}}}^{N-6}(\mathbf{W}))^{\frac{1}{2}}+{\varepsilon}\tilde{K}(N-5)\tilde{E}_{\text{\tiny{wBs}}}^{N-6}(\mathbf{W}).

Then Grönwall’s inequality, (
7.11
), (
1.29
), and (
1.30
) yields

|(\eta,w)|_{X_{\mu,\mathrm{bo}}^{N-6}}\leq\varepsilon\sqrt{\mu}tCe^{{\varepsilon}\tilde{K}(N-5)t}.

Finally, we observe that

{\varepsilon}\tilde{K}(N-5)t\leq C\min\{T_{1},T_{2}\},

and from the rough estimate

|v-v^{\text{\tiny wBs}}|_{H^{N-7}}=|\mathrm{t}(\mathrm{D})^{-1}w|_{H^{N-7}}\leq|w|_{H^{N-6}},

that there holds,

\displaystyle|(\zeta-\zeta^{\text{\tiny wBs}},v-v^{\text{\tiny wBs}})|_{L^{\infty}([0,t];H^{N-7}\times H^{N-7})}

\displaystyle\leq|(\eta,w)|_{L^{\infty}([0,t]:X^{N-6}_{\mu,\mathrm{bo}})}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\sqrt{\mu}tC,

for all
t\in[0,{\varepsilon}^{-1}\min\{T_{1},T_{2}\}]
. This completes the proof of estimate (
1.32
).

Step 2.

Proof of estimate (
1.33
)
.
For
\zeta_{0}\in H^{N+1}_{\mathrm{bo}}({\mathbb{R}}))
there exist a time
T_{3}>0
such that

\zeta^{\text{\tiny wB}}\in C([0,{\varepsilon}^{-1}T_{3}];H^{N+1}_{\mathrm{bo}}({\mathbb{R}})),

solves (
1.27
) and from it we define

v^{\text{\tiny wB}}=\mathrm{t}^{-1}(\mathrm{D})u^{\text{\tiny wB}}=\mathrm{t}^{-1}(\mathrm{D})\big(\sqrt{\mathrm{k}}(\mathrm{D})\zeta^{\text{\tiny wB}}-\frac{{\varepsilon}}{4}(\zeta^{\text{\tiny wB}})^{2}\big).

Moreover, by estimate (
8.11
) and (
8.12
) we have that

\sup\limits_{t\in[0,{\varepsilon}^{-1}T_{3}]}|(\zeta^{\text{\tiny wB}},v^{\text{\tiny wB}})|_{H^{N+1}_{\mathrm{bo}}\times H^{N-\frac{1}{2}}}\leq C(|\zeta_{0}|_{H^{N+1}_{\mathrm{bo}}}).

Then if we define
\mathbf{U}^{\text{\tiny wB}}=(\zeta^{\text{\tiny wB}},u^{\text{\tiny wB}})^{T}
, where
u^{\text{\tiny wB}}=\mathrm{t}(\mathrm{D})v^{\text{\tiny wB}}
, and then use Theorem
1.18
and argue as above to find that

\displaystyle|\mathbf{U}-\mathbf{U}^{\text{\tiny wB}}|_{L^{\infty}([0,t];H^{N-6}\times H^{N-6})}

\displaystyle\leq|\mathbf{U}-\mathbf{U}^{\text{\tiny wBs}}|_{L^{\infty}([0,t];H^{N-6}\times H^{N-6})}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+|\mathbf{U}^{\text{\tiny wBs}}-\mathbf{U}^{\text{\tiny wB}}|_{L^{\infty}([0,t];H^{N-6}\times H^{N-6})}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon({\varepsilon}+\sqrt{\mu}+\mathrm{bo}^{-1})tC,

for all
t\in[0,{\varepsilon}^{-1}\min\{T_{1},T_{2},T_{3}\}]
.

Step 3.

Proof of estimate (
1.34
)
. There exist a time
T_{4}>0
such that
\zeta^{\text{\tiny B}}\in C([0,{\varepsilon}^{-1}T_{4}];H^{N+1}_{\mathrm{bo}}({\mathbb{R}}))
solves (
1.3
) that is bounded by its intial data. Moreover, by Theorem
1.18
the solution satisfies

\partial_{t}\zeta^{\text{\tiny B}}+\sqrt{k}(\mathrm{D})\partial_{x}\zeta^{\text{\tiny B}}+\frac{3{\varepsilon}}{2}\zeta^{\text{\tiny B}}\partial_{x}\zeta^{\text{\tiny B}}=(\mu+\frac{\sqrt{\mu}}{\mathrm{bo}})R.

Then if we define the difference
\tilde{\eta}=(\zeta^{\text{\tiny wB}}-\zeta^{\text{\tiny B}})
it is straightforward to deduce that

\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}|\tilde{\eta}|_{H^{N-5}}^{2}\leq{\varepsilon}(|\zeta^{\text{\tiny wB}}|_{H^{N-4}}+|\zeta^{\text{\tiny B}}|_{H^{N-4}})|\tilde{\eta}|_{H^{N-5}}^{2}+(\mu+\frac{\sqrt{\mu}}{\mathrm{bo}})|R|_{H^{N-5}}|\tilde{\eta}|_{H^{N-5}}.

Then by Grönwall’s inequality, the bound on the solution, and (
9.1
) we find that

|\zeta^{\text{\tiny wB}}-\zeta^{\text{\tiny B}}|_{H^{N-5}}\leq(\mu+\frac{\sqrt{\mu}}{\mathrm{bo}})tC,

and the result is a direct consequence of the previous steps.

Step 4.

Proof of estimate (
1.35
)
. The proof is the same as in the previous step.

∎

Proof of Corollary
1.22
.

Since the well-posedness of the ILW equation is well-known for regular initial data we are simply left to prove a convergence estimate between its solutions and the ones of BO. However, comparing the linear terms we have that

\Big||\mathrm{D}|(1-\coth(\sqrt{\mu^{-}}|\mathrm{D}|))f\Big|_{L^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu^{-}}}|f|_{L^{2}},

using Lemma 2.3. given in
[
23
]
. Then we can deduce an estimate between the BO equation and the ILW equation and apply Theorem
1.20
to conclude.
∎

Appendix A
Properties of the Dirichlet-Neumann operators

In this section we will give several results on
\mathcal{G}^{\pm}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]
and
\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]
. Then we will use these results to share some details on the proof Proposition
4.2
. But as we will shortly see, the main quantities in (
1.15
) can be estimated in terms of the principal unknown
(\zeta,\psi)
where the estimates are very similar to the ones in
[
65
]
. We will therefore give more details when there is a difference, and when the estimates are the same, we will simply refer to the results in
[
65
]
.

A.1.
Properties of
\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{+}

We start this section by giving a precise definition of the positive Dirichlet-Neumann operator
\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}
.

Definition A.1
.

Let
t_{0}>\frac{1}{2}
,
\psi^{+}\in\dot{H}_{\mu}^{\frac{3}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
, and
\zeta\in H^{t_{0}+2}({\mathbb{R}})
be such that (
1.16
) is satisfied. Let
\Phi^{+}
be the unique solution in
\dot{H}^{2}(\Omega_{t}^{+})
of the boundary value problem

\begin{cases}\Delta^{\mu}_{x,z}\Phi^{+}=0\hskip 36.98866pt\qquad\text{in}\quad\Omega_{t}^{+}\\
\Phi^{+}=\psi^{+}\hskip 72.2698pt\text{on}\quad z=\varepsilon\zeta\\
\partial_{z}\Phi^{+}=0\hskip 71.13188pt\text{on}\quad z=-1,\end{cases}

then
\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi^{+}\in H^{\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
is defined by

\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[\varepsilon\zeta]\psi^{+}=(\partial_{z}\Phi^{+}-\mu\varepsilon\partial_{x}\zeta\partial_{x}\Phi^{+}){|_{z=\varepsilon\zeta}}.

Remark A.2
.

Under the provisions of Definition
A.1
and let
\phi^{+}=\Phi\circ\Sigma^{+}
where
\Sigma^{+}
is the diffeomorphism from the fixed domain
\mathcal{S}^{+}
onto
\Omega^{+}_{t}
given in Definition
1.23
, then we have that

(A.1)

\begin{cases}\nabla^{\mu}_{x,z}\cdot P(\Sigma^{+})\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}\phi^{+}=0\hskip 19.91684pt\qquad\text{in}\quad\mathcal{S}^{+}\\
\phi^{+}=\psi^{+}\hskip 115.23373pt\text{on}\quad z=0,\\
\partial_{n}^{P^{+}}\phi^{+}=0\hskip 105.2751pt\text{on}\quad z=-1,\end{cases}

where

\displaystyle\partial_{n}^{P^{+}}=\mathbf{e}_{z}\cdot P(\Sigma^{+})\nabla^{\mu}_{x,z}.

Moreover, we have an equivalent expression of
\mathcal{G}^{+}[\zeta]\psi^{+}
given by

(A.2)

\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[\varepsilon\zeta]\psi^{+}=\partial_{n}^{P^{+}}\phi^{+}|_{z=0}.

Remark A.3
.

For
{\varepsilon}=0
we have that
\mathcal{G}^{+}
becomes

(A.3)

\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[0]\psi^{+}=\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|\tanh(\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|)\psi^{+}.

Next, we will state several results on the Dirichlet-Neumann operator that are taken from
[
66
,
65
]
.

Proposition A.4
.

Let
t_{0}>\frac{1}{2}
,
s\in[0,t_{0}+1]
and
\zeta\in H^{t_{0}+2}({\mathbb{R}})
be such that (
1.16
) is satisfied. Then we have the following properties:

1.

For
\psi^{+}\in\dot{H}^{s+{\frac{1}{2}}}_{\mu}({\mathbb{R}})
there is a (variational) solution of (
A.1
) satisfying the estimates

(A.4)

\sqrt{\mu}|\psi^{+}|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}\leq M\|\Lambda^{s}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}\phi^{+}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{+})},

and

(A.5)

\|\Lambda^{s}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}\phi^{+}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{+})}\leq\sqrt{\mu}M|\psi^{+}|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}.

2.

We may extend definition
A.1
for

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]:\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})\rightarrow H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}}),

where for all
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}\in\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
, there holds,

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\psi_{1}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[\varepsilon\zeta]\psi_{2}\>\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\mathcal{S}^{+}}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}\phi_{1}^{+}\cdot P^{+}(\Sigma^{+})\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}\phi_{2}^{+}\>\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}z.

3.

For all
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}\in\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
, there holds,

(A.6)

\displaystyle\big(\Lambda^{s}\psi_{1},\Lambda^{s}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[\varepsilon\zeta]\psi_{2}\big)_{L^{2}}\leq\mu M|\psi_{1}|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}|\psi_{2}|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}.

4.

For
\psi^{+}\in H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}({\mathbb{R}})
the following estimates hold

(A.7)

\displaystyle\forall s\in[0,t_{0}+\frac{3}{2}],\quad|\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[\varepsilon\zeta]\psi^{+}|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}\leq\mu^{\frac{3}{4}}M|\psi|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}},

(A.8)

\displaystyle\forall s\in[0,t_{0}+1],\>\quad|\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[\varepsilon\zeta]\psi^{+}|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}\leq\mu M|\psi|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+1}}.

5.

For all
\psi_{1}\in\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s-\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
and
\psi_{2}\in\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
, there holds

(A.9)

\big(\big[\Lambda^{s},\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[\varepsilon\zeta]\big]\psi_{1},\Lambda^{s}\psi_{2}\big)_{L^{2}}\leq\mu{\varepsilon}M|\psi_{1}|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}|\psi_{2}|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}.

6.

For all
V\in H^{t_{0}+1}({\mathbb{R}})
and
\psi^{+}\in H_{\mu}^{\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
there holds,

(A.10)

\big((V\partial_{x}\psi^{+}),\frac{1}{\mu}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{+}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi^{+}\big)_{L^{2}}\leq M|V|_{W^{1,\infty}}|\psi^{+}|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}^{2}.

Remark A.5
.

The regularity on
\zeta
here is not optimal. Specifically, the dependence on
|\zeta|_{H^{t_{0}+2}}
in the definition of
M
can be lowered in the estimates above. However, we do not give these estimates since we will in other instances require more regularity on the free surface (as an example, see estimate (
3.3
)).

Remark A.6
.

The last estimate (
A.9
) is taken from
[
65
]
(see equation number
(2.24)
). However,
(2.24)
has an
{\varepsilon}
missing due to a small typo and has been added here
[
67
]
.

The next result concerns the shape derivative of
\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{+}
. The result is found in
[
66
,
65
]
.

Proposition A.7
.

Let
t_{0}>\frac{1}{2}
,
s\in[0,t_{0}+1]
, and for any
\zeta\in H^{t_{0}+2}({\mathbb{R}})
satisfying (
1.16
) we have the following properties:

1.

For
\psi^{+}\in\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
the shape derivative of
\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]
at
\zeta\in H^{t_{0}+2}({\mathbb{R}})
in the direction of
h\in H^{t_{0}+2}({\mathbb{R}})
is given by the formula

(A.11)

\mathrm{d}_{\zeta}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](h)\psi^{+}=-{\varepsilon}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](h\underline{w}^{+})-{\varepsilon}\mu\partial_{x}(h\underline{V}^{+}).

2.

For all
0\leq s\leq t_{0}+1
,
j\geq 1
there holds

(A.12)

|\mathrm{d}^{j}_{\zeta}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](h)\psi^{+}|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}\leq{\varepsilon}^{j}\mu^{\frac{3}{4}}M\prod\limits_{m=1}^{j}|h_{m}|_{H^{\max\{s,t_{0}\}+1}}|\psi^{+}|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}.

3.

For all
0\leq s\leq t_{0}+\frac{1}{2}
,
j\geq 1
, and
\psi^{+}\in\dot{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}({\mathbb{R}})
, there holds,

(A.13)

|\mathrm{d}^{j}_{\zeta}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](h)\psi^{+}|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}\leq{\varepsilon}^{j}\mu M\prod\limits_{m=1}^{j}|h_{m}|_{H^{\max\{s+\frac{1}{2},t_{0}\}+1}}|\psi^{+}|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+1}}.

4.

For all
0\leq s\leq t_{0}+\frac{1}{2}
,
j\geq 1
,
\psi^{+}\in\dot{H}^{\max\{s,t_{0}\}+1}_{\mu}({\mathbb{R}})
, there holds,

(A.14)

|\mathrm{d}_{\zeta}^{j}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](h)\psi^{+}|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}\leq{\varepsilon}^{j}\mu M|h_{k}|_{H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}\prod\limits_{m\neq k}^{j}|h_{m}|_{H^{\max\{s,t_{0}\}+\frac{3}{2}}}|\psi|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{\max\{s,t_{0}\}+1}}.

5.

For all
0\leq s\leq t_{0}
,
j\geq 1
,
\psi^{+}\in\dot{H}^{\max\{s+\frac{1}{2},t_{0}\}+1}_{\mu}({\mathbb{R}})
, there holds,

(A.15)

|\mathrm{d}_{\zeta}^{j}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](h)\psi^{+}|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}\leq{\varepsilon}^{j}\mu M|h_{k}|_{H^{s+1}}\prod\limits_{m\neq k}^{j}|h_{m}|_{H^{\max\{s+\frac{1}{2},t_{0}\}+\frac{3}{2}}}|\psi^{+}|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{\max\{s+\frac{1}{2},t_{0}\}+1}}.

6.

For all
0\leq s\leq t_{0}+1
,
j\geq 1
,
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}\in\dot{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}({\mathbb{R}})
, there holds,

(A.16)

\big|\big(\Lambda^{s}\mathrm{d}^{j}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](\mathbf{h})\psi_{1},\Lambda^{s}\psi_{2}\big)_{L^{2}}\big|\leq{\varepsilon}^{j}\mu M\prod\limits_{m=1}^{j}|h_{m}|_{H^{\max\{s,t_{0}\}+1}}|\psi_{1}|_{\dot{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}|\psi_{2}|_{\dot{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}.

7.

For for all
0\leq s\leq t_{0}+\frac{1}{2}
,
j\geq 1
,
\psi_{1}\in\dot{H}^{\max\{s,t_{0}\}+1}_{\mu}({\mathbb{R}}),\psi_{2}\in\dot{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}({\mathbb{R}})
, there holds,

(A.17)

\big|\big(\Lambda^{s}\mathrm{d}^{j}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](\mathbf{h})\psi_{1},\Lambda^{s}\psi_{2}\big)_{L^{2}}\big|\leq{\varepsilon}^{j}\mu M|h_{l}|_{H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}\prod\limits_{m\neq l}^{j}|h_{m}|_{H^{\max\{s,t_{0}\}+\frac{3}{2}}}|\psi_{1}|_{\dot{H}^{\max\{s,t_{0}\}+1}}|\psi_{2}|_{\dot{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}.

A.2.
Properties of
\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{-}

In this section will define and give the main properties of the negative Dirichlet-Neumann operator.

Definition A.8
.

Let
t_{0}\geq 1
,
\psi\in\mathring{H}^{\frac{3}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
, and
\zeta\in H^{t_{0}+2}({\mathbb{R}})
be such that (
1.16
) is satisfied. Let
\Phi^{-}
be the unique solution in
\dot{H}^{2}(\Omega_{t}^{-})
of the boundary value problem

\begin{cases}\Delta_{x,z}^{\mu}\Phi^{-}=0\hskip 36.98866pt\qquad\text{in}\quad\Omega_{t}^{-}\\
\Phi^{-}=\psi\hskip 79.6678pt\text{on}\quad z={\varepsilon}\zeta,\end{cases}

then
\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi^{-}\in H^{\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
is defined by

(A.18)

\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi^{-}=(\partial_{z}\Phi^{-}-{\varepsilon}\mu\partial_{x}\zeta\partial_{x}\Phi^{-}){|_{z={\varepsilon}\zeta}}.

Remark A.9
.

As noted in Remark
3.50

(2)
in
[
66
]
, we can define the negative Dirichlet-Neumann operator by formula (
A.18
) (or formula (
A.21
) below) for
\psi^{-}\in\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
if
t_{0}>\frac{1}{2}
and
s\geq\max\{0,1-t_{0}\}
, where the restriction is a consequence of (
B.14
). We therefore put
t_{0}\geq 1
for simplicity.

Remark A.10
.

The scaling for
\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{-}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]
is different from the one used in
[
66
]
, where the author used the scaling natural for infinite depth. In that case one would change
\mu=1
and
{\varepsilon}
by
\epsilon=\frac{a}{\lambda}
. In our case, the internal water wave model depends on both scales and is explained in detail in Subsection
1.2.1
.

Remark A.11
.

For
{\varepsilon}\zeta=0
we have that
\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}
becomes

(A.19)

\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{-}[0]\psi^{-}=-\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|\psi^{-}.

Remark A.12
.

Under the provisions of Definition
A.8
and let
\phi^{-}=\Phi\circ\Sigma^{-}
where
\Sigma^{+}
is the diffeomorphism from the fixed domain
\mathcal{S}^{-}
onto
\Omega^{-}_{t}
given in Definition
1.23
, then we have that

(A.20)

\begin{cases}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}\cdot P(\Sigma^{-})\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}\phi^{-}=0\hskip 19.91684pt\qquad\text{in}\quad\mathcal{S}^{-}\\
\phi^{-}=\psi\hskip 122.34692pt\text{on}\quad z=0,\end{cases}

and we have an equivalent expression of
\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi^{-}
given by

(A.21)

\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi^{-}=\partial_{n}^{P^{-}}\phi^{-}|_{z=0},

where
\partial_{n}^{P^{-}}=\mathbf{e}_{z}\cdot P(\Sigma^{-})\nabla^{\mu}_{x,z}
.

Here we use the results in
[
66
]
adapted to the current scaling.

Proposition A.13
.

Let
t_{0}\geq 1
,
s\in[0,t_{0}+1]
and
\zeta\in H^{t_{0}+2}({\mathbb{R}})
be such that (
1.16
) is satisfied. We have the following properties:

1.

For
\psi\in\mathring{H}^{s+{\frac{1}{2}}}({\mathbb{R}})
there is a (variational) solution of (
A.1
) satisfying the estimate

(A.22)

\ |\psi^{-}|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}\leq\mu^{-\frac{1}{4}}M\|\Lambda^{s}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}\phi^{-}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{-})},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

and

(A.23)

\|\Lambda^{s}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}\phi^{-}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{-})}\leq\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}M|\psi^{-}|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}.

2.

By remark
A.9
we may extend Definition
A.8
for

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]:\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})\rightarrow H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}}),

where for all
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}\in\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
, there holds,

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\psi_{1}\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi_{2}\>\mathrm{d}x=-\int_{\mathcal{S}^{-}}\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}\phi_{1}\cdot P^{-}(\Sigma^{-})\nabla_{x,z}^{\mu}\phi_{2}\>\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}z.

3.

For all
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}\in\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
, there holds,

(A.24)

\displaystyle\big(\Lambda^{s}\psi_{1},\Lambda^{s}\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi_{2}\big)_{L^{2}}\leq\sqrt{\mu}M|\psi_{1}|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}|\psi_{2}|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}.

4.

For
\psi^{-}\in\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
the following estimates hold

(A.25)

|\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi^{-}|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}\leq\sqrt{\mu}M|\psi^{-}|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}.

5.

For all
\psi_{1}\in\mathring{H}^{s-\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
and
\psi_{2}\in\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
, there holds,

(A.26)

\big(\big[\Lambda^{s},\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\big]\psi_{1},\Lambda^{s}\psi_{2}\big)_{L^{2}}\leq{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}M|\psi_{1}|_{\mathring{H}^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}|\psi_{2}|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}.

6.

For all
V\in H^{t_{0}+1}({\mathbb{R}})
and
\psi^{-}\in\mathring{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
there holds,

(A.27)

\big((V\partial_{x}\psi^{-}),\frac{1}{\mu}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{-}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi^{-}\big)_{L^{2}}\leq\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}M|V|_{W^{1,\infty}}|\psi^{-}|^{2}_{\mathring{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

Remark A.14
.

If we compare the estimates for
\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{+}
with the ones above, we note that there is a
\sqrt{\mu}
missing. However, this is a simple consequence of the functional setting where there is an additional gain in
\mu
from the observation that

\sqrt{\mu}|\xi|\mathrm{tanh}(\sqrt{\mu}|\xi|)\sim\mu\frac{|\xi|^{2}}{(1+\sqrt{\mu}|\xi|)}.

Remark A.15
.

The inequality (
A.26
) is a direct extension of inequality
(2.24)
in
[
65
]
. While the last inequality (
A.27
) is the extension of Proposition
3.30
in
[
66
]
to infinite depth. The extension is straightforward and is explained on page
88
of this book.

Lastly, we will need a shape derivative formula for
\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{-}
and estimates on higher order shape derivatives
[
66
]
:

Proposition A.16
.

Let
t_{0}\geq 1
,
s\in[0,t_{0}+1]
, and take
\zeta\in H^{t_{0}+2}({\mathbb{R}})
. Then we have the following properties:

1.

For
\psi^{-}\in\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
the shape derivative of
\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]
at
\zeta\in H^{t_{0}+2}({\mathbb{R}})
in the direction of
h\in H^{t_{0}+2}({\mathbb{R}})
is given by the formula

(A.28)

\mathrm{d}_{\zeta}\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](h)\psi^{-}=-{\varepsilon}\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](h\underline{w}^{-})-{\varepsilon}\mu\partial_{x}(h\underline{V}^{-}).

2.

For all
0\leq s\leq t_{0}+1
,
j\geq 1
and
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}\in\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
, there holds,

(A.29)

\big|\big(\Lambda^{s}\mathrm{d}^{j}\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](\mathbf{h})\psi_{1},\Lambda^{s}\psi_{2}\big)_{L^{2}}\big|\leq{\varepsilon}^{j}\sqrt{\mu}M\prod\limits_{m=1}^{j}|h_{m}|_{H^{\max\{s,t_{0}\}+1}}|\psi_{1}|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}|\psi_{2}|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}.

3.

For all
0\leq s\leq t_{0}+\frac{1}{2}
,
j\geq 1
,
\psi_{1}\in\mathring{H}^{\max\{s,t_{0}\}+\frac{3}{2}}({\mathbb{R}}),\psi_{2}\in\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
, there holds,

(A.30)

\big|\big(\Lambda^{s}\mathrm{d}^{j}\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](\mathbf{h})\psi_{1},\Lambda^{s}\psi_{2}\big)_{L^{2}}\big|\leq{\varepsilon}^{j}\sqrt{\mu}M|h_{l}|_{H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}\prod\limits_{m\neq l}^{j}|h_{m}|_{H^{\max\{s,t_{0}\}+\frac{3}{2}}}|\psi_{1}|_{\mathring{H}^{\max\{s,t_{0}\}+1}}|\psi_{2}|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}.

A.3.
Corollaries from the results in Section
2

We will give an important generalization of Proposition
2.4
where we prove that we can trade
\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{+}
with any operator
\mathrm{Op}(A):X\rightarrow H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
satisfying

(A.31)

\big|\big(\Lambda^{s}\mathrm{Op}(A)f_{1},\Lambda^{s}f_{2}\big)_{L^{2}}\big|\leq MM_{A}(f_{1})\sqrt{\mu}|f_{2}|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}},

where
M_{A}(\psi)
is some positive number defined by the norm on
X
. This can be seen from Step
1.
in the proof, which is the only place where we use that
(\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{-})^{-1}
is composed with
\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{+}
. We have the following result.

Corollary A.17
.

Let
t_{0}\geq 1
,
s\in[0,t_{0}+1]
, and
\zeta\in H^{t_{0}+2}({\mathbb{R}})
be such that (
1.16
) is satisfied. Then for
f\in\mathscr{S}({\mathbb{R}})
and
\mathrm{Op}(A)
satisfying condition (
A.31
), the mapping

(A.32)

(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\mathrm{Op}(A)\>:\>\begin{cases}X&\rightarrow\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})\\
f&\mapsto(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\mathrm{Op}(A)f\end{cases}

is well-defined and satisfies

(A.33)

|(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\mathrm{Op}(A)f|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}\leq MM_{A}(f).

Moreover, we have the particular cases of
\mathrm{Op}(A)
:

1.

If
\mathrm{Op}(A)=\partial_{x}
, then
X=\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
and there holds,

(A.34)

|(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\partial_{x}f|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}\leq\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}M|f|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}.

2.

If
\mathrm{Op}(A)=\mathrm{d}_{\zeta}^{j}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]
for
j\geq 1
, then
X=\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
and for
\mathbf{h}=(h_{1},...,h_{j})\in H^{t_{0}+2}({\mathbb{R}})^{j}
there holds,

(A.35)

|(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\mathrm{d}_{\zeta}^{j}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]f|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}\leq{\varepsilon}^{j}\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}M\prod\limits_{m=1}^{j}|h_{m}|_{H^{\max\{s,t_{0}\}+1}}|f|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}},

and for
X=\dot{H}_{\mu}^{\max\{s,t_{0}\}+1}({\mathbb{R}})
there holds,

(A.36)

|(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\mathrm{d}_{\zeta}^{j}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]f|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}\leq{\varepsilon}^{j}\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}M|h_{k}|_{H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}\prod\limits_{m\neq k}^{j}|h_{m}|_{H^{\max\{s,t_{0}\}+\frac{3}{2}}}|f|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{\max\{s,t_{0}\}+1}}.

3.

If
\mathrm{Op}(A)=\mathrm{d}^{j}\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]
for
j\geq 1
, then
X=\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
and for
\mathbf{h}=(h_{1},...,h_{j})\in H^{t_{0}+2}({\mathbb{R}})^{j}
there holds,

(A.37)

|(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\mathrm{d}^{j}_{\zeta}\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]f|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}\leq{\varepsilon}^{j}M\prod\limits_{m=1}^{j}|h_{m}|_{H^{\max\{s,t_{0}\}+1}}|f|_{\mathring{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}},

and for
X=\mathring{H}^{\max\{s,t_{0}\}+1}({\mathbb{R}})
there holds,

(A.38)

|(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\mathrm{d}^{j}_{\zeta}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]f|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}\leq{\varepsilon}^{j}M|h_{k}|_{H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}\prod\limits_{m\neq k}^{j}|h_{m}|_{H^{\max\{s,t_{0}\}+\frac{3}{2}}}|f|_{\mathring{H}^{\max\{s,t_{0}\}+1}}.

Proof.

As explained above, we only need to consider the specific cases of
\mathrm{Op}(A)
, where (
A.33
) is deduced as in the proof of Proposition
2.4
, Step 1., where we use (
2.15
) (instead of (
2.16
)) in combination with (
A.31
). In other words, since we have (
A.33
) at hand, we simply need to verify inequality (
A.31
) and identify the constant
M_{A}(f_{1})
.

Step 1.
For the proof of (
A.34
) we use that
\partial_{x}=-\mathcal{H}|\mathrm{D}|,
where
\mathcal{H}
is the Hilbert transform and then use Plancherel’s identity together with Cauchy-Schwarz inequality to deduce the bound

\displaystyle\big|\big(\Lambda^{s}\partial_{x}f_{1},\Lambda^{s}f_{2}\big)_{L^{2}}\big|\leq M|f_{1}|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}|f_{2}|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}.

Step 2.
For the proof of (
A.35
), we use estimate (
A.16
) and(
B.4
) to get the estimate

\displaystyle\big|\big(\Lambda^{s}\mathrm{d}^{j}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](\mathbf{h})f_{1},\Lambda^{s}f_{2}\big)_{L^{2}}\big|

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}^{j}\mu M|f_{1}|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}|f_{2}|_{\dot{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}\prod\limits_{m=1}^{j}|h_{m}|_{H^{\max\{s,t_{0}\}+1}}

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}^{j}\mu^{\frac{3}{4}}M|f_{1}|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}|f_{2}|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}\prod\limits_{m=1}^{j}|h_{m}|_{H^{\max\{s,t_{0}\}+1}}.

While the proof of (
A.36
) is obtained by (
A.17
).

Step 3.
This step is a direct consequence of estimates (
A.29
)
and (
A.30
), where estimate (
A.29
) implies

\displaystyle\big|\big(\Lambda^{s}\mathrm{d}^{j}\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](\mathbf{h})f_{1},\Lambda^{s}f_{2}\big)_{L^{2}}\big|

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}^{j}\sqrt{\mu}M|f_{1}|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}|f_{2}|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}\prod\limits_{m=1}^{j}|h_{m}|_{H^{\max\{s,t_{0}\}+1}}.

∎

A.4.
Basic definitions

We start this section by defining the main quantities involved in (
1.15
) and relating them to the primary variables
(\zeta,\psi)
.

Corollary A.18
.

Let
t_{0}\geq 1
,
s\in[0,t_{0}+1]
, and
\zeta\in H^{t_{0}+2}({\mathbb{R}})
satisfying (
1.16
). Moreover define
\psi^{\pm}
as in Remark
2.11
and as the trace of
\phi^{\pm}
satisfying (
2.22
). Then there holds:

1.

The tangential velocity is given by the mapping

(A.39)

\mathcal{V}^{\pm}_{\parallel}\>:\>\begin{cases}\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})&\rightarrow H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})\\
\psi&\mapsto\partial_{x}\psi^{\pm}\end{cases}

is well-defined and satisfies

(A.40)

s\in[0,t_{0}+1],\quad|\mathcal{V}^{\pm}_{\parallel}|_{H^{s}}\leq M|\partial_{x}\psi|_{H^{s}}.

2.

The horizontal component of the velocity is given by the mappings

(A.41)

\underline{w}^{\pm}\>:\>\begin{cases}\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})&\rightarrow H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})\\
\psi&\mapsto\frac{\mathcal{G}^{\pm}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi^{\pm}+{\varepsilon}\mu\partial_{x}\zeta\partial_{x}\psi^{\pm}}{1+{\varepsilon}^{2}\mu(\partial_{x}\zeta)^{2}},\end{cases}

is well-defined and satisfies

(A.42)

\displaystyle s\in[0,t_{0}+1],\quad|\underline{w}^{\pm}|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}\leq\mu^{\frac{3}{4}}M|\psi|_{\dot{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}},

and

(A.43)

\displaystyle s\in[0,t_{0}+\frac{1}{2}],\quad|\underline{w}^{\pm}|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}\leq\mu M|\psi|_{\dot{H}^{s+1}_{\mu}}.

3.

The vertical component of the velocity is given by the mappings

(A.44)

{\underline{V}}^{\pm}\>:\>\begin{cases}\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})&\rightarrow H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})\\
\psi&\mapsto\mathcal{V}^{\pm}_{\parallel}-{\varepsilon}\underline{w}^{\pm}\partial_{x}\zeta\end{cases}

is well-defined and satisfies

(A.45)

s\in[0,t_{0}+\frac{1}{2}],\quad|\underline{V}^{\pm}|_{H^{s}}\leq M|\partial_{x}\psi|_{H^{s}}.

4.

Let
r\geq 0
, and
\delta,N\in{\mathbb{N}}
such that
r+\delta\leq N-1
. Then we have the estimate

(A.46)

|\Lambda^{r+\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{t}^{\delta}\psi|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}\leq M\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}).

5.

Recall the definition of
\mathfrak{a}
:

\mathfrak{a}=\Big(1+{\varepsilon}\big((\partial_{t}+{\varepsilon}\underline{V}^{+}\partial_{x})\underline{w}^{+}-\gamma(\partial_{t}+{\varepsilon}\underline{V}^{-}\partial_{x})\underline{w}^{-}\big)\Big).

Then for
C>0
being a nondecreasing function of its argument, we have that

(A.47)

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{a}|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\big(\mathcal{E}^{\lceil t_{0}+2\rceil}(\mathbf{U})\big),

and

(A.48)

\displaystyle|\partial_{t}\mathfrak{a}|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\varepsilon C\big(\mathcal{E}^{\lceil t_{0}+3\rceil}(\mathbf{U})\big).

Proof.

We prove each point in separate steps.

Step 1.
For
\mathcal{V}^{+}_{\parallel}
we will use formula
\psi^{+}=(\mathcal{J}_{\mu})^{-1}\psi
and Lemma
2.10
with estimate (
2.24
) to get

\displaystyle|\mathcal{V}^{+}_{\parallel}|_{H^{s}}

\displaystyle\leq M|(\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\psi|_{\dot{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}+\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}M|(\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\psi|_{\dot{H}^{(s+\frac{1}{2})+\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}

\displaystyle\leq M|\partial_{x}\psi|_{H^{s}}.

For
\mathcal{V}^{-}_{\parallel}
we use formula
\psi^{-}=(\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{-})^{-1}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{+}\psi^{+}
, then Proposition
2.4
with estimate (
2.12
), and the above estimates to deduce:

\displaystyle|\mathcal{V}^{-}_{\parallel}|_{H^{s}}=|\partial_{x}\big((\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]^{-})^{-1}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi^{+}\big)|_{H^{s}}\leq M|\partial_{x}\psi^{+}|_{H^{s}}\leq M|\partial_{x}\psi|_{H^{s}}.

Step 2.
We consider the first estimate of
\underline{w}^{+}
, where we use estimate (
A.7
), the product estimates (
B.14
) and definition of
\psi^{+}
with (
2.24
) to get:

\displaystyle|\underline{w}^{+}|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq|\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi^{+}|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}+\mu{\varepsilon}|\partial_{x}\zeta|_{H^{\max\{t_{0},s-\frac{1}{2}\}}}|\partial_{x}\psi^{+}|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq\mu^{\frac{3}{4}}M|\psi^{+}|_{\dot{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}

\displaystyle\leq\mu^{\frac{3}{4}}M|\psi|_{\dot{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}.

For the second estimate on
\underline{w}^{+}
, we argue similarly using (
A.8
) instead of (
A.7
).

For
\underline{w}^{-}
we first use the definition of
\psi^{-}=(\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{-})^{-1}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{+}\psi^{+}
and apply the same estimates, combined with (
2.11
):

\displaystyle|\underline{w}^{-}|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq|\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi^{+}|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}+{\varepsilon}\mu|\partial_{x}\zeta|_{H^{\max\{t_{0},s-\frac{1}{2}\}}}|\partial_{x}\big((\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi^{+}\big)|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq\mu^{\frac{3}{4}}M|\psi|_{\dot{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}+{\varepsilon}\mu M|(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi^{+}|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq\mu^{\frac{3}{4}}M|\psi|_{\dot{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}.

For the second estimate on
\underline{w}^{-}
, we argue as above, where we use (
A.8
) instead of (
A.7
).

Step 3.
We now prove (
A.45
). For the first part of
V^{\pm}
we use estimates (
A.40
), while the second part is estimated by the product estimate (
B.14
) and then (
A.42
) to see that

\displaystyle|V^{\pm}|_{H^{s}}

\displaystyle\leq|\mathcal{V}_{\parallel}^{\pm}|_{H^{s}}+{\varepsilon}|\underline{w}^{\pm}\partial_{x}\zeta|_{H^{s}}

\displaystyle\leq M|\partial_{x}\psi|_{H^{s}}+{\varepsilon}|\partial_{x}\zeta|_{H^{\max\{t_{0},s\}}}|\underline{w}^{\pm}|_{H^{(s+\frac{1}{2})-\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq M(|\partial_{x}\psi|_{H^{s}}+\mu^{\frac{3}{4}}|\psi|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+1}}),

\displaystyle\leq M|\partial_{x}\psi|_{H^{s}}.

Step 4.
By definition we relate
\psi
with
\psi_{(\alpha)}=\partial_{x,t}^{\alpha}\psi-\varepsilon\underline{w}\zeta_{(\alpha)}
, where
\underline{w}=\underline{w}^{+}-\gamma\underline{w}^{-}
, and use it to obtain the estimate

\displaystyle|\Lambda^{r+\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{t}^{\delta}\psi|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{2}\>:\>|\alpha|\leq N-1}|\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{x,t}^{\alpha}\psi|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\leq\sum\limits_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{2}\>:\>|\alpha|\leq N-1}|\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\psi_{(\alpha)}|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{\frac{1}{2}}}+{\varepsilon}|\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}(\underline{w}\zeta_{(\alpha)})|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{\frac{1}{2}}}.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Then (
B.14
) and (
A.42
) yields,

\displaystyle{\varepsilon}|\underline{w}\zeta_{(\alpha)}|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{1}}\leq{\varepsilon}\mu^{-\frac{1}{4}}|\underline{w}\zeta_{(\alpha)}|_{H^{1}}\leq{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}M|\partial_{x}\psi|_{H^{2}}|\zeta_{(\alpha)}|_{H^{1}}.

Combining these estimates implies

|\partial_{t}^{\delta}\psi|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{(r+\frac{1}{2})+\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}\leq M\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}).

Step 5.

We prove first (
A.47
). By Sobolev embedding with
s>t_{0}
, we have that

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{a}|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 1+\varepsilon(|\partial_{t}\underline{w}^{+}|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}+|\partial_{t}\underline{w}^{-}|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}})+\varepsilon^{2}(|\underline{V}^{+}|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}|\partial_{x}\underline{w}^{+}|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}+|\underline{V}^{-}|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}|\partial_{x}\underline{w}^{-}|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}).

For the estimate on
\underline{w}^{+}
we use that it satisfies (
A.12
) (with
t_{0}<s\leq t_{0}+1
), which in combination with the estimates in Step 2. yields

\displaystyle|\partial_{t}\underline{w}^{+}|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}\leq\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{3}{4}}M|\partial_{t}\zeta|_{H^{s+1}}|\psi^{+}|_{H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}+\mu^{\frac{3}{4}}M|\partial_{t}\psi^{+}|_{H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}.

Moreover, for
\partial_{t}\psi^{+}=\partial_{t}\big((\mathcal{J}_{\mu})^{-1}\psi\big)
we use estimate (
A.60
) for the shape derivative of the inverse of
\mathcal{J}_{\mu}
to deduce that

|\partial_{t}\psi^{+}|_{H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}\leq{\varepsilon}M|\partial_{t}\zeta|_{H^{s+1}}|\psi|_{H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}+|\partial_{t}\psi|_{H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}},

which in turn implies

(A.49)

\displaystyle|\partial_{t}\underline{w}^{+}|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}\leq\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{3}{4}}M|\partial_{t}\zeta|_{H^{s+1}}|\psi|_{H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}+\mu^{\frac{3}{4}}M|\partial_{t}\psi|_{H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}.

The estimate on
\partial_{t}\underline{w}^{-}
,
\partial_{x}\underline{w}^{\pm}
is deduced similarly, and using (
A.45
) we find that

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{a}|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 1+\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{3}{4}}M\Big(|\partial_{t}\zeta|_{H^{s+1}}|\psi|_{H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}+|\partial_{t}\psi|_{H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\varepsilon^{2}\mu^{\frac{3}{4}}M\Big(|\partial_{x}\zeta|_{H^{s+1}}|\psi|_{H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}+|\psi|_{H^{s+\frac{3}{2}}_{\mu}}\Big)|\psi|_{H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}.

Then since the energy includes time derivatives we may employ (
A.46
) to find the bound:

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{a}|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 1+{\varepsilon}C\big(\mathcal{E}^{\lceil t_{0}+2\rceil}(\mathbf{U})\big).

On the other hand, the final estimate is proved similarly by accounting for one extra time derivative and that

|\partial_{t}\underline{V}^{+}|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}\leq|\partial_{t}\big((\mathcal{J}_{\mu})^{-1}\psi\big)|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}+\varepsilon|\partial_{t}(\underline{w}^{+}\partial_{x}\zeta)|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}\leq C\big(\mathcal{E}^{\lceil t_{0}+2\rceil}(\mathbf{U})\big).

using (
A.49
) and (
A.60
). The same estimate holds for
\partial_{t}\underline{V}^{+}
using similar arguments and we find that

(A.50)

|\partial_{t}\underline{V}^{\pm}|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}\leq C\big(\mathcal{E}^{\lceil t_{0}+2\rceil}(\mathbf{U})\big),

which allows us to conclude the proof of the Corollary.

∎

A.5.
Proof of Proposition
4.2

We will in this section give the details on the proof of Proposition
4.2
. Since the strategy in the proof is exactly the same as in
[
65
]
and the main quantities involved satisfy the same estimates (see Corollary
A.18
), we only prove the steps that are unique to the current regime. The first step is to derive linearization formulas. for the Dirichlet-Neumann operator
\mathcal{G}_{\mu}
.

A.5.1.
Linearization formulas

The main step in the quasilinearisation of the internal water waves system (
1.15
) is to get linearization formulas for

\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]=\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\Big(1-\gamma(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\Big)^{-1}\psi.

Here it will be important to get an explicit formula for the shape derivative of
\mathcal{G}_{\mu}
with respect to
\zeta
and track the dependencies in the small parameters.

Proposition A.19
.

Let
T>0
,
t_{0}\geq 1
and
N\in{\mathbb{N}}
be such that
N\geq 5
. Furthermore, let
U=(\zeta,\psi)^{T}\in\mathscr{E}_{\mathrm{bo},T}^{N,t_{0}}
be such that (
1.16
) is satisfied on
[0,T]
. For all
\alpha=(\alpha^{1},\alpha^{2})\in{\mathbb{N}}^{2}
,
\check{\alpha}^{j}=\alpha-\mathbf{e}_{j}
, with
1\leq|\alpha|\leq N
and define
\partial_{x,t}^{\alpha}=\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{1}}\partial_{t}^{\alpha_{2}}
, and let
\underline{w}^{\pm}
be as defined in (
A.41
). Moreover, define
\underline{w}
by

\displaystyle\underline{w}

\displaystyle=\underline{w}^{+}-\gamma\underline{w}^{-},

and

\zeta_{(\alpha)}=\partial_{x,t}^{\alpha}\zeta,\quad\psi_{(\alpha)}=\partial_{x,t}^{\alpha}\psi-{\varepsilon}\underline{w}\partial_{x,t}^{\alpha}\zeta,\quad\psi_{\langle\check{\alpha}\rangle}=(\psi_{\check{\alpha}^{1})},\psi_{(\check{\alpha}^{2})}).

Then for all
1\leq|\alpha|\leq N
, one has

\displaystyle\text{if}\>\>\alpha<N:

\displaystyle\quad\frac{1}{\mu}\partial_{x,t}^{\alpha}(\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi)=\frac{1}{\mu}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi_{(\alpha)}+{\varepsilon}R_{(\alpha)},

\displaystyle\text{if}\>\>\alpha\leq N:

\displaystyle\quad\frac{1}{\mu}\partial_{x,t}^{\alpha}(\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi)=\frac{1}{\mu}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi_{(\alpha)}-{\varepsilon}\mathcal{I}[U]\zeta_{(\alpha)}+\frac{1}{\mu}\mathcal{G}_{\mu,(\alpha)}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi_{\langle\check{\alpha}\rangle}+{\varepsilon}R_{\alpha},

where the linear operators
\mathcal{I}[U]
and
\mathcal{G}_{\mu,(\alpha)}
are given in Definition
4.1
, while
R_{\alpha}
is a function that satisfies the estimate

(A.51)

|R_{\alpha}(t)|^{2}_{H^{1}_{\mathrm{bo}}}\leq C\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}(t)),

for some
C(M,\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}))>0
nondecreasing function of its argument and for all
t\in[0,T]
.

Remark A.20
.

The principal part of the linearization formula for
\mathcal{G}_{\mu}
is given in terms of
\mathcal{G}_{\mu}\psi_{(\alpha)}
and
\mathcal{I}[U]\zeta_{\alpha}
. While the additional term
\mathcal{G}_{\mu,(\alpha)}\psi_{\langle\check{\alpha}\rangle}
is sub-principal that offers no difficulty in the proof, but is needed to deal with surface tension in the energy estimates (see the third point in Remark
5.1
).

We will now give the main ingredients in proving the linearization formulas in Proposition
A.19
. These formulas involve the precise formulation of the directional derivative of
\mathcal{G}_{\mu}
, and we therefore give the definition.

Remark A.21
.

Let
\psi\in H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
and
\zeta\in H^{t_{0}+2}({\mathbb{R}})
, then
\zeta\mapsto\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi
is smooth and the directional derivative, in the direction of
h\in H^{t_{0}+2}({\mathbb{R}})
, is given by

\mathrm{d}_{\zeta}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](h)\psi=\lim\limits_{\nu\rightarrow 0}\frac{\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta+\nu h]\psi-\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi}{\nu}.

The smoothness follows from the shape analyticity of
\mathcal{G}^{\pm}_{\mu}

[
66
]
.

Lemma A.22
.

Let
t_{0}\geq 1
and
\zeta\in H^{t_{0}+2}({\mathbb{R}})
be such that (
1.16
) is satisfied.

1.

For all
\psi\in\dot{H}_{\mu}^{\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
,
h\in H^{t_{0}+2}({\mathbb{R}})
, and
U=(\zeta,\psi)^{T}
, one has

(A.52)

\mathrm{d}_{\zeta}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](h)\psi=-{\varepsilon}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\big(h(\underline{w}^{+}-\gamma\underline{w}^{-})\big)-{\varepsilon}\mu\mathcal{I}[U]h.

where
\mathcal{I}[U]h
is defined by

\mathcal{I}[U]h=\partial_{x}(h\underline{V}^{+})+\gamma\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\partial_{x}\Big(h(\underline{V}^{+}-\underline{V}^{-})\Big).

2.

For all
0\leq s\leq t_{0}+1
,
j\geq 1
, there holds,

(A.53)

|\mathrm{d}_{\zeta}^{j}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](h)\psi|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}\leq{\varepsilon}^{j}\mu^{\frac{3}{4}}M\prod\limits_{m=1}^{j}|h_{m}|_{H^{\max\{s,t_{0}\}+1}}|\psi|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}.

3.

For all
0\leq s\leq t_{0}+\frac{1}{2}
,
j\geq 1
, there holds,

(A.54)

|\mathrm{d}_{\zeta}^{j}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](h)\psi|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}\leq{\varepsilon}^{j}\mu M\prod\limits_{m=1}^{j}|h_{m}|_{H^{\max\{s+\frac{1}{2},t_{0}\}+1}}|\psi|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+1}}.

4.

For all
0\leq s\leq t_{0}+\frac{1}{2}
,
j\geq 1
, there holds,

(A.55)

|\mathrm{d}_{\zeta}^{j}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](h)\psi|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}\leq{\varepsilon}^{j}\mu^{\frac{3}{4}}M|h_{k}|_{H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}\prod\limits_{m\neq k}^{j}|h_{m}|_{H^{\max\{s,t_{0}\}+\frac{3}{2}}}|\psi|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{\max\{s,t_{0}\}+1}}.

5.

For all
0\leq s\leq t_{0}
,
j\geq 1
, there holds,

(A.56)

|\mathrm{d}_{\zeta}^{j}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](h)\psi|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}\leq{\varepsilon}^{j}\mu M|h_{k}|_{H^{s+1}}\prod\limits_{m\neq k}^{j}|h_{m}|_{H^{\max\{s+\frac{1}{2},t_{0}\}+\frac{3}{2}}}|\psi|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{\max\{s+\frac{1}{2},t_{0}\}+1}}.

Proof.

The proof is similar to the one in
[
65
]
, but we have to track the dependence in the small parameters for the current scaling and adapt them to a different functional setting. We prove each point separately.

Step 1.
We do the details here, where we note that it is important not to compose the inverse of
\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{+}
with
\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{-}
(see Remark
2.5
). With this in mind, we observe by direct computations (suppressing the argument in
\zeta
) that

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}(\mathcal{G}_{\mu}\circ\mathcal{J}_{\mu})(h)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}(h)\circ\mathcal{J}_{\mu}+\mathcal{G}_{\mu}\circ\mathrm{d}\mathcal{J}_{\mu}(h).

Then use definition (
2.1
) and compose the above identity with
\mathcal{J}_{\mu}^{-1}
from the right to obtain that

(A.57)

\mathrm{d}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}(h)=\mathrm{d}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{+}(h)\circ(\mathcal{J}_{\mu})^{-1}-\mathcal{G}_{\mu}\circ\mathrm{d}\mathcal{J}_{\mu}(h)\circ(\mathcal{J}_{\mu})^{-1}.

Then we note by Proposition
2.11
that
(\mathcal{J}_{\mu})^{-1}\psi=\psi^{+}

allowing us to use formula (
A.11
) and express the first term by

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{+}(h)(\mathcal{J}_{\mu})^{-1}\psi

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{+}(h)\psi^{+}

\displaystyle=-{\varepsilon}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{+}(h\underline{w}^{+})-{\varepsilon}\mu\partial_{x}(h\underline{V}^{+}).

For the second term of (
A.57
), we first make the observation

\displaystyle(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu})^{-1}\circ\mathrm{d}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}(h)

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu})^{-1}\circ\mathrm{d}(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}\circ\big((\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu})^{-1}\circ\mathcal{G^{+}_{\mu}}\big))(h)

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu})^{-1}\circ\Big(\mathrm{d}\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}(h)\circ\big((\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu})^{-1}\circ\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}\big)-\gamma^{-1}\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}\circ\mathrm{d}\mathcal{J}_{\mu}(h)\Big)

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu})^{-1}\circ\mathrm{d}\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}(h)\circ(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu})^{-1}\circ\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}+\gamma^{-1}\mathrm{d}\mathcal{J}_{\mu}(h),

so that

\mathrm{d}\mathcal{J}_{\mu}(h)=\gamma(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu})^{-1}\circ\Big(\mathrm{d}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}(h)-\mathrm{d}\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}(h)\circ(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu})^{-1}\circ\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}\Big).

Then by Remark
2.11
we have that
\psi^{-}=(\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{-})^{-1}\circ\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{+}\psi^{+}
, together with the shape derivative formulas (
A.11
) and (
A.28
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\mathcal{J}_{\mu}(h)\circ(\mathcal{J}_{\mu})^{-1}\psi

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}\mathcal{J}_{\mu}(h)\psi^{+}

\displaystyle=\gamma(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu})^{-1}\Big(\mathrm{d}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}(h)\psi^{+}-\mathrm{d}\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}(h)\psi^{-}\Big)

\displaystyle=-{\varepsilon}\gamma(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu})^{-1}\Big(\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}(h\underline{w}^{+})-\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}(h\underline{w}^{-})\Big)-\gamma{\varepsilon}\mu(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu})^{-1}\Big(\partial_{x}(hV^{+})-\partial_{x}(hV^{-})\Big).

From (
A.57
) and definition of
\mathcal{I}[U]h
given by (
4.2
), we may collect the above identities and factorize the leading terms together to find that

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}(h)\psi

\displaystyle=-{\varepsilon}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}(h\underline{w}^{+})-{\varepsilon}\gamma\mathcal{G}_{\mu}\circ(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu})^{-1}\Big(\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}(h\underline{w}^{+})-\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}(h\underline{w}^{-})\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-{\varepsilon}\mu\Big(\partial_{x}(hV^{+})+\gamma\mathcal{G}_{\mu}\circ(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu})^{-1}\Big(\partial_{x}(hV^{+})-\partial_{x}(hV^{-})\Big)\Big)

\displaystyle=-{\varepsilon}\Big(1+\gamma\mathcal{G}_{\mu}\circ(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu})^{-1}\Big)\circ\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}(h\underline{w}^{+})+{\varepsilon}\gamma\mathcal{G}_{\mu}(h\underline{w}^{-})-\mu{\varepsilon}\mathcal{I}(U)h.

Clearly, the proof is a consequence of the following relation

(A.58)

\displaystyle\Big(1+\gamma\mathcal{G}_{\mu}\circ(\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{-})^{-1}\Big)\circ\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}=\mathcal{G}_{\mu}.

and can be seen in the identity

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{G}_{\mu}\circ\mathcal{J}_{\mu}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{G}_{\mu}-\gamma\mathcal{G}_{\mu}\circ(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu})^{-1}\circ\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}.

Step 2.
We first use the previous step and (
A.57
) to write

(A.59)

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}(h)\psi

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}(h)\psi^{+}-\mathcal{G}_{\mu}\circ\mathrm{d}\mathcal{J}_{\mu}(h)\psi^{+}.

Then for the first term, we use (
A.12
) and Proposition (
2.10
) with estimate (
2.24
) to get that

\displaystyle|\mathrm{d}^{j}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}(h)\psi^{+}|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}^{j}\mu^{\frac{3}{4}}M\prod\limits_{m=1}^{j}|h_{m}|_{H^{\max\{s,t_{0}\}+1}}|(\mathcal{J}_{\mu})^{-1}\psi|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}},

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}^{j}\mu^{\frac{3}{4}}M\prod\limits_{m=1}^{j}|h_{m}|_{H^{\max\{s,t_{0}\}+1}}|\psi|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}.

For the second term, we first let
j=1
and observe by direct calculations (or see previous step) that

\displaystyle|\mathrm{d}\mathcal{J}_{\mu}\psi^{+}|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq|(\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{-})^{-1}\mathrm{d}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{+}(h_{1})\psi^{+}|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}+|(\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{-})^{-1}\mathrm{d}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{-}(h_{1})\psi^{-}|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle=:R_{1}+R_{2}.

Here we treat
R_{1}
by (
A.35
) and then (
2.24
) to get that

R_{1}\leq{\varepsilon}\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}M|h_{1}|_{H^{\max\{s,t_{0}\}+1}}|\psi|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}.

For
R_{2}
we use (
A.37
), then the relation
\psi^{-}=(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu})^{-1}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{+}\psi^{+}
together with estimates (
2.11
) and (
2.24
) to obtain,

\displaystyle R_{2}

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}M|h_{1}|_{H^{\max\{s,t_{0}\}+1}}|\psi^{-}|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}M|h_{1}|_{H^{\max\{s,t_{0}\}+1}}|\psi|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}.

As a result, for
j=1
, we have by (
2.2
) (for
0\leq s\leq t_{0}+1
) and the above estimates that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{G}_{\mu}\circ\mathrm{d}\mathcal{J}_{\mu}(h)\psi^{+}|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq\mu^{\frac{3}{4}}M|\mathrm{d}\mathcal{J}_{\mu}(h)\psi^{+}|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\mu}M|\mathrm{d}\mathcal{J}_{\mu}(h)\psi^{+}|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}\mu^{\frac{3}{4}}M|h_{1}|_{H^{\max\{s,t_{0}\}+1}}|\psi|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}.

The remaining cases follow recursively and can be proved by induction.

Step 3
-
5.
The proof follows similarly, where for the first term in (
A.59
) we instead use (
A.13
) or (
A.14
), or (
A.15
). While for the second term, we can gain precision in
\mu
using (
2.4
).

∎

Remark A.23
.

In Step
1.
of the proof, we found a formula for the shape derivative of
\mathcal{J}_{\mu}
:

\mathrm{d}_{\zeta}\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](h)=\gamma(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\circ\Big(\mathrm{d}_{\zeta}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](h)-\mathrm{d}_{\zeta}\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](h)\circ(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\circ\mathcal{G^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]}\Big).

Furthermore, in Step
2.
, we proved the estimate

(A.60)

\displaystyle|\mathrm{d}_{\zeta}\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](\partial_{x}\zeta)\psi^{+}|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}\leq{\varepsilon}\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}M|\partial_{x}\zeta|_{H^{\max\{s,t_{0}\}+1}}|\psi|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}.

From these expressions, we can deduce an estimate on the inverse:

(A.61)

\displaystyle|\mathrm{d}_{\zeta}(\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}(\partial_{x}\zeta)\psi^{+}|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle=|(\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\circ\mathrm{d}_{\zeta}\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](\partial_{x}\zeta)\circ(\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\psi|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon}M|\partial_{x}\zeta|_{H^{\max\{s,t_{0}\}+1}}|\psi|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}.

For an application of these estimates, see proof of (
3.23
).

Remark A.24
.

In step
1
. in the proof we also have some convenient identities for
\mathcal{G}_{\mu}
. We saw that

\Big(1+\gamma\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\circ(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\Big)\circ\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]=\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta].

Composing this identity with
(\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}
on the right, we get a quantity we will use later:

(A.62)

\big(1+\gamma\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\big)=\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}

Furthermore, we have that

\displaystyle-\gamma(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]

\displaystyle=-\gamma(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}

\displaystyle=1-(\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1},

which implies

(A.63)

(\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}+\gamma(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]=1+2\gamma(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta].

and

(A.64)

\gamma(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]^{-1}(\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}=-\gamma\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]^{-1}(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}.

Proof of Proposition
A.19
.

The proof relies on the estimates provided by Lemma
A.22
. However, these estimates are the same as in
[
65
]
, and so we refer the reader to the proof of Proposition
6
in this paper.
∎

To prove the main result of this section, we also need the following Lemma:

Lemma A.25
.

Under the assumptions of Proposition
A.19
, one has for
|\alpha|\leq N-1
that

\mathcal{G}^{\pm}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi_{(\alpha)}=r_{\alpha}^{\pm},

In the case when
|\alpha|=N
, then

\mathcal{G}^{\pm}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi^{\pm}_{(\alpha)}=\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi_{(\alpha)}-\gamma{\varepsilon}\mu\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](\mathcal{G}^{\mp}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\partial_{x}\Big(\zeta_{(\alpha)}(\underline{V}^{+}-\underline{V}^{-})\Big)+r_{\alpha}^{\pm},

where the residual terms
r^{\pm}_{\alpha}
satisfies

|(\mathcal{G}^{\pm}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}r_{\alpha}^{\pm}|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{3}{2}}_{\mu}}^{2}\leq C\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U})(1+\gamma{\varepsilon}^{2}\sqrt{\mu}|\zeta|_{<N+\frac{1}{2}>}^{2}),

for some
C(M,\mathcal{E}^{N}(\mathbf{U}))>0
nondecreasing function of its argument and

|\zeta|_{<N+\frac{1}{2}>}=\sum\limits_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{2},|\alpha|=N}|\partial_{x,t}^{\alpha}\zeta|_{\mathring{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

Remark A.26
.

Here it is crucial to use that
\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})\subset\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbb{R}})
where we have the estimate

|f|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}\leq\mu^{-\frac{1}{4}}|f|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}.

To regain the precision in
\mu
, we note that is provided in Proposition
2.4
and Corollary
A.17
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to the proof of Proposition
7
in
[
65
]
, so we will just point out the differences. To that end, we focus on the case
|\alpha|=N
and apply
\partial^{\alpha}_{x,t}
to the relation
\mathcal{G}^{\pm}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]=\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]
. Then using identities (
A.11
), (
A.28
), and (
A.52
) (or Proposition
A.19
without sub-principal terms) to find that

\mathcal{G}^{\pm}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi_{(\alpha)}^{\pm}-{\varepsilon}\mu\partial_{x}(\zeta_{(\alpha)}\underline{V}^{\pm})=\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi_{(\alpha)}-{\varepsilon}\mu\mathcal{I}[U]\zeta_{(\alpha)}+r_{\alpha}^{\pm},

where
r_{\alpha}^{\pm}
is on the form

\mathrm{d}_{\zeta}^{j}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](\partial^{l^{1}}_{x,t}\zeta,...,\partial^{l^{j}}_{x,t}\zeta)\partial^{\delta}_{x,t}\psi-\mathrm{d}_{\zeta}^{j}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]^{\pm}(\partial^{l^{1}}_{x,t}\zeta,...,\partial^{l^{j}}_{x,t}\zeta)\partial^{\delta}_{x,t}\psi^{\pm},

with
\sum\limits_{i=1}^{j}|l^{j}|+|\delta|=N
,
0\leq|\delta|\leq N-1
and
|l^{i}|\leq N
. The proof of estimate is the same as in the Proposition
7
in
[
65
]
after using Remark
A.26
. Moreover, applying the definition of
\mathcal{I}[U]\zeta_{(\alpha)}
we find that

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi_{(\alpha)}^{+}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi_{(\alpha)}-\gamma{\varepsilon}\mu\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\partial_{x}\Big(\zeta_{(\alpha)}(\underline{V}^{+}-\underline{V}^{-})\Big).

Similarly, for
\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}
we observe that

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi_{(\alpha)}^{-}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi_{(\alpha)}-{\varepsilon}\mu\big(1+\gamma\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\big)\partial_{x}\Big(\zeta_{(\alpha)}(\underline{V}^{+}-\underline{V}^{-})\Big).

Then we conclude by relation (
A.62
).

∎

We will now turn to the proof of Proposition
4.2
. However, since the quantities involved satisfy the same estimates as in
[
65
]
, we only point out the main differences (see also for a similar approach
[
46
]
).

Proof of Proposition
4.2
.

Let
\psi^{\pm}
and
\psi
be defined as in the transmission problem (
2.22
) throughout the proof. Then for the first equation, we simply apply
\partial_{x,t}^{\alpha}
and conclude by Proposition
A.19
.

For the second equation, we need to prove that

\displaystyle\text{if}\>\>1\leq|\alpha|<N:

\displaystyle\quad\hskip 65.44142pt\partial_{t}\psi_{(\alpha)}+\mathfrak{Ins}[\mathbf{U}]\zeta_{(\alpha)}={\varepsilon}S_{\alpha},

(A.65)

\displaystyle\text{if}\qquad\>|\alpha|=N:

\displaystyle\quad\partial_{t}\psi_{(\alpha)}+\mathfrak{Ins}[\mathbf{U}]\zeta_{(\alpha)}-{\varepsilon}\mathcal{I}[\mathbf{U}]^{\ast}\psi_{(\alpha)}={\varepsilon}S_{\alpha}.

We will only prove the most difficult case
|\alpha|=N
, but first need an observation. Let
\partial
denote either the derivative with respect to
x
or
t
. Then the following identity holds,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}

\displaystyle\partial\psi+\partial\zeta-\frac{{\varepsilon}}{\mu}(\underline{w}^{+}-\gamma\underline{w}^{-})\partial_{t}\partial\zeta

\displaystyle+{\varepsilon}\underline{V}^{+}(\partial_{x}\partial\psi^{+}-{\varepsilon}\underline{w}^{+}\partial_{x}\partial\zeta)-\gamma{\varepsilon}\underline{V}^{-}(\partial_{x}\partial\psi^{-}-{\varepsilon}\underline{w}^{-}\partial_{x}\partial\zeta)

(A.66)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\mathrm{bo}}\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}}\partial\kappa({\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}\zeta).

To prove the claim (
A.5.1
), we apply
\partial
to the second equation (
1.15
) to find that

(A.67)

\partial_{t}\partial\psi+\partial\zeta+{\varepsilon}\big((\partial_{x}\psi^{+})(\partial_{x}\partial\psi^{+})-\gamma(\partial_{x}\psi^{-})(\partial_{x}\partial\psi^{-})\big)+{\varepsilon}\partial\mathcal{N}[{\varepsilon}\zeta,\psi^{\pm}]=-\frac{1}{\mathrm{bo}}\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}}\partial\kappa({\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}\zeta),

where

\displaystyle{\varepsilon}\partial\mathcal{N}[{\varepsilon}\zeta,\psi^{\pm}]

\displaystyle=-\partial\Bigg(\frac{{\varepsilon}}{2\mu}(1+{\varepsilon}^{2}\mu(\partial_{x}\zeta)^{2})\Big((\underline{w}^{+})^{2}-\gamma(\underline{w}^{-})^{2}\Big)\Bigg)

\displaystyle=-{\varepsilon}^{3}(\partial_{x}\zeta)(\partial_{x}\partial\zeta)\Big((\underline{w}^{+})^{2}-\gamma(\underline{w}^{-})^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-\frac{1}{\mu}(1+{\varepsilon}^{2}\mu(\partial_{x}\zeta)^{2})\Big((\underline{w}^{+})(\partial\underline{w}^{+})-\gamma(\underline{w}^{-})(\partial\underline{w}^{-})\Big).

To conclude, we observe by definition that (or use Lemma
4.13

[
66
]
):

\displaystyle{\varepsilon}(\partial_{x}\psi^{\pm})

\displaystyle(\partial_{x}\partial\psi^{\pm})-{\varepsilon}^{3}(\partial_{x}\zeta)(\partial_{x}\partial\zeta)(\underline{w}^{\pm})^{2}-\frac{{\varepsilon}}{\mu}(1+{\varepsilon}^{2}\mu(\partial_{x}\zeta)^{2})(\underline{w}^{\pm})(\partial\underline{w}^{\pm})

\displaystyle={\varepsilon}\underline{V}^{\pm}(\partial_{x}\partial\psi^{\pm}-{\varepsilon}\underline{w}^{\pm}\partial_{x}\partial\zeta)-\frac{{\varepsilon}}{\mu}\underline{w}^{\pm}\partial\big(\mathcal{G}_{\mu}^{\pm}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi^{\pm}\big).

By adding these observations and trading
\frac{1}{\mu}\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi^{+}
and
\frac{1}{\mu}\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\psi^{-}
with
\partial_{t}\zeta
, we deduce the identity (
A.5.1
).

The next step is to let
\alpha=\beta+\delta
with
|\delta|=1
, where we trade
\partial
with
\partial^{\delta}_{x,t}
in (
A.5.1
). Then we apply
\partial^{\beta}_{x,t}
to this equation, where we claim that it will result in the following equation:

(A.68)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}

\displaystyle\partial^{\alpha}_{x,t}\psi+\partial^{\alpha}_{x,t}\zeta-\frac{{\varepsilon}}{\mu}(\underline{w}^{+}-\gamma\underline{w}^{-})\partial_{t}\partial^{\alpha}_{x,t}\zeta

\displaystyle+{\varepsilon}\underline{V}^{+}(\partial_{x}\psi^{+}_{(\alpha)}+{\varepsilon}(\partial_{x}\underline{w}^{+})\partial_{x}\partial^{\alpha}_{x,t}\zeta)-\gamma{\varepsilon}\underline{V}^{-}(\partial_{x}\psi^{-}_{(\alpha)}+{\varepsilon}(\partial_{x}\underline{w}^{-})\partial_{x}\partial^{\alpha}_{x,t}\zeta)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\mathrm{bo}}\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}}\partial^{\alpha}_{x,t}\kappa({\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}\zeta)+{\varepsilon}S_{\alpha},

where
S_{\alpha}
is some generic function, satisfying

(A.69)

|S_{\alpha}|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}\leq C\mathcal{E}^{N}(U)\big(1+{\varepsilon}^{2}\sqrt{\mu}|\underline{V}^{+}-\underline{V}^{-}|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}|\zeta|^{2}_{<N+\frac{1}{2}>}\big).

The proof of this fact is the same as in
[
65
]
, where the estimate relies on the following inequalities

(A.70)

|\psi_{(\alpha)}^{\pm}|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}\leq 1+\gamma{\varepsilon}\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}|\underline{V}^{+}-\underline{V}^{-}|_{L^{\infty}}|\zeta|_{<N+\frac{1}{2}>},

and

(A.71)

\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu}}|\partial^{\beta}\underline{w}^{\pm}|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mu}}\leq 1+\gamma{\varepsilon}\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}|\underline{V}^{+}-\underline{V}^{-}|_{L^{\infty}}|\zeta|_{<N+\frac{1}{2}>}.

This can be seen in the proof of Lemma
9
in
[
65
]
. In our case, the proof is a consequence of Lemma
A.25
and Corollary
A.18
. However, the quantities involved satisfy the same estimates and therefore complete the estimate on
S_{\alpha}
.

We may now further decompose (
A.68
), where we may now use the definition of
\psi_{(\alpha)}
to find that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\psi_{(\alpha)}+\mathfrak{a}\zeta_{(\alpha)}+{\varepsilon}\underline{V}^{+}\partial_{x}\psi^{+}_{(\alpha)}-\gamma{\varepsilon}\underline{V}^{-}\partial_{x}\psi^{-}_{(\alpha)}=-\frac{1}{\mathrm{bo}}\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}}\partial^{\alpha}\kappa({\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}\zeta)+{\varepsilon}S_{\alpha},

where we identify
\mathfrak{a}
by

\mathfrak{a}=\Big(1+{\varepsilon}\big((\partial_{t}+{\varepsilon}\underline{V}^{+}\partial_{x})\underline{w}^{+}-\gamma(\partial_{t}+{\varepsilon}\underline{V}^{-}\partial_{x})\underline{w}^{-}\big)\Big).

To conclude, we simply need to work on the terms:

(A.72)

\displaystyle{\varepsilon}\underline{V}^{+}\partial_{x}\psi^{+}_{(\alpha)}-\gamma{\varepsilon}\underline{V}^{-}\partial_{x}\psi^{-}_{(\alpha)}

\displaystyle=\frac{{\varepsilon}}{2}\big(\underline{V}^{+}+\underline{V}^{-}\big)\partial_{x}\psi_{(\alpha)}+\frac{{\varepsilon}}{2}\big(\underline{V}^{+}-\underline{V}^{-}\big)\partial_{x}\big(\psi_{(\alpha)}^{+}+\gamma\psi^{-}_{(\alpha)}\big).

Then by identities (
A.63
), (
A.64
), and Lemma
A.25
implies

\displaystyle\psi_{(\alpha)}^{+}+\gamma\psi^{-}_{(\alpha)}

\displaystyle=\big((\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]^{-1}+\gamma(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\big)\psi_{(\alpha)}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-\gamma{\varepsilon}\mu\Big(\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]^{-1}(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}+\gamma(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta](\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\Big)\partial_{x}\big(\zeta_{(\alpha)}(\underline{V}^{+}-\underline{V}^{-})\big)+\tilde{r}_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\big(1+2\gamma(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\big)\psi_{(\alpha)}-(1-\gamma)\gamma{\varepsilon}\mu\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]^{-1}(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\circ\partial_{x}\big(\zeta_{(\alpha)}(\underline{V}^{+}-\underline{V}^{-})\big)+\tilde{r}_{\alpha},

with

\partial_{x}\tilde{r}_{\alpha}=\partial_{x}\big((\mathcal{G}^{+}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}r_{\alpha}^{+}+(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}r_{\alpha}^{-}\big).

Here
r_{\alpha}^{\pm}
is the term in Lemma
A.25
, which is of lower order. So that (
A.72
) becomes

{\varepsilon}\underline{V}^{+}\partial_{x}\psi^{+}_{(\alpha)}-\gamma{\varepsilon}\underline{V}^{-}\partial_{x}\psi^{-}_{(\alpha)}=-{\varepsilon}\mathcal{I}[\mathbf{U}]^{\ast}\psi_{(\alpha)}-(1-\gamma)\gamma{\varepsilon}^{2}\mu(\underline{V}^{+}-\underline{V}^{-})\mathfrak{E}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\big(\zeta_{(\alpha)}(\underline{V}^{+}-\underline{V}^{-})\big),

where
\mathfrak{E}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]
reads

\mathfrak{E}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\bullet=\partial_{x}\circ\mathcal{J}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]^{-1}(\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\circ\partial_{x}\bullet,

and we identify
\mathcal{I}[\mathbf{U}]^{\ast}
by

\mathcal{I}[\mathbf{U}]^{\ast}\bullet=-\underline{V}^{+}\partial_{x}\bullet-\gamma(\underline{V}^{+}-\underline{V}^{-})\partial_{x}\big((\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta])^{-1}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\bullet\big).

The surface tension term is linearized with the formula

\frac{1}{\mathrm{bo}}\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}}\partial^{\alpha}\kappa({\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}\zeta)=\mathrm{bo}^{-1}\partial_{x}\mathcal{K}({\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}\partial_{x}\zeta)\partial_{x}\zeta_{(\alpha)}+\mathcal{K}_{(\alpha)}[\sqrt{\mu}{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}\zeta]\zeta_{\langle\check{\alpha}\rangle}+{\varepsilon}S_{\alpha},

where
\mathcal{K}
is given in Definition
4.1
. We also identify
\mathfrak{Ins}[\mathbf{U}]
by

\mathfrak{Ins}[\mathbf{U}]\bullet=\mathfrak{a}\bullet-(1-\gamma)\gamma{\varepsilon}^{2}\mu[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]\mathfrak{E}_{\mu}[{\varepsilon}\zeta]\big(\bullet[\![\underline{V}^{\pm}]\!]\big)-\mathrm{bo}^{-1}\partial_{x}\mathcal{K}[{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}\partial_{x}\zeta]\partial_{x}\bullet.

∎

Appendix B
Tools

B.1.
Estimates on Fourier multipliers and classical estimates

In this section, we will give basic multiplier estimates. To be precise, we will give a definition of the Fourier multipliers.

Definition B.1
.

We say that a Fourier multiplier
\mathrm{F}
is of order
s

(s\in\mathbb{R})
and write
\mathrm{F}\in\mathcal{S}^{s}
if
\xi\in\mathbb{R}\mapsto F(\xi)\in\mathbb{C}
is smooth and satisfies

\displaystyle\forall\xi\in\mathbb{R},\forall\beta\in\mathbb{N},\qquad\sup_{\xi\in\mathbb{R}}\>\langle\xi\rangle^{\beta-s}|\partial^{\beta}F(\xi)|<\infty.

We also introduce the seminorm

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{s}(\mathrm{F})=\sup_{\beta\in\mathbb{N},\beta\leq 4}\>\sup_{\xi\in\mathbb{R}}\>\langle\xi\rangle^{\beta-s}|\partial^{\beta}F(\xi)|.

The first result is several basic multiplier estimates that are used throughout the paper.

Proposition B.2
.

Let
\mu,\gamma,\mathrm{bo}^{-1}\in(0,1)
and
f\in\mathscr{S}({\mathbb{R}})
. Then there exist a universal constant
C>0
such that:

1.

For the Fourier multiplier

\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[0]f(x)=\sqrt{\mu}\mathcal{F}^{-1}\Big(|\xi|\frac{\mathrm{tanh}(\sqrt{\mu}|\xi|)}{1+\gamma\mathrm{tanh}(\sqrt{\mu}|\xi|)}\hat{f}(\xi)\Big)(x),

there holds,

(B.1)

|\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[0]f|_{L^{2}}\leq\mu^{\frac{3}{4}}C|f|_{\dot{H}^{1}_{\mu}},

and

(B.2)

|\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[0]f|_{L^{2}}\leq\mu C|f|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{3}{2}}_{\mu}}.

2.

For the Fourier multiplier

(\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[0])^{\frac{1}{2}}f(x)=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\bigg(\Big(\sqrt{\mu}|\xi|\frac{\mathrm{tanh}(\sqrt{\mu}|\xi|)}{1+\gamma\mathrm{tanh}(\sqrt{\mu}|\xi|)}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\hat{f}(\xi)\bigg)(x),

there holds,

(B.3)

\frac{1}{C}|f|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu}}|(\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[0])^{\frac{1}{2}}f|_{L^{2}}\leq C|f|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

3.

There holds,

(B.4)

|f|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}\leq\mu^{-\frac{1}{4}}|\partial_{x}f|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}\leq\mu^{-\frac{1}{4}}|f|_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}},

and

(B.5)

|f|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}\leq|\partial_{x}f|_{H^{s}}.

Moreover, for
\mathfrak{P}=|\mathrm{D}|(1+\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|)^{-\frac{1}{2}}
and
S^{-}=-\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|
there holds,

(B.6)

|\frac{\mathfrak{P}^{2}}{S^{-}}f|_{H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}\leq\mu^{-\frac{3}{4}}|f|_{\dot{H}_{\mu}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}.

4.

Define Fourier multipliers

\displaystyle\mathrm{T}(\mathrm{D})f(x)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\Big(\frac{\tanh(\sqrt{\mu}|\xi|)}{\sqrt{\mu}|\xi|}\hat{f}(\xi)\Big)(x),

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}(\mathrm{D})f(x)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\Big((1+\gamma\tanh(\sqrt{\mu}|\xi|))^{-1}\hat{f}(\xi)\Big)(x)

Then there holds,

(B.7)

\displaystyle|(\mathrm{T}(\mathrm{D})-1)f|_{H^{s}}

\displaystyle\leq\mu C|\partial_{x}^{2}f|_{H^{s}},

(B.8)

\displaystyle|(\mathrm{I}(\mathrm{D})-1)f|_{H^{s}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\mu}C|\partial_{x}f|_{H^{s}}.

Moreover, let
\sqrt{\mathrm{k}}(\mathrm{D})
be the equare root of the symbol

\mathrm{k}(\mathrm{D})=\mathrm{I}(\mathrm{D})\mathrm{T}(\mathrm{D})\big((1-\mathrm{bo}^{-1}\partial_{x}^{2}\big).

Then there holds,

(B.9)

\displaystyle|\sqrt{\mathrm{k}}(\mathrm{D})f|_{H^{s}}

\displaystyle\leq C(|f|_{H^{s}}+\mathrm{bo}^{-\frac{1}{2}}|\partial_{x}f|_{H^{s}}),

(B.10)

\displaystyle|(\mathrm{k}(\mathrm{D})-1)f|_{H^{s}}

\displaystyle\leq(\sqrt{\mu}+\mathrm{bo}^{-1})C|\partial_{x}f|_{H^{s+1}},

(B.11)

\displaystyle|(\sqrt{\mathrm{k}}(\mathrm{D})-1)f|_{H^{s}}

\displaystyle\leq(\sqrt{\mu}+\mathrm{bo}^{-1})C|\partial_{x}f|_{H^{s+1}},

(B.12)

\displaystyle\Big|\big(\sqrt{k}(\mathrm{D})-(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|-\frac{\partial_{x}^{2}}{2\mathrm{bo}})\big)f\Big|_{H^{s}}

\displaystyle\leq(\mu+\frac{\sqrt{\mu}}{\mathrm{bo}})C|\partial_{x}^{2}f|_{H^{s+1}}.

•

5. Define the Fourier multiplier

\mathrm{t}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\mathrm{D})f(x)=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\Big(\Big((1+\gamma\tanh(\sqrt{\mu}|\xi|))\frac{\sqrt{\mu}|\xi|}{\tanh(\sqrt{\mu}|\xi|)}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\hat{f}(\xi)\Big)(x).

Then for any
h_{0}\in(0,1)
there is a
C>0
such that,

(B.13)

(1-\frac{h_{0}}{2})|f|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\sqrt{\mu}C|f|^{2}_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}\leq|\mathrm{t}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\mathrm{D})f|_{H^{s}}^{2}\leq C(|f|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\sqrt{\mu}|f|^{2}_{\mathring{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}).

Proof.

We first consider the symbol in frequency, where the elementary inequality holds

\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\mu}|\xi|\mathrm{tanh}(\sqrt{\mu}|\xi|)\leq\sqrt{\mu}|\xi|\frac{\mathrm{tanh}(\sqrt{\mu}|\xi|)}{1+\gamma\mathrm{tanh}(\sqrt{\mu}|\xi|)}\leq\sqrt{\mu}|\xi|\mathrm{tanh}(\sqrt{\mu}|\xi|).

Then by splitting in high and low frequency, we can prove that there is a number
C>0
such that

\frac{\mu}{C}\frac{|\xi|^{2}}{(1+\sqrt{\mu}|\xi|)}\leq\sqrt{\mu}|\xi|\mathrm{tanh}(\sqrt{\mu}|\xi|)\leq\mu C\frac{|\xi|^{2}}{(1+\sqrt{\mu}|\xi|)}.

To conclude, use Plancherel’s identity for (
B.1
) to find that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{G}_{\mu}[0]f|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim\mu^{2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\frac{|\xi|^{4}}{(1+\sqrt{\mu}|\xi|)^{2}}|\hat{f}(\xi)|^{2}\>\mathrm{d}\xi\lesssim\mu^{\frac{3}{2}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\langle\xi\rangle^{1}\frac{|\xi|^{2}}{(1+\sqrt{\mu}|\xi|)}|\hat{f}(\xi)|^{2}\>\mathrm{d}\xi,

while (
B.2
) and (
B.3
) follows directly.

The proof of the estimates in point three follows directly by Plancherel’s identity and elementary inequalities.

For the proof of the fourth point, we observe for
r\geq 0
that

\tanh(r)=\int_{0}^{r}(1-\tanh^{2}(s))\>\mathrm{d}s.

Then since
\tanh(r)\leq r
, we have that

|(T(\xi)-1)|\lesssim\mu\xi^{2},

and may conclude by Plancherel’s identity that (
B.7
) holds true, while the remaining points follows similarly. For the sake of completeness we give the proof of (
B.12
). To do so, we let
r=\sqrt{\mu}|\xi|
and for
0<r<1
we observe:

\displaystyle k(r)

\displaystyle=\frac{\tanh(r)}{r}\frac{1}{1+\gamma\tanh(r)}\big(1+\frac{r^{2}}{\mu\mathrm{bo}}\big)

\displaystyle=1-\gamma r+\frac{r^{2}}{\mu\mathrm{bo}}+A_{1}+A_{2}+A_{3},

where use Taylor expansions to see that

\displaystyle A_{1}

\displaystyle:=\Big(\frac{\tanh(r)}{r}-1\Big)\frac{1}{1+\gamma\tanh(r)}\big(1+\frac{r^{2}}{\mu\mathrm{bo}}\big)=cr^{2}\big(1+\frac{r^{2}}{\mu\mathrm{bo}}\big)

\displaystyle A_{2}

\displaystyle:=\Big(\frac{1}{1+\gamma\tanh(r)}-1\Big)\frac{r^{2}}{\mu\mathrm{bo}}=c\frac{r^{3}}{\mu\mathrm{bo}}

\displaystyle A_{3}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{1+\gamma\tanh(r)}-(1-\gamma r)=cr^{2},

for some universal constant
c
. Now using a Taylor expansion of
r\mapsto\sqrt{r}
implies that

\sqrt{k}(r)=1-\frac{\gamma}{2}r+\frac{r^{2}}{2\mu\mathrm{bo}}+c\big(r^{2}+\frac{r^{3}}{\mu\mathrm{bo}}\big),

for
0<r<1
. While for
r\geq 1
, we trivially have that

\Big|\sqrt{k}(r)-\Big(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}r+\frac{r^{2}}{2\mu\mathrm{bo}}\Big)\Big|\lesssim 1+\frac{r^{2}}{\mu\mathrm{bo}}+r\leq r^{2}+\frac{r^{3}}{\mu\mathrm{bo}}.

In particular, by Plancherel’s identity we find that

\Big|\big(\sqrt{\mathrm{k}}(\mathrm{D})-(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|-\frac{\partial_{x}^{2}}{2\mathrm{bo}})\big)f\Big|_{H^{s}}\lesssim(\mu+\frac{\sqrt{\mu}}{\mathrm{bo}})|\partial_{x}^{2}f|_{H^{s+1}}.

For the proof of (
B.13
), we note that the upper bound is trivial while the lower bound follows from
\tanh(r)\leq r
,
0\leq\tanh(r)\leq 1
, and the inequality

(1+\gamma\tanh(r))\frac{r}{\tanh(r)}\geq(1-\frac{h_{0}}{2})\frac{r}{\tanh(r)}+\frac{h_{0}}{2}\frac{r}{\tanh(r)}\geq(1-\frac{h_{0}}{2})+\frac{h_{0}}{2}r.

∎

We will also use the following product estimates (see Proposition B. 2. and Proposition B. 4. in
[
66
]
).

Lemma B.3
.

Let
t_{0}>\frac{d}{2}
,
s\geq-t_{0}
,
f\in H^{\max\{t_{0},s\}}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
, and take
g\in H^{s}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
then

(B.14)

|fg|_{H^{s}}\lesssim|f|_{H^{\max\{t_{0},s\}}}|g|_{H^{s}}.

Moreover, if there exist
c_{0}>0
and
1+g\geq c_{0}
then

(B.15)

\Big|\frac{f}{1+g}\Big|_{H^{s}}\lesssim C(c_{0},|g|_{L^{\infty}})(1+|f|_{H^{s}})|g|_{H^{s}}.

Lastly, we will use several commutator estimates for Fourier multipliers. The first result is a generalization of the classical Kato-Ponce estimate
8
8

8

See
[
53
]
for the case
\mathrm{F}=\Lambda^{s}
.
and is given by Proposition B. 8. in
[
66
]
:

Proposition B.4
.

Let
t_{0}>\frac{1}{2}
,
s\geq 0
. In the case
\mathrm{F}\in\mathcal{S}^{s}
,
f\in H^{\max\{t_{0}+1,s\}}(\mathbb{R})
then, for all
g\in H^{s-1}(\mathbb{R})
,

(B.16)

\displaystyle|[\mathrm{F},f]g|_{L^{2}}\leq\mathcal{N}^{s}(\mathrm{F})|f|_{H^{\max\{t_{0}+1,s\}}}|g|_{H^{s-1}}.

In the case
\mathrm{F}\in\mathcal{S}^{0}
,
f\in H^{t_{0}+1}(\mathbb{R})
then, for all
g\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
,

(B.17)

\displaystyle|[\mathrm{F},f]g|_{H^{1}}\leq\mathcal{N}^{0}(\mathrm{F})|f|_{H^{t_{0}+1}}|g|_{L^{2}}.

Moreover, we will also need some commutator estimates on multipliers with non-smooth symbol.

Proposition B.5
.

Let
\mu,\mathrm{bo}^{-1}\in(0,1)
,
f,g\in\mathscr{S}({\mathbb{R}})
,
t_{0}>\frac{1}{2}
then there is a universal constant
C>0
such that

(B.18)

|[f,|\mathrm{D}|^{\frac{1}{2}}]g|\leq C|f|_{H^{t_{0}+\frac{1}{2}}}|g|_{L^{2}}

(B.19)

|[f,(1+\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{D}|)^{\frac{1}{2}}]g|_{L^{2}}\leq C|f|_{H^{t_{0}+\frac{1}{2}}}|g|_{L^{2}}.

Moreover, let

\displaystyle\mathrm{\sqrt{T}}(\mathrm{D})f(x)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\Big(\Big(\frac{\tanh(\sqrt{\mu}|\xi|)}{\sqrt{\mu}|\xi|}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\hat{f}(\xi)\Big)(x),

\displaystyle\sqrt{\mathrm{I}}(\mathrm{D})f(x)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\Big((1+\gamma\tanh(\sqrt{\mu}|\xi|))^{-\frac{1}{2}}\hat{f}(\xi)\Big)(x),

and let
\sqrt{\mathrm{k}}(\mathrm{D})
and
\sqrt{\mathrm{t}}(\mathrm{D})
be defined by

\displaystyle\sqrt{\mathrm{k}}(\mathrm{D})

\displaystyle=\sqrt{I}(\mathrm{D})\sqrt{T}(\mathrm{D})\sqrt{1+\mathrm{bo}^{-1}|\mathrm{D}|^{2}}

\displaystyle\sqrt{\mathrm{t}}(\mathrm{D})f

\displaystyle=\sqrt{I}(\mathrm{D})\sqrt{T}(\mathrm{D})

then for
s\geq t_{0}+1
there holds

(B.20)

|[\Lambda^{s}\sqrt{\mathrm{k}}(\mathrm{D}),f]\partial_{x}g|_{L^{2}}\leq C|f|_{H^{s+1}_{\mathrm{bo}}}|g|_{H^{s+1}_{\mathrm{bo}}},

(B.21)

|[\Lambda^{s}\sqrt{\mathrm{t}}(\mathrm{D}),f]\partial_{x}g|_{L^{2}}\leq C|f|_{H^{s}}|g|_{H^{s}},

(B.22)

|\partial_{x}[\sqrt{\mathrm{t}}(\mathrm{D}),f]g|_{L^{2}}\leq C|f|_{H^{t_{0}+1}}|g|_{L^{2}}.

Proof.

For the proof of (
B.18
), we write the commutator as a convolution product in frequency:

\displaystyle\big|[f,|\mathrm{D}|^{\frac{1}{2}}]g\big|_{L^{2}}=\bigg|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\Big(|\xi|^{\frac{1}{2}}-|\rho|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big)\hat{f}(\xi-\rho)\hat{g}(\rho)\>d\rho\bigg|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}.

Then the proof is a direct consequence of the estimate
|\xi|^{\frac{1}{2}}-|\rho|^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq 1+|\xi-\rho|^{\frac{1}{2}}
when combined with Minkowski integral inequality and Cauchy-Schwarz inequality.

Inequality (
B.19
) is proved similarly.

For the remaining estimates, they are proved in
[
84
]
in the case
\sqrt{I}(\mathrm{D})
is identity with the same result. However, noting that
\sqrt{I}(\mathrm{D})
is bounded on
L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})
we observe that

\displaystyle|[\mathrm{a}(\mathrm{D})\mathrm{b}(\mathrm{D}),f]\partial_{x}g|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq|\mathrm{a}(\mathrm{D})[\mathrm{b}(\mathrm{D}),f]\partial_{x}g|_{L^{2}}+|[\mathrm{a}(\mathrm{D}),f]\mathrm{b}(\mathrm{D})\partial_{x}g|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=A_{1}+A_{2}.

Then to prove (
B.20
) we take
\mathrm{a}(\mathrm{D})=\sqrt{I}(\mathrm{D})
and
\mathrm{b}(\mathrm{D})=\sqrt{T}(\mathrm{D})\sqrt{1+\mathrm{bo}^{-1}|\mathrm{D}|^{2}}
to deduce that

\displaystyle A_{1}=|\mathrm{a}(\mathrm{D})[\mathrm{b}(\mathrm{D}),f]\partial_{x}g|_{L^{2}}\leq|[\mathrm{b}(\mathrm{D}),f]\partial_{x}g|_{L^{2}}\leq C|f|_{H^{s+1}_{\mathrm{bo}}}|g|_{H^{s+1}_{\mathrm{bo}}},

where the last estimate is proved in
[
84
]
, Lemma 2.7. For the second term it is clearly enough to prove that

A_{2}\leq|[\mathrm{a}(\mathrm{D}),f]\mathrm{b}(\mathrm{D})\partial_{x}g|_{L^{2}}\lesssim|f|_{H^{s}}|\mathrm{b}(\mathrm{D})g|_{L^{2}}.

In other words, we must prove that the commutator
[\mathrm{a}(\mathrm{D}),f]
absorbs one derivative uniformly in
\mu
. In particular, writing it as a convolution product in frequency, we find that:

[\mathrm{a}(\mathrm{D}),f]\partial_{x}g=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\Big(a(\xi)-a(\rho)\Big)\hat{f}(\xi-\rho)\>\widehat{\partial_{x}g}(\rho)\>d\rho,

and we can conclude if

(B.23)

k(\xi,\rho)|=|a(\xi)-a(\rho)|\langle\rho\rangle\leq\langle\xi-\rho\rangle.

To prove (
B.23
), we consider three cases. First, if
|\rho|\leq 1
we have trivially that

|a(\xi)-a(\rho)|\langle\rho\rangle\lesssim 1,

since
\xi\mapsto a(\xi)
is bounded by one. Next, we consider the region
|\xi|\geq|\rho|>1
. Then we observe that since
\xi\mapsto a(\xi)
is decreasing then

\Big(\frac{a(\xi)}{a(\rho)}\Big)^{2}\leq\frac{a(\xi)}{a(\rho)}\leq 1,

from which we deduce that

\displaystyle k(\xi,\rho)

\displaystyle=\Big(1-\frac{a(\xi)}{a(\rho)}\Big)a(\rho)\langle\rho\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(1-\Big(\frac{a(\xi)}{a(\rho)}\Big)^{2}\bigg)a(\rho)\langle\rho\rangle.

Now, use the definition of
a
to simplify the expression further:

\displaystyle k(\xi,\rho)

\displaystyle\leq\big(\tanh(\sqrt{\mu}|\xi|)-\tanh(\sqrt{\mu}|\rho|)\big)\frac{\gamma a(\rho)}{1+\gamma\tanh(\sqrt{\mu}|\xi|)}\langle\rho\rangle.

and then use the Mean value Theorem and that
|\rho|<|\xi|
